cancelling the successive Gray bits from l through m-1. The sum of all
characters is N on equal labels and zero otherwise: pair labels in any
coordinate in which they differ. Subtracting the constant and distinguished
characters gives the EXACT kernel

    B_kl=<w_k,w_l>
      =1/3-eta+sum_(j<m)(2^j/(N-1)-eta)
                       (-1)^(bit_j(k)+bit_j(l))
                     +eta N 1_(k=l)
      =T-2(k xor l)/(N-1)+2eta popcount(k xor l)
                     -eta N+eta N 1_(k=l).           (3)

In particular B_kk=T. The mixed characters are present in every formula.

The exact adjacent level energy and inner product are

    E_l=(2^(l+1)-1)/(N-1)+eta(N/2-l-1),
    B_l^adj=T-2E_l.

Let rho_l be the positive root of

    rho_l^2+(H_l-2)(B_l^adj/T)rho_l+1-H_l=0.

These are the prescribed diagnostic roots. Put

    x=r_0=rho_0(1-4/N), y=r_1=rho_1(1-4/N),
    r_l=1 for l>=2,
    sigma_0=1, sigma_(k+1)=sigma_k r_(nu_2(k+1)),
    v_k=sigma_k w_k, D0=conv{0,v_0,...,v_n}.          (4)

No high-level root replaces r_l=1. Exact low-level counts give

    sigma_k=x^ceil(k/2)y^floor((k+1)/4).             (5)

All character amplitudes and all sigma_k are positive. The full Walsh
matrix, including its constant vertical row, is invertible by the same
orthogonality identity. Thus the N vectors v_k are a basis of R^N. Let V
be the matrix with these vectors as columns and G=V^T V, so G is positive
definite. These independence statements do not omit small characters.

## 3. Legal potentials, actual values, and the complete minimum set

Define

    P_k=sum_(l<k)h_l v_l, A_kl=<P_k,v_l>,
    C_kl=A_kl-A_kk+G_kl-G_kk,
    K_k=A_kk+G_kk, c_n=A_nn+G_nn/2.

For an initializer z_0, write fpot_k=<z_0,v_k>. These are inner-product
potentials, NOT sampled function values. Conversely every real vector
fpot is realized uniquely by

    z_0=V G^(-1) fpot.                              (6)

Consider the fixed function

    Phi(z)=max_(v in D0){<z,v>-||v||^2/2}.

Completing the square gives the unique maximizer projection_D0(z).
A proposed maximizer v satisfies

    <z-v,w-v><=0 for every w in D0.                 (7)

Necessity follows by differentiating squared distance along a segment
from v to w. Conversely expanding ||z-w||^2 shows (7) makes v the unique
nearest point. It suffices to check generating vertices, by linearity.
Adding (7) for two projections proves their firm nonexpansiveness and
hence their 1-Lipschitz property. The two affine maxima bound the remainder
Phi(z')-Phi(z)-<projection_D0(z),z'-z> between 0 and ||z'-z||^2.
Thus Phi is finite, convex, differentiable and 1-smooth, with gradient
projection_D0. These elementary facts also appear in accepted fact
`1e0bf3f41457d8e4`, Part A Section 2; no stronger assertion from that fact
is required here.

At the prescribed points z_k=z_0-P_k, (7) is EXACTLY

    fpot_l-fpot_k<=C_kl for every original ordered pair k,l,
    fpot_k>=K_k for every original k.               (8)

For the second line use w=0. Therefore (8) is both necessary and sufficient
for all v_k, including the terminal v_n, to be the actual projections.
Under (8), z_(k+1)=z_k-h_k grad Phi(z_k) for all k<n, and

    Phi(z_k)=fpot_k-A_kk-G_kk/2,
    Phi(z_n)=fpot_n-c_n>=G_nn/2>0.                 (9)

There is no physical h_n. The complete minimum set is

    Kpolar=cone{v_k}^polar={z:V^T z<=0}, min Phi=0.  (10)

A polar point makes all defining expressions nonpositive, while v=0
supplies zero. If <z,v_k>>0, a sufficiently small positive multiple of
v_k belongs to D0 and has positive defining expression; this proves the
converse. This is the full polar cone, not a selected vertical slice.

## 4. Exact closest-minimum geometry, perspective, and zero boundary

For ANY fpot, let z_0 be (6) and write Rtrue=dist(z_0,Kpolar). Changing
variables d=V^T(z_0-zstar) in the distance minimization gives

    Rtrue^2=r(fpot):=min_(d>=fpot) d^T G^(-1)d.       (11)

Indeed zstar is polar iff V^T zstar=fpot-d<=0, and the vector difference
is V G^(-1)d with precisely that squared norm. The positive definite
quadratic is coercive, so its minimum over the closed nonempty orthant
translate exists and is unique.

For completeness its geometry follows without a duality theorem. At its
minimizer d*, let alpha=G^(-1)d*. Coordinate variations give alpha_k>=0;
if d*_k>fpot_k, both variation signs are allowed and alpha_k=0. Thus

    alpha>=0, d*>=fpot, alpha_k(d*_k-fpot_k)=0.

Put p=V alpha and zstar=z_0-p. Then p belongs to cone{v_k}, zstar belongs
to its polar, and <p,zstar>=0. For any polar y,

    ||z_0-y||^2=||p||^2+||zstar-y||^2-2<p,y>
               >=||p||^2.

This proves the full closest-minimum assertion directly. In particular a
legal fpot has positive radius: fpot_0>=K_0=G_00>0 excludes polarity.

Consider the finite convex program

    maximize 2F_n-2tau c_n
    tau>=0,
    F_l-F_k<=tau C_kl for ALL original ordered pairs,
    F_k>=tau K_k,
    d>=F, d^T G^(-1)d<=tau.                         (12)

The variables F are rescaled potentials. Convexity means the feasible set
is convex and the maximand is linear. There is no solver use here.

Forward direction: for legal fpot set tau=1/r(fpot), F=tau fpot and
choose d=tau d*. Then d^T G^(-1)d=tau and the objective is exactly
2(fpot_n-c_n)/r(fpot).

Reverse direction: given a feasible point with tau>0, fpot=F/tau obeys
ALL of (8), has the unique actual realization (6), and

    r(fpot)<=d^T G^(-1)d/tau^2<=1/tau.

By (9) its terminal numerator is strictly positive. Hence its actual
score is AT LEAST the value of (12). Pointwise equality is not asserted
when the supplied quadratic inequality is slack. At an optimum the two
suprema nonetheless coincide by the two inequalities just proved.

Zero boundary: tau=0 forces d=0 by positive definiteness. The inequalities
d>=F and F>=0 force F=0, so the objective is zero. This point always
exists, independently of itinerary feasibility. Thus (12) equals

    sup({0} union {actual scores of legal initializers}).              (13)

If a legal initializer exists, its score is positive and (13) is exactly
the proposed supremum. If none exists, (12) has only its zero point, while
the conventional supremum of the empty legal set is not zero. Equivalently,
positive-tau feasibility is precisely legal-initializer feasibility.
This qualification matters because full itinerary feasibility is not proved.

The maximum in (12) exists. Since K_0=G_00, feasibility gives
F_0>=tau G_00, d_0>=F_0, and

d^T G^(-1)d>=d_0^2/G_00>=tau^2 G_00.

The first inequality follows by applying Cauchy--Schwarz to
V G^(-1)d and v_0. Consequently tau<=1/G_00 when tau>0. The quadratic
constraint bounds d. The lower bounds F_k>=tau K_k and upper bounds F<=d
then bound F. The feasible set is closed and bounded. If its maximum is
positive, a positive-tau maximizer recovers an actual maximizer via (6).

## 5. Weak-dual signs and complete-square certificate

Let lambda_kl>=0 and nu_k>=0 be any fixed multipliers, and define

    rho=2e_n+out(lambda)-in(lambda)+nu,
    kappa=2c_n-sum lambda_kl C_kl+sum nu_k K_k.

Here e_n is the coordinate vector, out_k=sum_l lambda_kl and
in_k=sum_l lambda_lk. Suppose rho>=0 componentwise and kappa>0.
For every legal potential,

    rho^T fpot-kappa
       =2(fpot_n-c_n)
        +sum lambda_kl [C_kl-(fpot_l-fpot_k)]
        +sum nu_k(fpot_k-K_k)
       >=2(fpot_n-c_n).                             (14)

This explicitly fixes the orientation and both signs. For its closest
polar point zstar, g=V rho belongs to cone{v_k}, so

    rho^T fpot=<z_0,g>
       <=<z_0-zstar,g><=Rtrue ||g||.

Thus, writing b=||g|| and u=1/Rtrue,

    2(fpot_n-c_n)/Rtrue^2
       <=b u-kappa u^2
        =b^2/(4kappa)-kappa(u-b/(2kappa))^2
       <=rho^T G rho/(4kappa).                     (15)

Alternatively, in (12), (14) becomes objective<=rho^T F-kappa tau,
which is <=rho^T d-kappa tau<=b sqrt(tau)-kappa tau; complete the square
in sqrt(tau). This also includes tau=0. No strong-duality assertion is
needed. These multipliers belong to itinerary normals, not the earlier
original smooth-convex GD interpolation dual.

## 6. The exact two-vertex interface and inherited prefix constants

Let r=m-2, Lq=2^r=N/4, i=Lq-1, and put

    theta=x^2 y, s=theta^(Lq/4)=sigma_i,
    t=theta^(N/4)=sigma_n=s^4,
    B=<w_i,w_n>=T-3N/[2(N-1)]+4eta-eta N.           (16)

The index xor is 3N/4 and its popcount is 2. The exact submatrix is

    Gpair=[[s^2 T, s t B],[s t B,t^2 T]].             (17)

It is positive definite as a principal submatrix of G. In particular
T+B>0. Also B<0 and

    T+B=7/6+2/N^2-3/[2(N-1)]-eta(N-4)<7/6.         (18)

The last inequality uses N>=4, with 2/N^2<3/[2(N-1)].

Here are closed finite dyadic formulas for all chronological constants;
they are symbolic identities, not instructions to run a recurrence.
For integers 2<=j<=m, let Pcal_j(z,w) be the following POLYNOMIAL,
with empty products equal to 1:

 [H_0 z+x H_1 z^2 w+x H_0 z^3 w]
       product_(b=0)^(j-3)[1+w(theta z^4)^(2^b)]
 +sum_(l=2)^(j-1) H_l w^l(theta z^4)^(2^(l-2))
       product_(b=0)^(j-l-2)[1+w(theta z^4)^(2^(l-1+b))].              (19)

Its exact expansion is

    Pcal_j(z,w)=sum_(k<2^j-1)h_k sigma_k z^(k+1)w^popcount(k).

For the three low positions k=4q,4q+1,4q+2 use respectively radii
 theta^q,x theta^q,x theta^q and popcounts pop(q),pop(q)+1,pop(q)+1.
For level l>=2 write k=(2q+1)2^l-1: its radius is
 theta^((2q+1)2^(l-2)) and popcount is l+pop(q). Expanding the binary
products in (19) enumerates precisely these disjoint level classes.
This proves the formula without enumeration by a program.

Define the exact sums

    U_j=Pcal_j(1,1),
    M_j=(partial_z Pcal_j)(1,1)-U_j,
    Z_j=(partial_w Pcal_j)(1,1).

Thus they sum h_k sigma_k, k h_k sigma_k, and popcount(k)h_k sigma_k,
respectively. A scalar alternative for the first is

    U_j=(1-theta^(2^(j-2)))
       [(H_0(1+x)+x H_1)/(1-theta)
        +sum_(l=2)^(j-1) H_l theta^(2^(l-2))
                                      /(1-theta^(2^(l-1)))].         (20)

Every denominator is positive. This follows by summing each level's
finite geometric progression in (19).

Put U=U_r,M=M_r,Z=Z_r and

    b_i=T-2(Lq-1)/(N-1)+2eta r-eta N,
    b_n=T-2+2eta m-eta N,
    J_i=b_i U+2M/(N-1)-2eta Z=<P_i,w_i>,
    J_pre=b_n U+2M/(N-1)-2eta Z=<P_i,w_n>,
    J_n=b_n U_m+2M_m/(N-1)-2eta Z_m=<P_n,w_n>.

These use k xor i=i-k for k<i and k xor n=n-k for k<n. In particular

    A_ii=s J_i, A_in=t J_pre, A_nn=t J_n.             (21)

To make the reverse row equally explicit, define

    E_i=sum_(q=0)^3 s^q[J_i+(-2q Lq/(N-1)+2eta popcount(q))U]
       +H_r s T
       +H_(r+1) s^2[T-2Lq/(N-1)+2eta-eta N]
       +H_r s^3[T-4Lq/(N-1)+2eta-eta N].

Then A_ni=s E_i. For each quarter interior k=qLq+j, j<Lq-1, the
radius is s^q sigma_j and k xor i=(q+1)Lq-1-j. The three bridge
indices are qLq-1, q=1,2,3. Their xor values against i are 0,Lq,2Lq.
The q=1 bridge is the actual diagonal and therefore contributes T,
not T-eta N. This proves the displayed expression including its
otherwise easily missed diagonal correction.

All pair data are now exact:

    K_i=s J_i+s^2 T, K_n=t J_n+t^2 T,
    c_n=t J_n+t^2 T/2,
    C_in=t J_pre-s J_i+s t B-s^2 T,
    C_ni=s E_i-t J_n+s t B-t^2 T.                   (22)

Writing u=fpot_i,v=fpot_n, the two-dimensional potential domain is

    u>=K_i, v>=K_n, v-u<=C_in, u-v<=C_ni.            (23)

For p=s^2T,q=t^2T,b=s t B<0, its exact closest-halfspace radius is
min_(d1>=u,d2>=v)(q d1^2-2b d1 d2+p d2^2)/(pq-b^2). Its elementary
active-face cases are:

* If u<=0 and v<=0, the minimum is 0 at d=(0,0).
* If u>0 and v<=b u/p, it is u^2/p at d=(u,b u/p).
* If v>0 and u<=b v/q, it is v^2/q at d=(b v/q,v).
* Otherwise both faces are active: d=(u,v) and the displayed quadratic
  is the value; its two coordinate derivatives are nonnegative.

These follow by setting free-coordinate derivatives to zero and checking
the remaining derivative sign. Boundary overlaps give identical values.
Alternatively the complementarity argument in Section 4 proves the same
exhaustive cases. The positive terminal numerator in (23) is at least
q, and the certificate below forces a positive radius on any feasible
point of (23), so the zero-distance case cannot cause a positive-score
singularity here.

This is a relaxation of the full initializer problem: retaining two
halfspaces enlarges the polar minimum set, so the pair distance is no
greater than Rtrue. The numerator and chronological constants stay
unchanged. Therefore an upper bound for all of (23), with this smaller
distance, is an upper bound for every full legal initializer.

## 7. Uniform radial bounds without a numerical evaluation

The following elementary estimates hold throughout m>=1536:

    N>=2^16, Kmix>=N/2,
    12/5<c<121/50, 2/5<a<5/12,
    beta>999/1000, 4/3<T<27/20.                      (24)

For c use 7/5<sqrt(2)<17/12 and epsilon<1/6000; for the sharper
lower bound on a use beta>999/1000 and c<121/50. The inequalities for
N and Kmix follow from 2^m>=2m+2 and monotonicity under doubling.

For l=0,1 write H_l=1+b with b=a or beta. The root equation gives

    (1-rho_l)(rho_l+b)=2(1-b)E_l rho_l/T.            (25)

Since H_l<2, the polynomial at 1 is positive and its constant term is
negative; therefore 0<rho_l<1. Also rho_l/(rho_l+b)<=1/(1+b).
The exact E_l and Kmix>=N/2 give

    N E_0<1+3/N, N E_1<4.

Consequently

    N(1-rho_0)<(9/14)(1+3/N)<13/20<2/3,
    N(1-rho_1)<6epsilon<1/1000.                     (26)

For the lower estimate, rho_0>1-2/(3N)>999/1000, 1-a>7/12,
rho_0+a<17/12, N E_0>1, and T<27/20. Equation (25) yields

    N(1-rho_0)>(280/459)(999/1000)>121/200.

Using x=rho_0(1-4/N) therefore gives

    23/5<N(1-x)<93/20<5,
    4<N(1-y)<4001/1000<5.                          (27)

For the first lower bound, subtract at most 8/(3N) from 4+121/200;
N>=2^16 makes the result exceed 4+3/5.

Set

    zeta=-(N/4)log(theta)=-(N/4)(2log x+log y).

For 0<u<1, u<=-log(1-u)<=u/(1-u); integrate 1/(1-u), or compare
its monotone integrand to its endpoint bounds. Thus (27) implies

    33/10<zeta<13301/[4000(1-5/N)]<10/3.             (28)

The last rational comparison holds already for N>200000/97, hence here.
In particular s=exp(-zeta/4), t=s^4=exp(-zeta), and

    3/7<s<4/9,   t<1/27.                           (29)

Here are rational exponential checks, so no decimal approximation is used.
For 0<u<3, the tail from the quadratic term is bounded by a geometric
series with successive ratio at most u/3. Thus

    exp(5/6)<=1+5/6+(25/72)/(1-5/18)
              =361/156<7/3.

The first four terms at 33/40 sum to 867417/384000>9/4, proving the
upper s bound. The first six terms at 11/10 sum to
36015101/12000000>3. Cubing gives exp(33/10)>27 and proves the t bound.

For u_k=(k+1)/N, (5) gives an exact useful comparison. At k=4q+d,
the ratio sigma_k/exp(-zeta u_k) is, respectively,

    theta^(-1/4), y^(-1/2), x^(-1/2)y^(-3/4), 1
    for d=0,1,2,3.

By (27), -log x,-log y<6/N. Each logarithm of these ratios is
nonnegative and below 15/(2N). Since exp(u)<=1/(1-u) for 0<=u<1,
N>=2^16 gives

    0<=sigma_k-exp(-zeta u_k)<8/N.                  (30)

At all bridge indices qLq-1 the comparison is equality: sigma=s^q.
The original radius is never replaced; (30) only bounds a signed error.

## 8. The physical prefix estimate paying the pair certificate

Let S=S_r and define the exact scalar

    J=<P_i,w_i+w_n>+(1+s^3)<P_n-P_i,w_n>.           (31)

We prove J>(7/15)S. First note the exact four-quarter clock identity

    S_m=4S+2H_r+H_(r+1),
    D_m=8S+4H_r+2H_(r+1)+1.                        (32)

Also

    H_r<S/2, H_(r+1)>(9/10)S, D_m<12S.             (33)

Here are all needed finite comparisons. Put lambda0=1+sqrt(2), used only
in this paragraph. Then

 c(c-2)-beta=(2lambda0^2-2lambda0+1)epsilon
                         +lambda0^2 epsilon^2>c epsilon.

The coefficient exceeds 7 while c<5/2. Thus c-2-a>epsilon, and
0<a/(c-2)<1. Formula (2) gives S<c^r and hence
H_(r+1)=1+beta c^r>beta S>(9/10)S. Also Lq=2^r>8m: this holds at
m=16 and persists when m increments because 2m>m+1. Therefore

 H_r=(c-2)S-(c-2-a)Lq+c-1<(c-2)S<(21/50)S<S/2.

For this use (c-2-a)Lq>epsilon*8m=2>c-1. Then
H_(r+1)=c H_r+1-c<(2541/2500)S, and (32), S>4, imply
D_m/S<29282/2500+1/4<12. This also gives S_m<6S.

For k<n, (3) with n xor k=n-k gives

    |<w_k,w_n>-[-2/3+2u_k]|<3/N.                  (34)

Indeed its difference is 1/N^2-2(1-u_k)/(N-1)
+eta[2(m-popcount(k))-N]. Its absolute value is at most
3/N^2+2/(N-1)<3/N. For k<i the identical expansion with i xor k=i-k
and m-2 lower bits gives

    |<w_k,w_i>-[5/6+2u_k]|<3/N.                   (35)

The corresponding middle error is -2(1/4-u_k)/(N-1), of even smaller
absolute value. The eta terms in (34)-(35) were included before being
bounded using eta N<=2/N^2; none was set to zero.

Define ideal scalar functions on their indicated physical intervals:

    f(u)=exp(-zeta u)(1/6+4u), 0<=u<=1/4,
    g(u)=exp(-zeta u)(-2/3+2u), 1/4<=u<=1.

Let Jideal be the literal h-weighted sum of f(u_k) for k<i plus
(1+s^3) times the literal h-weighted sum of g(u_k) for i<=k<n.
Combining (30), (34), and (35) bounds the first integrand error by
6/N+(8/N)(7/6)<16/N. The second is bounded by
3/N+(8/N)(4/3)<16/N. Since sigma_k<=1 and 1+s^3<2,

    |J-Jideal|<32 S_m/N<192S/N<256S/N.             (36)

This bound covers the actual terminal prefix, every original step,
and all sign-indefinite early-tail contributions.

Each quarter interior k=qLq+j, 0<=j<Lq-1, is the original H_r word.
Its total weight is S, and its h-weighted mean of u_k is (q+1/2)/4.
To prove the mean, pair j+1 with Lq-(j+1): their valuations are equal,
so their equal step weights have mean Lq/2. No gradient or trajectory
is reset in using this physical clock symmetry.

The functions are concave where needed:

 f''(u)=zeta exp(-zeta u)[zeta(1/6+4u)-8]<0
                                                 on [0,1/4],
 g''(u)=zeta exp(-zeta u)[zeta(-2/3+2u)-4]<0
                                                 on [1/4,3/4].

Use zeta<10/3, so their bracketed upper bounds are 35/9-8 and
25/9-4, respectively. A concave function is at least its endpoint
chord. Summing that affine chord with the exact mean just proved gives
these lower bounds, each multiplied by S:

    quarter 0: (1+7s)/12,
    quarter 1: (-s+2s^2)/12,
    quarter 2: (2s^2+5s^3)/12.

For quarter 3, g(u)>=5s^4/6 since -2/3+2u>=5/6 and
exp(-zeta u)>=s^4. The three actual bridges have weights H_r,
H_(r+1),H_r and positions u=1/4,1/2,3/4. Their total ideal tail
contribution is exactly

    H_r(-s+5s^3)/6+H_(r+1)s^2/3.                  (37)

No physical bridge is absorbed into an auxiliary word. Since s<4/9,
5s^2<80/81<1, so the H_r coefficient in (37) is negative. Using (33)
with that sign retained, the four tail pieces and bridges together are
at least S times

    Bfun(s)=-s/6+19s^2/30+5s^3/6+5s^4/6.           (38)

On s>=3/7 its derivative is positive: already 19s/15>1/6, and all
other derivative terms are nonnegative. Direct rational substitution
in the displayed polynomial gives

    Bfun(s)>Bfun(3/7)=1664/12005>2/15.

Thus Bfun is positive, allowing us to replace its prefactor 1+s^3 by
1 in a lower bound. The first-quarter contribution exceeds S/3.
Consequently

    Jideal/S>1/3+1664/12005=7/15+38/7203.

Because N>=2^16, 256/N<=1/256<1/200<38/7203. Subtracting (36)
proves the strict, fully finite estimate

    J>(7/15)S.                                    (39)

## 9. Explicit positive pair weights and the final uniform upper

Take the sole nonzero itinerary multiplier to be

    lambda_in=ell=2t/(s+t)>0,
    lambda_ni=0, nu_i=nu_n=0.

Then rho_i=ell and rho_n=2-ell=2s/(s+t), both positive. Their vector
combination retains the exact angle:

    Vpair rho=2st/(s+t)(w_i+w_n),
    rho^T Gpair rho=8s^2t^2(T+B)/(s+t)^2.            (40)

Using the exact inherited constants (22), let Jtail=J_n-J_pre. Then

 kappa=2c_n-ell C_in
      =ell s[J_i+J_pre+(1+s^3)Jtail
                  +sT-tB+(s+t)tT/(2s)]
      >ell s J=2st J/(s+t)>0.                      (41)

Every omitted term in the strict inequality is positive: T,s,t>0 and
B<0. In particular the actual terminal half-square in c_n has been
retained before this comparison.

Apply (15) to the two-dimensional relaxation (23), or its perspective
form. Equations (40)-(41) show every feasible relaxed score is at most
rho^T Gpair rho/(4kappa), and strictly less than

    t(T+B)/[(1+s^3)J].                              (42)

Now use (18), (29), (33), and (39), all for the same enclosing m:

    t<1/27, T+B<7/6,
    1+s^3>1+27/343=370/343,
    J>(7/15)S, D_m<12S.

Therefore the right side of (42) is less than

    (1/27)(7/6)(343/370)(15/7)/S
       =343/(3996S)
       <343/(333D_m)
       <17/(16D_m).                                (43)

The last comparison is 343*16=5488<5661=333*17. This proves the
announced candidate result (1), including every potential case in
Section 6, without an optimizer or conic-duality assumption.

The same positive certificate bounds the zero-adjoined full perspective
program (12). It does not establish its positive-tau feasibility. If a
full legal initializer is later constructed, it necessarily satisfies
(43). Thus there is no relative-potential escape above the requested
threshold inside these frozen hypotheses. Changing the body, radius law,
itinerary, or function class is outside this result.

## 10. Author audit, provenance, and resource boundary

All new steps in Sections 2-9 are a single joint unverified derivation;
no unverified intermediate is promoted to a fact. Accepted
`1e0bf3f41457d8e4` is used only as a consistency reference for the
projection function and full polar minimum set, which are rederived.
The earlier six designated public reports supplied frozen definitions
and numerical awareness, not mathematical premises. Their finite-depth
potentials have positive residuals and supply no exact initializer here.

The audit explicitly checked:

* potentials versus actual function values and the terminal half-square;
* both directions of (12), the inequality in its reverse direction,
  the always-feasible zero point, and the empty-set caveat;
* full polar geometry and sign/complete-square derivation of the weak dual;
* both original pair orientations and zero constraints in (23), exact
  A_ni with its diagonal eta N correction, and all chronological constants;
* original quarter interiors and all three bridges, the sign of the
  H_r coefficient in (37), and no restart of the radial law;
* every tiny-character error before bounding it, and finite m>=1536 bounds;
* the distinction between a fixed-itinerary upper and a full-function upper.

Literature retrieval was background only: Matlas returned Theorem 1
(Moreau cone decomposition) of Mortagy, Gupta, and Pokutta, *Walking in
the Shadow: A New Perspective on Descent Directions for Constrained
Minimization*, arXiv:2006.08426. Its definition of the polar uses inner
products <=0, as here. The PDF was downloaded. `pdftotext` was unavailable
(`command not found`), and pypdf, fitz, and pdfminer were absent. The exact
arXiv v4 HTML was then downloaded and extracted using the standard-library
HTML parser; its theorem and projection-normal proof context were read.
The retrieved theorem is not invoked as a black box; Section 4 proves all
needed geometry directly. No external mathematical premise is used.

Resources: ONE allocated invocation, ONE substantive analytical mechanism.
ZERO mathematical programs, numerical/arithmetic/trajectory/recurrence
replays, solvers, optimizers, benchmark DP, formal calls/fact_submit, new
fact IDs, installations, consults, or subagents. The symbolic polynomial,
derivatives, finite sums and exponential inequalities above were derived
in text, not evaluated by software. Administrative operations were text
retrieval/extraction, persistence, JSON bookkeeping and byte hashing only.
No old binary array was loaded and no old computational grant was used.

No TASK overwrite, worker/service control, finalization, revocation,
publication, shutdown, Goal completion or automatic renewal occurs.
The allocated capped round ends with its report and HANDOFF; the original
minimax problem and arbitrary projection-body geometry remain OPEN.

# Full zero-remainder interval cuts: the exact unresolved crossing inequality

Status: COMPLETE JOINT ANALYTICAL REPORT, UNVERIFIED. The proposed
factor-free hereditary interval implication is NOT proved or refuted.
The two coefficient mechanisms below are BLOCKED at a displayed weighted
crossing-edge inequality. There is an exact counterexample to a stronger
entrywise sign shortcut, not to interval heredity.

This is max2 round39, the ONE analytical round authorized by TASK.md and
master_guidance f163aa7e3fa73be3 after completed38. Completed ceiling39,
attempted ceiling40. No numerical computation, solver, machine algebra,
DP/replay, formal verifier, installation, consult, or subagent is used.
No fact_submit is authorized. There is no renewed round or claimed
process-exit observation. The original Goal and W=2R problem remain OPEN.

Accepted mathematical inputs are fact04219554e41d9750, proof Sections10--11,
for the original full polynomial identity and primitive join identity,
and fact1252b8823bb5a290 for the interpolation orientation. Earlier
ac0c1e866da0d926 and the other factorization drafts are awareness only.
Every auxiliary algebraic assertion used in this report is proved here
jointly. None is asserted verifier-accepted.

The symbols below are local to this report, implicitly suffixed ic39.
In particular P is a prefix step sum, C_L and C_R are boundary sums, and
v_i is a local alternating product. They are not benchmark capacities,
denominator rays, or the factors in the closed one-pair program.

## 1. Original full array and polynomial identity

Fix a physical length r>=1 and h_i>=0 for 0<=i<r. Set

 s=sum_(i<r)h_i, A=1+s,
 T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, T_it=0 for t>i,
 e=ones(r+1), L_flow=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,
 D=diag(h_i(2-h_i) for i<r, -s(s+2)).

The hypotheses under examination are exactly

 Lambda_ij>=0 for i!=j, Lambda_ii=0,
 L_flow^T e=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(r-1),2s)^T,
 D+(L_flow T+T^T L_flow^T)/2=0 on ALL R^(r+1).       (1)

All original formal gradients and every ordered pair are retained.
Define

 x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t,
 Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
 Qbar_ij=-2Q_ji
        =2(f_i-f_j)-2<g_j,x_i-x_j>-||g_i-g_j||^2,
 mu_ij=Lambda_ji/2,
 V_h=2sum_(i<r)h_i(f_i-f_r)
       +sum_(i<r)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-sA||g_r||^2.

The accepted original identity in fact04219554e41d9750, Section10,
turns (1) into the polynomial identity

                 V_h=sum_(i!=j)mu_ij Qbar_ij.          (2)

Equation (2) holds on the full formal space, for arbitrary formal values
and gradients. Actual interpolation is not needed when evaluating this
polynomial identity. Conversely, value and quadratic coefficient matching
in (2) gives the exact flow and full zero matrix in (1).

## 2. Algebraic isolation of zero steps

This section supplies the required zero-boundary bookkeeping; it is not
advertised as a new result beyond the earlier round38 necessary conditions.

Put t_i=h_i-1 for i<r and define

 d_i=sum_(j!=i)(mu_ij-mu_ji),
 I_i=sum_(j<i)mu_ji, J_i=sum_(j>i)mu_ji.

Value matching gives d_i=h_i for i<r and d_r=-s. At a nonterminal i,
the coefficient of ||g_i||^2 on the right of (2) is

             -d_i-2I_i-2J_i+2h_i J_i.

Indeed each squared difference contributes its two incident weights;
only Qbar_(j,i) with j>i contributes a trajectory diagonal, with
coefficient 2h_i. Matching V_h therefore gives

                 t_i J_i-I_i=(1-t_i^2)/2.             (3)

If 0<h_i<=1, its left side is nonpositive and its right side is positive.
Thus every positive h_i exceeds1. If h_i=0, equation (3) gives
I_i+J_i=0. Nonnegative entries make the entire incoming mu column zero;
d_i=0 then makes its entire outgoing row zero as well. Hence the entire
original Lambda row AND column vanish at every zero-step index.

List the positive-step indices as iota(0)<...<iota(m-1) and retain the
ORIGINAL terminal index by setting iota(m)=r. Restrict Lambda to these
indices and relabel h_(iota(j)) as the jth positive step. Every removed
step is zero and every incident multiplier was already zero. The
retained principal T is precisely the prefix matrix for this relabeling.
Moreover, if h_i=0, the ith column of original T is the coordinate column
e_i. Since the corresponding column and row of L_flow are zero, both
the ith column and row of the full remainder vanish. Thus (1) restricts
to the retained array and extends back to every independent original
zero-step gradient without imposing a relation among any gradients.

All-zero words have the zero array and no nonempty positive interval.
Otherwise Sections3--7 use this purely algebraic positive relabeling,
with its length again denoted r. This changes no physical horizon and
is not a deletion of actual GD iterations in a benchmark comparison.

For completeness, evaluating (2) on the scalar unit quadratic, with
g_0=1 and g_(i+1)=-t_i g_i, makes every Qbar_ij zero and gives

                    A product_(i<r)t_i=1.             (4)

To check it, V_h telescopes to 1-A^2 g_r^2; all t_i are now positive.
Neither (3) nor (4) supplies the missing interval implication.

## 3. Exact mixed matching with every crossing entry

For a<b, one half of the coefficient of <g_a,g_b> on the right of (2) is

 mu_ab+mu_ba-h_a sum_(j<=a)mu_jb+h_b sum_(j>b)mu_ja.

Here h_r is assigned the auxiliary value0 only when b=r. The first two
terms are from the squared differences; the next two are respectively
from trajectory segments ending at b and starting at a. Consequently

 t_a mu_ab=mu_ba-h_a sum_(j<a)mu_jb
                           +h_b sum_(j>b)mu_ja.        (5)

The sum preceding a has a MINUS sign. In particular it cannot be
discarded as an additional nonnegative supply.

One useful cumulative form is obtained by setting

 F_ab=sum_(j<=a)mu_jb, F_(-1)b=0,
 R_ab=sum_(j>=b)mu_ja, R_a,(r+1)=0,   a<b.

Equation (5) becomes

 t_a F_ab+F_(a-1)b=R_ab+t_b R_a,(b+1),                 (6)

where t_r=0 is only an auxiliary terminal placeholder. The original
signs imply F_ab>=F_(a-1)b>=0 and R_ab>=R_a,(b+1)>=0.
They do not give a separable expression for either cumulative array.
No factorization has been imposed in (5)--(6).

## 4. First attempted mechanism: an isolated interval quadratic

Fix ANY nonempty interval I=[l,k), with 0<=l<k<=r. Let

 H=sum_(i=l)^(k-1)h_i,
 v_l=1, v_(i+1)=-t_i v_i for l<=i<k,
 p=v_k, q=|p|=product_(i=l)^(k-1)t_i,
 A_i^out=sum_(j<l)mu_ji,
 B_i^out=sum_(j>=k)mu_ji,       l<=i<k.

These last two quantities are the actual incoming auxiliary weights
from the two exterior sides. Set formal g_i=v_i on I and g_i=0 outside
I, including the terminal, and set every f_i=0. Up to an arbitrary
translation, positions inside are v_i, positions on the left are1,
and positions on the right are p.

For an internal ordered pair, Qbar_ij=-(v_i^2-v_j^2). An incoming edge
from the left to i has residual v_i^2-2v_i; an incoming edge from the
right has residual v_i^2-2p v_i; every outgoing edge from i to the
exterior has residual -v_i^2. All-exterior residuals vanish. Using
d_i=h_i, the right side of (2) is therefore

 -sum_(i in I)h_i v_i^2
           -2sum_(i in I)v_i(A_i^out+p B_i^out).

The left side is sum_(i in I)h_i(1-h_i)v_i^2. Since
h_i(2-h_i)v_i^2=v_i^2-v_(i+1)^2, this gives the exact identity

 sum_(i in I)v_i(A_i^out+p B_i^out)=(p^2-1)/2.         (7)

This is a factor-free interval cut identity. Its exterior terms remain
signed through v_i and p. Nonnegative A_i^out,B_i^out alone give no
upper bound on the alternating weighted sum that would compare q with
1/(1+H). Deleting A_i^out for an interior interval is unjustified.
Thus this first direct attempt stops at the signed boundary moment (7).

## 5. Second mechanism: an exact interval-defect identity

The next test retains the exterior times, rather than replacing the
exterior gradients by zero. Define global prefix sums S_i=sum_(t<i)h_t
and put P=S_l. Retain H,v,p,q from Section4 and define formal gradients

 psi_i=1                         if i<l,
 psi_i=v_i                       if l<=i<k,
 psi_i=p                         if k<=i<=r.

Choose x_l=1. The complete original GD equations then give

 x_i=1+S_l-S_i                   if i<l,
 x_i=v_i                         if l<=i<k,
 x_i=p(1-(S_i-S_k))              if k<=i<=r.

Use these formal values:

 f_i=1/2+S_l-S_i                 if i<l,
 f_i=v_i^2/2                     if l<=i<k,
 f_i=p^2/2-p^2(S_i-S_k)          if k<=i<=r.           (8)

Values and positions agree at the two boundaries. This is a FORMAL
polynomial test. It is not claimed to be interpolable by one static
convex smooth function, and is not used as a primal witness. Indeed
some of its residuals below can be negative.

All within-region residuals vanish. The complete list of possibly
nonzero residuals is

 Qbar_ij=2(S_l-S_i)(1-psi_j),    i<l, j>=l,
 Qbar_ji=2(S_j-S_k)p(psi_i-p),   j>=k, i<k.            (9)

The reverse orientation between the left and middle regions, and the
reverse orientation between the middle and right regions, is zero.
For a left/right pair, both expressions in (9) apply to its respective
orientations, so no crossing pair is lost or counted twice.

For example, for i<l and l<=j<k, put d=S_l-S_i. Direct substitution is

 Qbar_ij=1+2d-v_j^2-2v_j(1+d-v_j)-(1-v_j)^2
         =2d(1-v_j),
 Qbar_ji=v_j^2-1-2d-2(v_j-1-d)-(v_j-1)^2=0.

For l<=i<k and j>=k, put b=S_j-S_k. Direct substitution gives
Qbar_ij=0 and Qbar_ji=2b p(v_i-p). For a left/right pair the same
substitution gives 2d(1-p) in the first orientation and 2b p(1-p)
in the second. This proves the full list (9).

Define the two ACTUAL weighted boundary sums

 C_L=sum_(i<l,j>=l)mu_ij(S_l-S_i)(1-psi_j),
 C_R=sum_(j>=k,i<k)mu_ji(S_j-S_k)p(psi_i-p).            (10)

Here empty sums, including C_L when l=0 and C_R when k=r, are zero.
Equation (2) evaluated on (8) is exactly

            (P+1)^2-(P+H+1)^2 p^2=2(C_L+C_R).         (11)

For an explicit check of its left side, write R=s-P-H. The prefix
contribution to 2sum h_i f_i+sum h_i(1-h_i)psi_i^2 is P^2+2P;
the interval contribution is 1-p^2; the suffix contribution is
p^2(2R-R^2). The remaining terminal terms are
-s(s+2)p^2+2sR p^2. Their sum is (P+1)^2-(P+H+1)^2p^2.

Define the hereditary defect

                    Delta_I=(1+H)^2p^2-1.

Since q>0 and 1+H>0, the requested inequality is equivalent to
Delta_I>=0. Rearranging (11) gives the precise original-array formula

 Delta_I=P[P+2-(P+2H+2)p^2]-2(C_L+C_R).              (12)

Thus the remaining coefficient inequality, with ALL original signs,
flow, and mixed matching retained, is

 2(C_L+C_R) <= P[P+2-(P+2H+2)p^2]                    (13)

for every 0<=l<k<=r. This report does not prove (13).

At the first singleton l=0,k=1, C_L=0, p=-t_0<0, and the only i<k
is i=0 with psi_i=1. Consequently every term of C_R is nonpositive,
so (12) proves that boundary case. At a general prefix, C_L remains
zero but psi_i alternates; the same entrywise argument is invalid.
At an interior interval both C_L and C_R must remain in (13).

### 5.1 What summing the prefix crossing flows does and does not provide

For l=0 write H=S_k and, for k<=j<=r, set

 F_j=sum_(i<k)mu_ij, b_j=sum_(i<k)mu_ji,
 B_j=sum_(i<k)mu_ji v_i, w_j=-B_j/p,
 e_j=S_j-S_k.

All F_j,b_j are nonnegative. The signed quantity w_j is not a factor
and is not assumed nonnegative. Multiplying (5), rearranged in its
cumulative form, by v_i and summing i=0,...,k-1 telescopes because
t_i v_i=-v_(i+1). The exact result, including j=r with h_r=0, is

             w_j+h_j sum_(m>j)w_m=F_j.                (18)

More explicitly, the left cumulative sum in (6) telescopes to
-p F_j, and the right one is B_j+h_j sum_(m>j)B_m.
This proves (18) without a separation of the two indices.

The quadratic cut (7), now with no incoming left boundary, gives

             sum_(j>=k)w_j=(p^(-2)-1)/2.              (19)

The original value flow over the prefix gives

             sum_(j>=k)(F_j-b_j)=H.                   (20)

Since e_j=sum_(t=k)^(j-1)h_t, summing (18) yields

 sum_(j>=k)e_j w_j=sum_(j>=k)F_j-sum_(j>=k)w_j.

Also, directly from (10),

                  C_R=-p^2 sum_(j>=k)e_j(w_j+b_j).    (21)

Substitute these formulas in (11). After (19)--(20) cancel the signed
total, the remaining nonnegative boundary moment is exactly

                   sum_(j>=k)(1+e_j)b_j=H^2/2.       (22)

For clarity, this last cancellation reads
1-(1+H)^2p^2=-2p^2[H+sum(1+e_j)b_j-(p^(-2)-1)/2].
Subtract1 and divide by -p^2 to obtain (22).

Thus prefix flow and the quadratic cut fix two totals, (19) and (22),
even in the full unfactored array. The remaining prefix inequality is

                   sum_(j>=k)e_j(w_j+b_j)>=0.         (23)

Backward substitution in (18) is w_j=F_j-h_j sum_(m>j)w_m;
nonnegativity of F_j alone does not provide the sign in (23).
The additional original constraints on the same array have not been
shown to force it here. At k=r-1 only j=r has positive e_j and
w_r=F_r>=0, so (23) holds for that boundary case. This observation is
not an induction through arbitrary intervals: eliminating a suffix
would change the other crossing entries and their equations.

## 6. Minimal-violation screening does not fix the missing sign

If heredity fails, choose an inclusion-minimal nonempty violating
interval I. Every proper nonempty contiguous subinterval then obeys
the proposed bound. For any l<j<k, let H_left and H_right be the step
sums on [l,j) and [j,k), and let q_left,q_right be their excess products.
Then q=q_left q_right<1/(1+H), while

 q_left>=1/(1+H_left), q_right>=1/(1+H_right).

It follows that

 q_left<=q(1+H_right)<1,
 q_right<=q(1+H_left)<1,
                         q<|v_j|<1.                 (14)

Therefore every psi_j<=1, including the two exterior constants, and
C_L>=0. The right-hand summands in (10) still have both signs when
there are internal indices of both relevant parities: p(psi_i-p) is
positive when psi_i has the sign of p and larger magnitude, and is
negative when psi_i has the opposite sign. For a singleton, the left
boundary sum remains a separate issue when l>0.

Thus even restricting (13) to an inclusion-minimal hypothetical
violation does not justify dropping the right-hand positive terms or
the left boundary. It changes the missing mechanism to a quantitative
balance between those ACTUAL edges. This is the precise obstruction
left by the second direct attempt, not a claim that (13) is false.

## 7. Exact full-array counterexample to the entrywise sign shortcut

This construction refutes only the stronger claim that every summand
of C_R is nonpositive for a prefix interval. It DOES NOT refute heredity.

Let d be the positive root of d^2+5d-4=0, put

 C=5+d, kappa=C^2/16, K=kappa-1,
 h=(1+d,3/2,3/2), s=4+d.

The polynomial is negative at0, positive at1 and strictly increasing
on [0,infinity), so 0<d<1. In particular C>5 and K>0. In original
index order0,1,2,3 define the COMPLETE matrix

 Lambda =
 [ 0,       0,           0,             Kd                 ]
 [ 3K/2,    0,           kappa,         kappa/2+3K/2        ]
 [ 3K/2,    7kappa/2,    0,             17kappa/2+3K/2      ]
 [ K,       kappa,       14kappa,       0                  ]. (15)

Every entry is nonnegative, including the two stated off-diagonal zeros.
The root equation implies dC=4 and

                    K(4-d)=2(1+d).                   (16)

Identity (16) follows from the accepted join identity with child
capacities1 and4; it is also checked directly as follows:

 (C^2-16)(4-d)-32(1+d)
 = (9+10d+d^2)(4-d)-32-32d
 = 4-d-6d^2-d^3
 = -(1+d)(d^2+5d-4),

which is zero. Thus the four row-minus-column sums in (15) are

 K(d-4), -3kappa+3K, -3kappa+3K,
                            6kappa-K(d+2)
 = -2(1+d), -3, -3, 8+2d,

the exact required flow.

For the full matrix check, the two-step child (3/2,3/2) has array

 Lambda_child=[ [0,1,1/2], [7/2,0,17/2], [1,14,0] ].

Its flow is (-3,-3,6), its L_child T_child is exactly

 [ -3/4, -7/4, -1/2 ]
 [  7/4, -3/4, -17/2]
 [  1/2, 17/2, 15   ],

and its D_child is diag(3/4,3/4,-15). Hence its remainder is zero on
all three independent formal gradients. The empty left child has
capacity1 and zero remainder. Fact04219554e41d9750, Section11, applied
with right capacity4 and d(1+4+d)=4, gives

 V_h=kappa V_child-K[(3/2)Q_10+(3/2)Q_20+Q_30
                               +d Q_03+(3/2)Q_13+(3/2)Q_23].

Inserting the just-checked child identity gives exactly every entry
of (15). Therefore its FULL original remainder M is identically zero;
no restricted Gram test, quotient, or unidentified gradient is involved.

Now take the prefix interval I=[0,2). Its local data are

 v_0=1, v_1=-d, p=d/2, H=5/2+d,
 S_3-S_2=3/2, mu_30=Lambda_03/2=Kd/2.

The (j,i)=(3,0) summand of C_R is

                 (3Kd^2/8)(1-d/2)>0.                (17)

Thus even an actual full M=0 array does not give a nonpositive sign
to each right-boundary term. The opposing (3,1) term is negative.
Their total is nonpositive, as required for this particular interval:

 (1+H)q=(7/2+d)d/2=2-3d/4>1.

This is a counterexample to an attempted proof mechanism only. For
clarity, the entire word in (15) satisfies heredity: its singleton
products are d(d+2)=4-3d>1 and5/4,5/4; the left two-step interval
has product2-3d/4>1; the right two-step interval has product1;
the full interval has product C d/4=1.

No capacity comparison or original minimax disproof is asserted.

## 8. Failure synthesis and bounded-round handoff

The mixed-coefficient route was carried through in two forms:

* the isolated interval quadratic gives (7), with signed incoming
  moments from BOTH exterior sides;
* the affine/quadratic/affine formal test gives (12), reducing the
  desired sign to the explicit weighted crossing bound (13). Even
  the pure-prefix reduction, with exact cut totals (19) and (22),
  still needs the signed weighted-tail inequality (23).

The first method has no bound on its alternating left and right
moments. The second needs an upper bound on their combined weighted
sum, not nonnegativity of the original coefficients individually.
Minimality of a hypothetical violating interval only gives (14).
The exact full array (15) rules out the stronger entrywise C_R<=0
argument, while continuing to satisfy interval heredity itself.

Method status: BLOCKED at (13). Target status: interval implication
UNRESOLVED; no actual interval-violating array has been produced.
There is no claim of a lossless reduction of the full primitive cone
to M=0, no hereditary capacity theorem for this cone, and no conclusion
about general M>=0 or the original exact-profile Goal.

The earlier zero-step and global product restrictions are only
bookkeeping inputs rederived inside this joint report. The positive
factor weighted-tail argument was not reused as a premise or silently
extended to the full array. The complete crossing expressions, including
their unfavorable terms, are the handoff for a different future method.

Primary-source retrieval: two Matlas queries about the exact hereditary
claim returned unrelated statements. Web retrieval did not locate a
factor-free hereditary theorem. The exact local source Salah R. Chikhi,
Last-Iterate Performance of Gradient Descent and Relaxed Proximal Point
via s-Composability, arXiv:2609.13765v2, Section4 and its coefficient
proof were read. Its constant-step two-sequence construction is the
already audited restricted regime, and supplies no missing crossing
bound. No external theorem is used as an unverified black box here.

One assigned round is exhausted on return. No further round or resource
is inferred. All new algebra and the mechanism counterexample remain
UNVERIFIED; the fact graph has not been changed.

# Two actual children in one projection body

Status: joint proof draft, **UNVERIFIED**. Eighth launch, cumulative round18.
No solver, parameter sweep, installation or verifier was used. The original
all-schedule exact-profile target is unresolved. Accepted facts
1252b8823bb5a290 and b98bb807d514e80d specify the original interpolation model,
performance W_n, and exact comparison B_n; every new construction below is
derived here. Earlier unverified packages are not premises.

## 1. Full input, function, values, and true distance

A child consists of a nonnegative finite word p of length ell, actual vectors
x_i,g_i for 0<=i<=ell, and the optimum sample x_*=g_*=0. Set

    K=conv(0,g_0,...,g_ell), y_i=x_i-g_i,
    x_(i+1)=x_i-p_i g_i,
    <g_j-g_i,y_i><=0 for every i,j,
    <g_i,y_i>>=0 for every i,
    x_ell=g_ell=w !=0.                                      (1)

Include ALL samples, even duplicated ones or those at zero steps. Nonzero
residuals y_i are permitted. Set

    A=1+sum p_i, R_i=sum_(t=i)^(ell-1) p_t,
    R^2=||x_0||^2, Delta=A^2||w||^2-R^2,
    kappa=A^2||w||^2/R^2.

The values and common function are specified, not freely shifted:

    F_K(x)=max_(g in K)(<x,g>-||g||^2/2),
    f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2, f_*=0.                        (2)

The maximizer is the unique Euclidean projection onto K: completing the
square proves this, and its exact optimality condition is (1). Adding the
two projection normals for x,x' proves
||P_K(x)-P_K(x')||^2<=<x-x',P_K(x)-P_K(x')>. Thus it is 1-Lipschitz.
Evaluation of the defining maxima at each other's maximizers bounds the
differentiability remainder by ||x-x'||^2. Consequently F_K is finite,
convex, differentiable and 1-smooth, with gradient P_K and values (2).

The minimizer set is exactly K^polar={z:<z,g><=0 for every g in K}.
The forward implication follows by using epsilon*g in (2) and sending
epsilon down to0; the reverse implication follows directly from (2).
The endpoint and GD identity give

    x_0=w+sum_(i<ell) p_i g_i.                              (3)

Hence <x_0,z><=0 for every minimizer z, so 0 is a closest minimizer and
dist(x_0,argmin F_K)^2=R^2. No arbitrary chosen-optimum normalization is used.
For the original signed interpolation residual of fact1252b8823bb5a290,
substitution of (2) gives the identity

    Q_ij=<g_i-g_j,y_j>.                                    (4)

This includes Q_i*=0 and Q_*i=-<g_i,y_i><=0. Thus checking (1) checks EVERY
original interpolation inequality, including both optimum incidences.

The input contains these vectors and values, not merely Delta or kappa.

## 2. A real cross Gram block and its exact legality conditions

Take two inputs L,R with capacities A,C, initial squared distances R_L^2,
R_R^2, and endpoints w_L,w_R. Choose a bridge 1+r with r>0. Write
e=w_L/||w_L|| and d=x_R,0/R_R. Embed the two spaces in
R direct-sum e^perp direct-sum d^perp by

    J_L u=(<u,e>, u-<u,e>e, 0),
    J_R v=(-<v,d>, 0, v-<v,d>d),
    a_scale=r||w_L||/R_R.                                 (5)

Both J are real isometries. Use J_L x_L,i,J_L g_L,i for the complete left
transcript and a_scale J_R x_R,j,a_scale J_R g_R,j for the complete right
transcript. The bridge is exact because

    J_L w_L-(1+r)J_L w_L=-r J_L w_L=a_scale J_R x_R,0.

All child updates are unchanged. The endpoint is a_scale J_R w_R. The
cross Gram block, on any pair of child vectors, is explicitly

    <J_L u,a_scale J_R v>
       =-r <u,w_L><v,x_R,0>/R_R^2.                         (6)

Real PSD follows from (5), not from an unspecified positive block matrix.
Left values are unchanged; right values are a_scale^2 f_R,j. Let the parent
body be the convex hull of0 and EVERY displayed parent gradient, including
the left bridge gradient J_L w_L and the terminal gradient.

Within-child and optimum normals remain (1), with the right ones scaled
by a_scale^2. The only additional inequalities are, for every i,j,

    -r <g_R,j,x_R,0><w_L,y_L,i>/R_R^2
          <= <g_L,i,y_L,i>,                               (7L)
    -<g_L,i,w_L><x_R,0,y_R,j>
          <= r||w_L||^2 <g_R,j,y_R,j>.                     (7R)

These follow by substituting (6) into the two cross normals; conversely they
are those normals. They include every old gradient against every new residual
and the bridge gradient against all right samples. Thus (7L)-(7R) are exact
necessary and sufficient conditions for this SPECIFIED function and data.
When they hold, (2)-(4) provide the common function, values, all Q inequalities,
and true initial distance R_L, since (3) uses retained left gradients.

The physical parent capacity is P=A+C+r. Its exact ratio is

    kappa_P=P^2 r^2 kappa_L kappa_R/(A^2 C^2).              (8)

For the source primitive bridge rP=AC, equivalently
r=(-A-C+sqrt((A+C)^2+4AC))/2, this is

    kappa_P=kappa_L kappa_R,
    Delta_P=R_L^2[(1+Delta_L/R_L^2)(1+Delta_R/R_R^2)-1].    (9)

The cross term in (9) is an exact consequence of a legal construction ONLY
when (7) holds. No favorable interaction is presumed from two scalar defects.

## 3. The planar core blocks even arbitrary transverse correlations

Here is the previously detected planar core, rederived. Set s=sqrt2,
lambda=1+s, theta=s-1. At its source word (s,2,s), take

    U=1/lambda^2, eta=-(1/2+theta)/s,
    u=(1/lambda,0), v=(eta,sqrt(1/2-eta^2))/lambda,
    w=-u-s v,
    (x_i,g_i)=((lambda u,u),(u,u),(-u,v),(w,w)).             (10)

We have eta=-1+1/(2s), so eta^2<1/2. The residuals are s u,0,-u-v,0.
At sample0 all four vertex normals against0,u,v,w are nonpositive:
<v,u>=eta U<U and <w,u>=(s-3/2)U<U. At sample2,
<v,y>=(1/2-1/(2s))U>0, the u normal is -U/2, and
<w-v,y>=0 by direct substitution. The zero normal is -<v,y><0.
Thus (10) satisfies the full input contract. It has R^2=1,
||w||^2=1/lambda^4, A=lambda^2 and kappa=1.

Consider ANY isometric endpoint-aligned splice with this right child,
regardless of how transverse coordinates are correlated. The actual bridge
gradient necessarily represents -x_R,0/r in right-child units. At right
sample2 its required normal is

    <-x_R,0/r-v,y_2><=0,
    r >= -<x_R,0,y_2>/<v,y_2>=lambda^2=A.                  (11)

The equality follows from
-<x_R,0,y_2>=lambda(1+eta)U and
<v,y_2>=theta(1/2)U/s. The balanced primitive bridge is r=theta A<A.
It therefore fails this ORIGINAL cross-child inequality even at zero defect.
Changing the remaining cross Gram block cannot affect (11).

For completeness the actual modified planar cores use
p0=(16-11s)/3, q0=(8s-10)/3, 0<=t<=1/50,
a=s-p0 t, b=2+q0 t, z=b-1, A=1+2a+b,

    U=1/(a+1)^2,
    eta=-[z^2+(a+1)/2]/[z(a+2)],
    u=(1/(a+1),0), v=(eta,sqrt(1/2-eta^2))/(a+1),
    w=-z u-a v,
    (x_i,g_i)=(((a+1)u,u),(u,u),(-z u,v),(w,w)).             (12)

Squaring rational endpoints gives 1.4142<s<1.4143. It follows that
1.411<a<1.415, 1<=z<1.01, -.654<eta<-.639,
-.1<<u,w>/U<0, -.062<<v,w>/U<0. Also
||w||^2=J U with
J=[z^2(2-a)+a(a^2-2)/2]/(a+2), and .169<J<.177.
These estimates follow by separately bounding the displayed numerators;
in particular .654^2<1/2 proves a real Gram realization. At sample0 the
u-normal tests hold by these correlations. At sample2 the normals against0
and u are (z eta+1/2)U<0 and
[-z+(z-1)eta+1/2]U<=-U/2; the w normal is identically0.
Samples1 and3 have zero residual. Thus these are actual cores, not formal jets.

Writing N=z^2(2-a)+a(a^2-2)/2 and mu=q0-2p0=10s-14>0,
differentiation of A^2 J/(a+1)^2 gives logarithmic derivative

    2mu/A+2p0/(a+1)+N'/N+p0/(a+2),
    N'=2z(2-a)q0-p0[-z^2+(3a^2-2)/2].

The rational bounds .147<p0<.149, .437<q0<.439, .578<N<.603
give N'>.361 and the displayed logarithmic derivative>3/4.
Its initial value is1; integration proves kappa>=1+3t/4.
These defective inputs do not repair the alignment obstruction:
-<x_0,y_2>/<v,y_2>=(a+1)(z+eta)/(-z eta-1/2)>4.97,
whereas the balanced source bridge theta A<2.43. The ratio bound uses
(a+1)(z+eta)>2.411*.346 and -z eta-1/2<1.01*.654-.5.
Thus this entire parameter interval fails the bridge normal, independently
of any free transverse correlation.

## 4. A second actual core retains its nonzero first post-step residual

For arbitrary a>=0, b>1 put c=a, H=a+1, z=b-1, xi=z/H>0, and take
orthogonal vectors U=(1,0), V=(0,sqrt(xi)). The word is (a,b,a), and

    x_0=(H+z)U+H V,       g_0=U,
    x_1=b U+H V,         g_1=U,
    x_2=H V,             g_2=V,
    x_3=V,               g_3=V.                            (13)

The body is conv(0,U,V). At sample0 the V normal is z-(a+b-1)=-a.
At sample1 it is z-(b-1)=0. At sample2 the residual is aV and its U and0
normals are -a||V||^2. At sample3 the residual is0. At samples0 and1,
the zero normals are -(a+b-1) and -(b-1). This lists every vertex normal.
The value formula (2) and true distance proof (3) apply; in particular
y_1=zU+HV is nonzero and is retained everywhere below.

Writing D=1+3xi+xi^2, its exact data are

    A=H(2+xi), R^2=H^2 D, ||w||^2=xi,
    kappa=xi(2+xi)^2/D,
    kappa-1=(1+xi)(xi^2+2xi-1)/D.                          (14)

All four boundary pairings <g_i,w>,<y_i,w>,<g_i,x_0>,<y_i,x_0>
are nonnegative. Therefore ANY two such cores satisfy (7): each left side
is nonpositive and each right side nonnegative. Formula (9) is consequently
an actual, loss-free, simultaneous two-defect aggregation for these inputs.

For the same modified word as (12), H=lambda-p0 t and z=1+q0 t.
Then xi>theta for t>0; (14) proves kappa>1. This is a SECOND defective
core for the SAME physical three-step word, including the face missed by
the x_1=g_1 restriction. In fact xi-theta=(q0+theta p0)t/H>t/5,
and differentiating the middle expression in (14) gives
kappa'(xi)=1+(2xi+3)/D^2>1. Thus kappa>=1+t/5 uniformly on 0<=t<=1/50.
At t=0, kappa=1 exactly. This is a direct algebraic proof, not a reused draft.

## 5. Exact boundary state and a finite obstruction to repeating the join

For samples with e_i=<g_i,y_i>>0 define

    a_i=<g_i,w>/||w||^2, c_i=<g_i,x_0>/R^2,
    B_i=<w,y_i>/e_i, D_i=<x_0,y_i>/e_i,
    k=<x_0,w>/R^2, zeta=||w||^2/R^2.                       (15)

Include all gradient samples in a,c. If a residual has e_i=0, use the original
inequalities (7); division by e_i is not licensed. In the seed (13), e_i=0
only for zero residuals, and this property survives isometric joins.
For two identical children the tests reduce exactly to

    r max_(i,j)(-c_j B_i)<=1,
    max_(i,j)(-a_i D_j)<=r.                                (16)

The parent's retained boundary data are

    a_left=-[k/(r zeta)]a,       a_right=a,
    B_left=-r k B,              B_right=B,
    c_left=c,                   c_right=-r k c,
    D_left=D,                   D_right=-[k/(r zeta)]D,
    k_parent=-r k^2, zeta_parent=r^2 zeta^2.                (17)

These follow by substituting (6), and preserve each sample rather than
assuming extrema are sufficient for an arbitrary Gram completion.

Now repeat the identical-child construction with source primitive bridges:
A_j=lambda^j A_0, r_j=theta A_j, H_0=(a,b,a), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+r_j,H_j).
Use seed (13) with xi>=theta. Its length is3, so H_j has length4*2^j-1.
Set omega=H/A_0=1/(2+xi)<=theta and q=A_0 k_0=omega kappa_0.
The exact formulas give 0<q<1 because
D-xi(2+xi)=1+xi>0. Set u=lambda omega<=1 and v=theta q<1.
In the seed, max a=1, max B=1, max D_i=A_0, and
max c=c_*=(1+xi)/(H D); all four lists are nonnegative. The extrema are
attained: B=1 and D=A_0 at sample1, and c=c_* at sample0.

After the first join their signed extrema are

    a in[-u,1], B in[-v,1], c in[-v c_*,c_*], D_i in[-u A_0,A_0]. (18)

For later formal joins, (17) gives A_j k_j=-q^(2^j) and
k_j/(r_j zeta_j)=-lambda omega^(2^j) for j>=1.
Thus the newly multiplied pieces in (17) have factors between0 and1,
and every extremum in (18) persists at an actual inherited sample.
Consequently, for every j>=1, the exact tests (16) are

    r_j v c_*<=1,      u A_0<=r_j.                         (19)

The second holds for all j>=1 because u<=1 and r_1=A_0. For the first set

    T(xi)=xi(2+xi)^2(1+xi)/(1+3xi+xi^2)^2.

Then r_j v c_*=lambda^(j-2) T(xi). We have 0<T(xi)<1 because
D^2-xi(2+xi)^2(1+xi)=xi^3+3xi^2+2xi+1>0.
Also T is increasing: its positive logarithmic derivative has numerator
2+xi+xi^2+xi^3 after multiplication by xi(1+xi)(2+xi)D.
At xi=theta, T=2-s>theta. Therefore joins j=0,1,2 are legal, producing
actual H_1,H_2,H_3 of lengths7,15,31, with kappa_j=kappa_0^(2^j).
But join j=3 necessarily fails since lambda T(xi)>=lambda(2-s)=s>1.

This failure is a SPECIFIC original inequality. In the proposed H_4 of
length63, left sample25 inherits B=-v (base sample1, then two right-copy
embeddings); right sample32 inherits c=c_* (the right child's first sample).
Its signed interpolation residual is

    Q_(32,25)=e_25[lambda T(xi)-1]>0,                       (20)

where e_25>0. Both children H_3 are legal, including for every t>0 above,
and both carry positive defects. Their next rank-one cross block is real
PSD and satisfies GD, but violates (20). This pinpoints why the formal
product kappa_0^16 is NOT a valid next-level witness.

## 6. Calibration, zero mass, and scope

At t=0 the actual seed and its first three legal joins have kappa=1,
so the construction gives no spurious positive source defect. The illegal
next join is discarded rather than used against an upper certificate.
The exact finite comparison remains B_n, not an assumed Silver equality.
A subsequent objective-tail conversion with the exact source ConPD bridge
has payoff (1+delta)/B_concat with its actual mass B_concat; its derivation
and calibration are included in the next joint continuation package.

The generic input and cross tests in sections1-2 include empty or zero-mass
children: then A=1, all child points and gradients equal w, and y_i=0.
No test is divided by a zero residual or a zero mass. The obstruction in
section5 concerns the explicitly nonempty orthogonal seed with b>1;
it does not extend a nonempty-right dilution claim to empty right blocks.

The modified-seed repeated-word family changes the leading primitive mass:
A_j(t)=lambda^j[lambda^2+(10s-14)t], whereas A_j(0)=lambda^(j+2).
Their ratio is the fixed number1+(10s-14)t/lambda^2>1. This is not the
previously closed common-offset family with only O(n) additional mass.
The actual unbuffered child hypotheses have been proved only through H_3;
(20) forbids extending this particular construction further. No all-h
decomposition, primitive-capacity bound, or asymptotic conclusion follows.

The next operation is to retain both actual children but add explicit
constant-gradient coordinates to pay for their signed boundary correlations.
Its true-distance cost must be carried in the ratio law; it is not a free
translation, a free function-value shift, or a consequence of PSD alone.
