# The first landing price closes the fixed odd kink family

**JOINT UNVERIFIED — one self-contained proof draft, no new accepted fact.**

Project gd-exact-profile-20260926; worker max3; invocation85 after ended84;
completed ceiling85 / attempted86; controller cap1. Current assignment and
guidance82bfc9d550f46b42; plan06531aafba00f849.

This report carries through the prescribed FIRST mechanism: charge the first
exterior sign-crossing landing to the ORIGINAL radius, then assemble the
entire fixed odd-family bound. No second mechanism is activated. The stronger
3/2 terminal-gradient assertion is not needed and is not decided.

Every mathematical ingredient used in the proof is derived below. Earlier
drafts3f622b3ff76b77fd and a0925e826bbc6304 supplied awareness, not accepted
premises. No fact_submit or other formal call was permitted in this allocation.
The theorem below is a candidate theorem, not verifier-accepted truth.

## 1. Statement and notation

Fix one integer m>=1536, and use these SAME enclosing parameters at every depth:

    epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon, lambda=1+sqrt(2),
    c=lambda*(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,
    d_j=a*c^j, h_j^lev=1+d_j,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,h_j^lev,H_j), 0<=j<m,
    S_j=sum H_j, A_j=1+S_j,
    S=S_m, D=1+2S, n=2^m-1, ell=1+a.

The n chronological physical steps are h_i, 0<=i<n. Each occurrence of a
level-j bridge has size h_j^lev. The auxiliary d_m is not an extra step.
The symbols in this report have these local meanings; in particular H_j,
G, M_j, and A_j do not redefine similarly named project-global symbols.

Choose any finite B>=kappa and any 0<kappa<=1. Put

    v=1-kappa, u=v+B,
    G(r)=min(B,max(kappa*r,r-v)), r>=0,
    g(x)=sign(x)*G(|x|), g(0)=0,
    f(x)=integral_0^x g(t)dt, F(r)=f(r), r>=0.

This is ONE fixed odd gradient law and its even objective throughout each
trajectory. Let Psi_j(s) be the actual complete H_j output starting at s, and

    M_j(R)=max_(|s|<=R)|Psi_j(s)|.

For an actual nonzero start s, R=|s| is its ORIGINAL radius, including whenever
a proof examines one of its children. The actual full-word recursion is
x_0=s, x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g(x_i), and x_n=Psi_m(s).

**Candidate whole-family theorem.** For all the parameters just specified,
and every nonzero start s,

    2f(Psi_m(s))/s^2 <= (1+21/(4m))/D < 17/(16D).       (1.1)

All original physical steps and the actual terminal are retained. The bound
is uniform in kappa, B, s, and the same enclosing m. The fixed law may have
a collapsed middle interval B=kappa or v=0.

The proof splits the parameter domain as follows.

* If kappa*(1+d_(m-1))<=1, the bound is (1+21/(4m))/D.
* If kappa*S>=12, the bound is 1/D.
* Every remaining parameter choice has
  5<kappa*S<12 and
  kappa*(1+d_(m-2))<1<kappa*(1+d_(m-1)).
  Both root children therefore preserve the core individually. For this
  entire remaining band the bound is 1/D.

The new first-landing estimate and its exhaustive use appear in Sections9-11.
Sections2-8 supply the full quantitative ingredients needed for this assembly,
without citing earlier unverified mathematics.

This is a restricted scalar function-family result. It is not a bound on the
supremum over ALL smooth convex objectives in the original PROBLEM.md. The
full-class hypothesis in accepted fact931b9653ce0f7201 remains unproved by
this report, and the original all-word minimax Goal remains OPEN.

## 2. One legal objective, its exact areas, and basic ray estimates

Integration gives the exact energy, including all knot and tail areas:

    2F(r)=kappa*r^2,                         0<=r<=1,
    2F(r)=(r-v)^2+kappa*v,                   1<=r<=u,
    2F(r)=2B*r-C_tail,                       r>=u,
    C_tail=B^2+v*(2B-kappa).                         (2.1)

At r=1 both adjacent values equal kappa; at r=u both equal B^2+kappa*v.
The first derivatives agree as well. The middle interval collapses when
B=kappa. When kappa=1, v=0 and this is the unit-curvature Huber law.

The derivative g is continuous and nondecreasing, with piecewise slopes
kappa,1,0. Partitioning any interval at the knots proves that it is
1-Lipschitz. Thus f is everywhere finite, convex, differentiable, and
1-smooth. Its unique minimizer is0, because G(r)>0 for every r>0. Its value
there is0, and its true initial distance is |s|.

For completeness the complete actual transcript is smooth-convex legal.
For x<y put Delta=g(y)-g(x), so 0<=Delta<=y-x. On [x,y],

    g(t)-g(x)>=max(0,Delta-(y-t)),
    g(y)-g(t)>=max(0,Delta-(t-x)).

Integrating each lower triangle yields Delta^2/2. These two inequalities
give every ordered interpolation row

    f_i-f_j+g_i*(x_j-x_i)+(g_i-g_j)^2/2<=0

for actual physical, terminal, and optimum samples. Repeated positions
have identical values and gradients. The scalar Gram matrix is positive
semidefinite: each quadratic form is the square of the corresponding real
linear combination of actual vectors. No terminal sample or ordered pair
is omitted.

We shall use

    0<=G(r)<=min(r,B),
    G(r)>=kappa*min(r,1).                              (2.2)

For the first inequality, kappa*r<=r and r-v<=r. For the second, inside
the core equality holds; outside the core both the cap B and the uncapped
gradient are at least kappa.

For p=G(r), monotonicity and smoothness give G(t)<=min(t,p) on [0,r].
Since p<=r, integration yields

    2F(r)<=2r*p-p^2.                                  (2.3)

For r<=u a second conversion is valid:

    2F(r)<=G(r)^2/kappa.                              (2.4)

It is equality in the core. In the middle p=r-v>=kappa, and the difference
between its right and left sides is v*(p^2-kappa^2)/kappa>=0.
This covers r=1,u and B=kappa. It is NOT asserted beyond the cap knot:
the energy there keeps growing although the gradient is constant.

A strict sign crossing means two successive nonzero iterates have opposite
signs. If a trajectory never strictly crosses and never hits zero, all
radii decrease. For a segment of total step mass T, terminal radius r,
terminal gradient p, and original segment radius R, all its queried
gradients are at least p. Therefore

    R>=r+T*p,
    2F(r)<=2R*p-(1+2T)*p^2<=R^2/(1+2T).              (2.5)

The last inequality is completion of the square. A zero hit stays zero.
In particular every full-word history without a strict crossing has score
at most1/D, including a final same-side entry from the middle or tail.

For an advertised smoothness L_adv>0, the objective L_adv*f and physical
steps h_i/L_adv yield exactly these points and this normalized payoff.
The project normalization remains mathcal W=2*mathcal R.

## 3. Same-m masses, finite margins, and the actual suffix

The enclosing parameter choices imply

    0<epsilon<1/1000, beta>999/1000,
    12/5<c<17/7<5/2,
    2/5<a<1/2, beta>c-2, c*a=beta<1.                 (3.1)

For the c bounds use 7/5<sqrt(2)<17/12 and
(29/12)*(1001/1000)<17/7. Then
a>(99/100)*(7/17)>2/5 and a<1/(12/5)<1/2.
The remaining assertions follow from these bounds.

Set eta=a/(c-2). The identity lambda*(lambda-2)=1 and c>lambda give
c*(c-2)>1>beta, hence 0<eta<1. The exact word recursion gives

    A_(j+1)=2A_j+d_j,
    A_j=(1-eta)*2^j+eta*c^j.                        (3.2)

The formula follows by substitution in the recursion and A_0=1. In particular,

    A_j>d_j/(c-2) for every j>=0,
    A_j<c^j for j>=1,
    (c-2)*d_j<A_j,
    D<2c^m=2d_m/a.                                 (3.3)

The third inequality follows from the first and c-2<1.
For L_j=S_j/d_j and j>=1,

    L_j>7/5.                                       (3.4)

At j=1, L_1=(1+a)/beta>7/5. If L_j>7/5, then
L_(j+1)=(2L_j+1+1/d_j)/c>(19/5)/(17/7)>7/5,
which proves (3.4).

The following generous finite margins will be useful:

    S>1000, d_(m-1)>9, d_(m-1)>1/epsilon,
    d_m>2/a, d_m^2>D, D/S<201/100.                 (3.5)

Indeed a>2/5 and c>2 imply d_(m-1)>2^m/5.
For integers m>=8, 2^m>20m follows from its value at8 and induction:
doubling a value above20m is above20(m+1). Consequently
d_(m-1)>4m=1/epsilon. For m>=1536 this also gives the bounds9 and1000,
using S>=1+d_(m-1). Moreover 2/a<5 and d_m>d_(m-1)>9, proving
d_m>2/a; (3.3) then gives d_m^2>D. Finally D/S=2+1/S<201/100.

At any level-k physical bridge in a complete H_j, let P be its chronological
prefix mass measured from the beginning of THAT complete word. The occurrence
has its full immediate left H_k present, so P>=S_k. Its physical suffix is

    H_k, h_(l_1)^lev,H_(l_1), ..., h_(l_t)^lev,H_(l_t),
    k<l_1<...<l_t<=j-1.                              (3.6)

To obtain this list ascend its binary word tree. Each ancestor entered
through its left child contributes its bridge and full right child;
each ancestor entered through its right child contributes nothing.
Thus (3.6) keeps every actual step and allows skipped levels without
inserting a fictitious child factor.

## 4. One-step branches and the first-crossing price

For h=1+d>=1 the positive-source signed update is

    (1-h*kappa)*r,          0<=r<=1,
    h*v-d*r,               1<=r<=u,
    r-h*B,                 r>=u.                    (4.1)

The expressions agree at r=1,u. The exact image radius of [-R,R] is

    E_h(R)=max(
       |1-h*kappa|*min(R,1),
       [d*min(R,u)-h*v]_+,
       [R-h*B]_+ ),                                  (4.2)

where [w]_+=max(w,0). Each available affine piece has its extrema at
an endpoint. The middle positive endpoint is bounded by the core
endpoint; its negative endpoint gives the second term. The tail has
its negative endpoint at the cap knot and its positive endpoint at R.
If R<1, the unavailable middle expression satisfies
dR-hv<=(h*kappa-1)R, since the difference is h*v*(R-1)<=0.
If R<u, a positive unavailable tail expression is at most the actual
positive endpoint R-h*G(R). These observations prove (4.2) also when
a formal piece is absent.

Each signed update is continuous, odd, and fixes0, so its interval image
is the entire symmetric interval specified by (4.2). Thus M_j is a
nondecreasing interval-image radius, and exactly

    M_(j+1)=M_j composed with E_(1+d_j) composed with M_j. (4.3)

These are images of actual compatible starts. No recurrence was executed.

For a strict crossing with source r and gradient p=G(r), its landing z obeys

    z=(1+d)*p-r<=d*p.                                (4.4)

For a pre-cap source r<u with a positive same-side output, necessarily
h*kappa<1, and that output lies in the core. For r<=1 this is direct
from (4.1). For 1<=r<u, the output equals
1-h*kappa-d*(r-1), which is at most1-h*kappa<1.
In either case its radius is bounded both by (1-h*kappa)*r and by
(1-h*kappa)*min(r,1). A source exactly at u may instead be classified
as saturated; its two formulas agree.

If all steps of H_j satisfy h*kappa<=1, the core [0,1] is absorbing
and its radius contracts with nonnegative factors. The full physical
core product is

    P_j(kappa)=product_(h in H_j)(1-kappa*h)
       <=exp(-kappa*S_j).                            (4.5)

The inequality follows by multiplying 1-z<=exp(-z). No suffix is dropped.

Consider a first strict crossing of a complete-word trajectory with
exterior source r>1. Its gradient satisfies r>=v+p: in the middle it
is equality, and on the tail r>=u=v+B=v+p. If its level is k and P
is its original prefix mass, all earlier radii decreased and their
gradients were at least p. Thus

    R>=r+P*p>=v+(1+P)*p>=v+A_k*p.                    (4.6)

Strict crossing also requires p>v/d_k. Put

    Q=(c-1)/(c-2)=1+1/(c-2)>10/3.

When v>0, (4.6) and (3.3) give the original-radius price

    R>v*(1+A_k/d_k)>v*Q.                             (4.7)

In a complete word whose steps all have h*kappa<=1, the first crossing
necessarily has exterior source, since any prior core entry is absorbing.
There v>0 for a nonempty word. Thus (4.7) applies to every crossing
history of such a word, even if its later first core entry is at the
very last physical step.


## 5. Joint discrete-amplitude capture at every child depth

We prove simultaneously for all j>=1 up to m:

    G(M_j(d_j*c^(-q)*B))<=a*c^(-q)*B
       for every integer q>=0,                     (5.1)

and

    any strict crossing in a complete H_j
       implies terminal gradient<=aB.              (5.2)

The law, cap B, and core knot1 remain fixed for all q. This is a discrete
amplitude grid, not an assertion for arbitrary amplitudes with B replaced.

First explain how (5.2) follows using only (5.1) at proper-child depths.
After a level-k crossing, (4.4) gives landing<=d_k*B. If k>=1, the
immediate full H_k gives terminal gradient<=aB by its q=0 grid bound.
If k=0, no H_0 steps follow locally; the landing itself has gradient
at most its radius, hence at most aB. At any later retained ancestor
l from (3.6), the incoming gradient is at most aB<B and so its source
is pre-cap. If this bridge crosses, its landing is at most d_l*aB<=d_lB,
and the full H_l again gives gradient<=aB. If it stays on the same side
with nonzero output, Section4 puts the output in the core without
increasing its gradient, and kappa*(1+d_l)<1. Every following H_l step
is then core preserving, and its gradient cannot increase. Zero is
absorbing. This accounts for the entire literal suffix in (3.6).
For H_j every child depth invoked here is at most j-1.

For the base j=1, set w=c^(-q)*B and R=d_1*w=beta*w. Since R<B,
there is no positive saturated stay. Every crossing has landing
at most a*G(r)<=a*r<=a*R. Every positive pre-cap same-side stay
has gradient at most

    kappa*[1-ell*kappa]_+*min(R,1)<=a*R,

because kappa*[1-ell*kappa]_+<=1/(4ell)<a; the last inequality follows
from 4aell>4*(2/5)*(7/5)>1. These are all cases, proving the base
grid bound (even with aR<a*w). The preceding suffix argument or (4.4)
also proves (5.2) at j=1.

Assume both conclusions through j>=1. Start the parent H_(j+1) at
any actual radius

    R<=d_(j+1)*c^(-q)*B=c*d_j*w, w=c^(-q)*B.

Write y for the actual first-child output radius, and z for the
root-bridge output radius. We partition that original bridge.

(a) A strict crossing. If q=0, G(y)<=B=w. If q>=1, the first child
receives a radius at most d_j*c^(-(q-1))*B, so its grid bound yields

    G(y)<=a*c^(-(q-1))*B=beta*w<=w.

Its bridge landing is therefore at most d_j*w. The full right H_j
and its q-th grid bound yield terminal gradient<=a*w.

(b) A saturated positive same-side stay, including y=u. The first
child could not have crossed, since (5.2) would give G(y)<=aB<B.
It also could not have hit zero. Its radii thus decreased to y>=u,
so every one of its queried gradients was exactly B. Consequently
y=R-S_j*B, and

    z=R-(A_j+d_j)*B
       <=c*d_j*w-(A_j+d_j)*B<=d_j*w.                (5.3)

For the last inequality, w<=B and A_j>(c-2)*d_j from (3.3)
give A_j+d_j>(c-1)*d_j. The complete second-child grid applies.
In particular this step does not infer all-tail behavior from a
mere bound on y; it first rules out every first-child crossing.

(c) A positive pre-cap same-side stay with no crossing in the first
child. By Section4, kappa*(1+d_j)<1 and the complete right child
stays in the core. Set t=kappa*d_j and L=L_j=S_j/d_j. Before this
bridge the first-child radii decreased, so

    R>=y+S_j*G(y)>=(1+kappa*S_j)*y.

The second inequality uses G(y)>=kappa*y at this PRE-CAP source.
No such lower bound is used for saturated large radii. By (4.5),

    p_out<=kappa*exp(-L*t)*(1-kappa-t)*R/(1+L*t)
       <=c*t*(1-t)*exp(-L*t)/(1+L*t)*w
       <=c*t*(1-t)/(1+L*t)^2*w
       <=c/[4*(1+L)]*w<a*w.                       (5.4)

Here 0<t<1, exp(Lt)>=1+Lt, and

    (1+L*t)^2-4*(1+L)*t*(1-t)=(1-(L+2)*t)^2.

Finally c/[4(1+L)]<(17/7)/(4*(12/5))=85/336<2/5<a.

(d) A positive pre-cap same-side stay with a crossing in the first
child. Again kappa*(1+d_j)<1, so both children have absorbing cores.
The first exterior crossing in the original left child pays R>vQ
by (4.7). The bridge output is at most v-t, where t=kappa*d_j,
and the complete second child has the product (4.5). Hence

    p_out<=kappa*(v-t)*exp(-L*t).

Since R<=c*d_j*w and R>vQ, one has w>vQ/(c*d_j). Therefore

    p_out/w<c*t*(v-t)*exp(-L*t)/(vQ)
       <=c*v/(4Q)<=c/(4Q)<a.                       (5.5)

Here 0<t<v, t*(v-t)<=v^2/4, and
c/(4Q)<(17/7)/(40/3)=51/280<2/5<a.

A zero bridge output stays zero. Cases(a)-(d) exhaust all other
bridge outcomes, proving (5.1) at the parent. The suffix argument
above then proves (5.2) at the parent using only proper-child
plateaus. This is a noncircular joint induction.

## 6. Global gradient control, Huber reference, and exterior histories

For 0<R<=d_jB choose an integer q>=0 such that

    d_j*c^(-(q+1))*B<R<=d_j*c^(-q)*B.

A grid boundary is taken as the upper endpoint of an interval.
The strict lower inequality and (5.1) imply

    G(M_j(R))<=a*c^(-q)*B<beta*R/d_j.               (6.1)

For R>d_jB, take any actual start |s|<=R. A crossing history has
terminal gradient<=aB<beta*R/d_j by (5.2). In a noncrossing
history with terminal radius r and gradient p, the prefix payment
in (2.5) gives |s|>=r+S_j*p>=A_j*p. Consequently

    p<=R/A_j<(c-2)*R/d_j<beta*R/d_j.               (6.2)

Zero hits give p=0. Maximizing over actual starts establishes

    G(M_j(R))<=beta*R/d_j for every R>0.           (6.3)

Every crossing history is also pre-cap at its terminal: its gradient
is at most aB<B, whereas G(r)=B exactly for r>=u. Thus its terminal
radius is strictly below u. This justifies (2.4) whenever used below.

We now derive the full Huber reference jointly. Let J_B(r) denote
the even unit-curvature Huber objective with positive-ray derivative
min(r,B). At B=1 it is the kappa=B=1 member of this same family.
Every crossing trajectory under H_m then ends with radius<=a<1
by (5.2), so its twice-energy is its terminal squared gradient.
If its original R<=d_m, (6.1) gives score<=beta^2/d_m^2<1/D.
If R>d_m, (5.2) gives score<=a^2/R^2<a^2/d_m^2<1/D.
We used d_m^2>D from (3.5). Every noncrossing history has score<=1/D
by (2.5). The scaling J_B(x)=B^2*J_1(x/B) leaves the physical word
unchanged, proving for every B>0 and every real s that

    2J_B(Psi_m^Huber(s))<=s^2/D.                   (6.4)

This scaling concerns only the auxiliary Huber reference. It does
not replace kappa,B, or the core knot of the actual kink trajectory.

Here is the exterior comparison in the scope needed for the root
argument: assume EVERY query and the terminal of an actual kink
trajectory have radius>1. No condition on inner reversal is needed.
For any of its crossing steps, (4.4) and (2.2) bound the landing by
d*min(r,B). A noncrossing positive stay from a middle source would
land at most1-h*kappa<1 by (4.1), and so is excluded by the exterior
hypothesis. Therefore every same-side stay in this history is
saturated, including a cap tie if it occurs, and equals r-hB.
Every physical step is thus bounded by

    V_(h,B)(r)=max(d*min(r,B), r-hB), d=h-1.       (6.5)

This is a nondecreasing function of r and is exactly the Huber
symmetric-interval image radius. The latter follows by maximizing
the absolute affine core output -d*x and the tail output x-hB on
[0,r]. Induction through the original physical word bounds the
actual terminal radius by the full Huber interval-image radius
from [-R,R]. The composite Huber map is continuous, so this radius
is attained by some actual start s_* in [-R,R]. Since G<=min(r,B),
integration gives F<=J_B pointwise. Thus (6.4) gives

    2F(r_n)<=2J_B(|Psi_m^Huber(s_*)|)
       <=s_*^2/D<=R^2/D.                          (6.6)

This proof explicitly establishes comparison for exterior-only
histories even when the root could reverse core points.

## 7. All starts with kappa*S>=12

A history without strict crossing is covered by (2.5). For a crossing
history, its actual terminal is pre-cap by Section6. Apply (2.4) and
(6.3) with the TRUE full-word original radius R:

    D*2F(r_n)/R^2<=D*beta^2/(kappa*d_m^2)
       =(D/S)*(beta*S/d_m)^2/(kappa*S).

From A_m<c^m=d_m/a, beta*S/d_m<c. Therefore if kappa*S>=12,

    D*2F(r_n)/R^2
       <(201/100)*(17/7)^2/12
       =58089/58800<1.                            (7.1)

This closes every history in this parameter region, with no
restriction on the number of crossings or later core visits.

## 8. No inner reversal and reduction of the remaining band

Assume first kappa*(1+d_(m-1))<=1. Every physical step preserves the
core and contracts radii there. A noncrossing history uses (2.5).
A crossing history that never enters the core uses (6.6). If a
crossing history does enter the core, (4.7) for the ORIGINAL full
word gives R>vQ and core absorption gives 2F(r_n)<=kappa, so

    2F(r_n)/R^2<kappa/(v^2*Q^2).                   (8.1)

This also covers a core entry at the terminal, with no subsequent
contraction factor assumed.

Put d=d_(m-1) and t_c=c*(c-2). The function z/(1-z)^2 increases on
(0,1). Thus kappa<=1/(1+d) gives kappa/v^2<=(1+d)/d^2. Combining
D<2c^m, a=beta/c, d=a*c^(m-1), and (c-1)^2=t_c+1 yields

    D*kappa/(v^2*Q^2)
       < [2*t_c^2/(beta*(t_c+1))]*(1+1/d).        (8.2)

For example the substitution 2c^m/d=2c/a=2c^2/beta
directly gives this coefficient. The identity lambda^2=2lambda+1
also gives

    t_c=1+2*(lambda+1)*epsilon+lambda^2*epsilon^2
       <1+8epsilon.

Indeed lambda<5/2 and epsilon<1/1000 make the coefficient of
epsilon less than7+(25/4)/1000<8. Since t_c>1 and d>1/epsilon,
the right side of (8.2) is less than

    (1+8epsilon)^2*(1+epsilon)/(1-epsilon).

The exact difference
(1-epsilon)*(1+19epsilon)-(1+8epsilon)^2
=2epsilon-83epsilon^2 is positive. Hence the last expression is
less than (1+19epsilon)*(1+epsilon)<=1+21epsilon.
Equations(8.1)-(8.2) prove

    score<=(1+21epsilon)/D                         (8.3)

throughout the no-inner-reversal region. Since
21/(4m)<=21/6144<1/16, its coefficient is less than17/16.

It remains to consider parameters with kappa*S<12 and
kappa*(1+d_(m-1))>1. For d=d_(m-1), (3.3) gives

    kappa*S>S/(1+d)>
       [c*d/(c-2)-1]/(1+d)>5.                    (8.4)

The last comparison follows from c/(c-2)>17/3 and d>9:
(c/(c-2)-5)*d>(2/3)*9=6.

Moreover (c-2)/c^2<3/40 on the parameter interval. To see this,
3c^2-40c+80 decreases there and at c=17/7 equals27/49>0.
The inequality d_m<(c-2)*(S+1) from (3.3) thus implies

    kappa*(1+d_(m-2))
       <12*[1/S+(3/40)*(1+1/S)]
       =9/10+129/(10S)<1.                         (8.5)

We used S>1000. Therefore the first inner-reversing level is exactly

    J=m-1>=1,
    kappa*(1+d_(J-1))<1<kappa*(1+d_J).            (8.6)

The original left and right H_J each have only core-preserving
physical steps. The sole physical step capable of an inner sign
reversal is the original root h_J^lev. Exterior crossings in
either child remain allowed.

Two convenient additional band bounds are

    v>49/50, D*kappa<603/25<25.                   (8.7)

Indeed kappa<12/S<12/1000=3/250<1/50, while
D*kappa=(D/S)*(kappa*S)<(201/100)*12=603/25.
These bounds use the actual S of the same full word.


## 9. New landing price measured from the ORIGINAL start

Consider the FIRST strict sign crossing of the full H_m trajectory,
assume its source is exterior, and write r for that source radius,
p=G(r), k for the physical level, P for its ORIGINAL full-word prefix
mass, and z for its positive landing radius. Equations(4.6) and (4.1)
give

    R>=r+P*p>=v+(1+P)*p>=v+A_k*p,
    z=(1+d_k)*p-r<=d_k*p-v.                       (9.1)

Every premise in this calculation is tied to the actual trajectory:
before its first strict crossing there was no zero hit and the radii
decreased; all previous queried gradients were at least p; the full
immediate left H_k occurred within the original prefix; and exterior
sources satisfy r>=v+p, including the cap knot and every tail point.
At r=u the equality r=v+p holds. There is no loss of a cap area or
replacement of the original radius by a child's radius.

Since A_k>d_k/(c-2), (9.1) implies R-v>=A_k*p>0 and

    z<=d_k*p-v
       <=[d_k/A_k]*(R-v)-v
       <(c-2)*(R-v)-v=:L_land(R).                 (9.2)

Strictness comes from the mass inequality A_k>d_k/(c-2), even if
the source is at a knot and all preceding gradients equal p.
The positivity z>0 also forces L_land(R)>0 whenever this case occurs.

Define the critical ORIGINAL radius

    Rcrit=v+(1+v)/(c-2).                           (9.3)

It satisfies L_land(Rcrit)=1. If this first crossing lands outside
the core, z>1 forces R>Rcrit. At a landing z=1, strictness in (9.2)
also forces R>Rcrit, though equality is classified as core below.

In the residual band (8.7), c-2<3/7 yields

    Rcrit>v+(7/3)*(1+v)
       =(10/3)*v+7/3>28/5.                       (9.4)

The final strict inequality uses v>49/50:
(10/3)*(49/50)+7/3=28/5. Consequently

    D*kappa/Rcrit^2
       <(603/25)/(28/5)^2
       =603/784<1.                              (9.5)

These are symbolic rational comparisons, not executed numerical
estimates or recurrence values.

There is a useful paid radius consequence. If R<=Rcrit and all steps
AFTER this first crossing preserve the core, then (9.2) gives z<1,
and the terminal radius is at most z<L_land(R). Moreover

    L_land(R)/R=(c-2)-(c-1)*v/R

increases with R>0, because v>0 in this band. Its value at Rcrit is
1/Rcrit. Therefore in this case

    r_n/R<=1/Rcrit.                               (9.6)

If R>Rcrit and a later absorbing core entry occurs, then r_n<=1,
so r_n/R<1/Rcrit as well. The phrase "later absorbing" is essential:
the root could reverse a core state; Section11 checks its location
in every case before applying this consequence.

## 10. Positive-J core-output gate at the original root

In the residual band set J=m-1, d=d_J, L=L_J=S_J/d_J, and

    t=kappa*d, q=kappa*(1+d)-1=t-v>0.
    K(z)=z for 0<=z<=1, K(z)=exp(z-1) for z>=1,
    Z=K(q)*exp(-L*t).

Here K is only a scalar comparison function, not a new objective or
gradient. Since level J-1 is nonreversing in the core,

    v<t<=c*v, q<(3/2)*v, q<(3/5)*t, L>7/5.        (10.1)

The upper bound on t follows by multiplying
kappa*d_(J-1)<=v by c. Then q/t=1-v/t<=1-1/c<3/5,
and q<= (c-1)*v<3v/2. These inequalities use the actual positive J,
which exists by (8.6), and no smaller child receives a new enclosing m.

Let y=|Psi_J(s)| be the actual terminal radius of the ORIGINAL left
child. Assume 0<y<=1; if y=0 the remainder stays zero. The root
gradient is exactly kappa*y, and its strict reversing landing is

    z=q*y<=q.

This landing is allowed to exceed1. The complete right H_J has
core-preserving physical steps. A strict crossing in that child
would require its own starting radius z>vQ by (4.7), whereas
z<=q<3v/2<vQ. Thus the complete right child has no strict crossing.
If its terminal were still exterior, all its queries would have
radii>1 and gradient at least kappa, forcing its terminal radius
to be at most z-kappa*S_J<=q-L*t<0. This is impossible. Hence it
returns to the core by its actual terminal, allowing entry on its
last step.

For a quantitative return bound, for each actual right-child step
define the auxiliary map

    C_h(r)=r-h*kappa*min(r,1).

Because 0<=h*kappa<=1 in H_J, this map is nonnegative and
nondecreasing on [0,infinity). Along the actual noncrossing child,
(2.2) gives r_next=r-h*G(r)<=C_h(r). Monotonicity thus bounds the
actual output by the chronological composition of the C_h maps
from the actual z. This compares trajectories without changing
the original function or cap.

For r>0 put b(r)=log r when r<=1 and b(r)=r-1 when r>=1.
For 0<=alpha<=1 and a positive output,

    b(r-alpha*min(r,1))<=b(r)-alpha.              (10.2)

Inside the core this is log(1-alpha)<=-alpha. Above the core with
an exterior output it is equality. For a transition from above1
to w=r-alpha<=1, it follows from log w<=w-1. A zero comparison
output and its actual bounded output stay zero.

If z>0, one has b(z)<L*t: for z<=1, b(z)<=0, while for z>1,
b(z)=z-1<q<L*t. Summing (10.2) over EVERY right-child physical
step shows that its final comparison radius is below1 and at most
K(z)*exp(-L*t). Also for 0<=y<=1,

    K(q*y)<=y*K(q).                                (10.3)

For q<=1 there is equality. If q>1 and qy<=1, use q<=exp(q-1).
If qy>=1, the function exp(qy-1)/y is increasing with y on
[1/q,1], since its derivative has the sign of qy-1; its maximum
is exp(q-1). These are all cases. Therefore the TRUE terminal
radius of H_m is bounded by

    r_n<=y*Z.                                     (10.4)

The entire right child and the actual terminal have been retained,
even when the root landing was exterior.

We now show Z<1/4 uniformly. Write e=exp(1)>2.
If q<=1, (10.1) gives L*t>7q/3, so

    Z<q*exp(-7q/3)<=3/(7e)<1/4.

The maximum follows by differentiating w*exp(-7w/3) for w>=0;
the last comparison uses e>2 and 3/14<1/4.
If q>1, then

    Z=exp(q-1-L*t)<exp(-1-4q/3)
       <exp(-7/3)<1/4,

since e>2 gives exp(7/3)>e^2>4.

Only the original-radius payment remains. If the original left
H_J had no strict crossing, its radii decreased to y, and
G(y)=kappa*y. Thus

    R>=y+S_J*kappa*y=(1+L*t)*y.

In this band L*t>(7/5)*(49/50)>1, so y/R<1/2.
Using (8.7), (10.4), and the exact core energy gives

    D*2F(r_n)/R^2
       =D*kappa*r_n^2/R^2<25/(16*4)=25/64<1/2.  (10.5)

If the original left child DID strictly cross, its absorbing core
forces its first crossing to be exterior. Equation(4.7), measured
from the ORIGINAL R, gives

    R>vQ>(49/50)*(10/3)=49/15>3.

Since y<=1, now y/R<1/3, and

    D*2F(r_n)/R^2<25/(16*9)=25/144<1/2.          (10.6)

Thus the entire y<=1 branch has score below1/(2D). This rederives
the precise positive-J core-output gate needed here. There are
no later ancestors after the original root; (10.4) is already the
actual original terminal, not an intermediate return surrogate.

## 11. Exhaustive closure when the root source is exterior

Remain in the band (8.4)-(8.7), and now assume

    y=|Psi_(m-1)(s)|>1.

A zero hit anywhere would force y=0 if before the root, or terminal
zero if later, so zero histories have already been settled.
Histories with no strict crossing anywhere have score<=1/D by
(2.5). In the rest of this section a strict crossing occurs.

The original left child cannot ever have entered the closed core,
because all its physical steps preserve it and its terminal y
is exterior. Hence every point up through the root source is
exterior.

Case A: a strict crossing occurred in the original left child.
Its FIRST crossing, which is also the full trajectory's first,
has exterior source and exterior landing z>1. Section9 therefore
gives R>Rcrit. If the root output or any later point enters the
closed core, all remaining steps lie in the right H_(m-1) and
preserve that core. Thus r_n<=1, and

    D*2F(r_n)/R^2<=D*kappa/R^2
       <D*kappa/Rcrit^2<603/784<1.               (11.1)

If neither the root output nor any later point enters the core,
the entire original history, including all queries and its
terminal, stays exterior. Apply the unrestricted exterior
comparison (6.6) to get score<=1/D. This includes every repeated
exterior crossing.

Case B: the left child had no strict crossing. The first crossing
occurs at the root or somewhere in the right child. Its source
is exterior. At the root this follows from y>1. In the right
child, any previous core entry (at the root output or earlier
in that child) would be absorbing and would forbid this strict
crossing. This proves the exterior-source premise without
assuming that a generic root step preserves core points.

Every step AFTER this first crossing belongs to the right child
and is core preserving. If R<=Rcrit, (9.2) gives its landing
z<L_land(R)<=1, and (9.6) gives r_n/R<=1/Rcrit.
The true terminal lies in the core, so

    D*2F(r_n)/R^2<=D*kappa/Rcrit^2<603/784<1.      (11.2)

The endpoint R=Rcrit is included: the landing is strictly below1.

If R>Rcrit and some point from this first crossing onward enters
the closed core, it stays there and r_n<=1. Equation(11.1) again
applies. If there is no such entry, then the entire trajectory
is exterior: before the first crossing it could not have
entered an absorbing right-child core, and the original left
child was already known exterior. The exterior comparison
(6.6) yields score<=1/D.

These cases exhaust y>1. In particular a saturated same-side
root step which enters the core and has no earlier crossing
cannot have a later crossing; it belongs to the direct ray
case(2.5). A same-side entry on the last physical step belongs
to the crossing-and-core case if there was an earlier crossing,
and otherwise to (2.5). No post-entry factor is inserted when
the entry is terminal.

All boundaries are included. Core means radius<=1, so the tie1
is absorbed in a core-preserving child. The cap knot u has the
same value, gradient, update, and area on both adjacent formulas.
When B=kappa, u=1 and the empty middle causes no missing branch.
A zero landing stays zero. The strict mass margin in (9.2)
survives source and cap ties.

## 12. Whole-family assembly and exact limitations

Every allowed parameter choice belongs to the no-inner-reversal
region of Section8, the curvature-mass region of Section7, or the
residual root-only band. In that band the actual left-child
terminal is either y<=1, covered by Section10, or y>1, covered
exhaustively by Section11. Thus every actual start has the bound
(1.1). This proves the stated whole-family theorem as a JOINT
UNVERIFIED candidate.

The proof controls the actual final objective, with all core,
middle, and tail areas retained in (2.1), and with the original
denominator s^2. It uses no amplitude substitution for B, no
reset enclosing m, no omitted physical suffix, and no full-depth
claim inferred merely from a short-child numerical test.

The new root landing-price argument survived this textual case
and boundary screen. No exact legal failure of its landing
inequality or exhaustive partition was identified, so the
conditional second mechanism was not opened. The stronger
3/2 terminal-gradient assertion remains undecided, and its
earlier failed uniformly widened-child induction is not used.

This candidate is a mathematical upper bound for the specified
fixed odd family only. It does not bound the complete
finite-dimensional smooth-convex supremum W_n(H_m). Consequently
it does not discharge the FULL-function-class hypothesis of

# Independent audit of the frozen actual F/M/E construction

Project gd-exact-profile-20260926; worker max3; invocation69 after the
assigned ended68 baseline, ended ceiling69 / attempted ceiling70,
controller cap1. Dispatch: master_guidance98411955389a4a0f.
Author of the candidate: max4 invocation72. Auditor: max3.

AUDIT VERDICT: no_gap_found_at_declared_scope.

This is an independent analytical audit of precisely the frozen complete
22933-byte candidate, SHA256
e14a2f326de09af437f7fad53f4ed8b25e531e45cba44aa999f52cc8d3fe0e33,
shared proof_attempt7d434affa1888c02 and handoffd27db48f3bd3df6c.
The verdict covers the THREE exact BASIC-lambda F/M/E physical words,
EVERY integer m>=16, their actual different horizons, one fixed static
function per run, the two claimed terminal comparisons, and arbitrary
advertised L>0. It does not address arbitrary schedules.

All mathematical derivations below are joint UNVERIFIED audit evidence.
An independent audit is not verifier acceptance, a new fact, or a solution
of the original unrestricted exact-profile problem. No fact_submit,
numerical trajectory, recurrence evaluation, optimizer, DP, installation,
consult, subagent, fourth law, new search program or second audit occurred.

## 1. Input identity, prior state, and permitted correctness premises

The assigned worker-relative input file was absent: the initial cat and
sha256sum calls returned "No such file or directory." I recovered the
complete evidence of7d434affa1888c02 through shared memory into the OWN
audit directory. The first save added one trailing newline, producing
22934 bytes and SHA256
7bb70bca651488705580fedf42b25a9fef67272758d0d8988f9540cc0c5053af.
That recovery was preserved as recovery_extra_newline.md. The exact
22933-byte recovery, input_candidate.md, then matched the assigned SHA
above. This is an input-recovery artifact issue, not a mathematical gap.
Neither the author's artifact nor any original shared record was edited.

The exact source record1ef7c860ceeda980 was recovered from the shared
proof_attempt store into input_word_source_1ef.md. Its physical definitions
in Section1 agree with the frozen candidate. Its complete evidence SHA is
0d544c87038c5b0aba07f33c2b8daa8d7cdd1748df18f1a11d77363122408cc6.
This record and the old formal-array failures are UNVERIFIED awareness.
No earlier restricted-channel bound, 3072 table numeral, upper benchmark
comparison, full-objective array law, or counterexample is used as a premise.

The only accepted scopes invoked here are:

* fact_id1252b8823bb5a290: statement normalization and original signed
  interpolation residual; proof paragraph1's necessity. No terminal-edge
  deletion, dual existence, or formal-array implication is needed.
* fact_id04219554e41d9750: original ALL-INTEGER-SPLIT scalar definitions,
  unconditional monotonicity in proof Section3, and unconditional doubling
  in proof Section7, equation(7.1). No improving-seed assumption, finite
  table, enclosure constant, or substituted-word theorem is used.

These exact scopes were read in the fact files. Their read-time full-file
SHA256 values were respectively
923d109bea16c9a7a75750972c4ba852dcf73700ade9c434e4e29f5776439b73 and
6bcf9fd34f91050423454c20e29458fbd2f5b1f336295e2501e2ad9bb950ba89.
The construction and every new formula below are derived inside this
joint audit; they are not downstream uses of an unverified finding.

Skills: query-memory to recall the assignment, shared state, old failure
scopes and accepted inputs; construct-counterexamples to attack the fixed
candidate's geometry, boundary queries and offsets; search-math-results
for projection/conjugacy background. One Matlas query returned three
inapplicable statements: a one-homogeneous min-max theorem, a constrained
projected-gradient bound, and a general conjugate duality theorem. None
was used. Web fallback recovered the primary Taylor--Hendrickx--Glineur
paper, arXiv1502.05666 (https://arxiv.org/abs/1502.05666). The exact OWN already downloaded text
downloads/taylor-hendrickx-glineur-1502.05666.txt was read at Section2.4,
Lemma2 including both proof directions, and Remark2. Its smooth class
F_(0,L) and the conjugate nonsmooth strongly convex class F_(1/L,infinity)
are distinct. No external theorem is a correctness premise here; the
projection and all-pair arguments below are direct.

## 2. Original words, every count and mass, and the m16 guard

Use the ordinary real Euclidean plane with orthonormal basis
e1=(1,0), e2=(0,1). Put lambda=1+sqrt(2), m an integer at least16,
and A=lambda^m. The original BASIC lists are

    q_0=(),
    q_(j+1)=(q_j,1+(lambda-2)lambda^j,q_j),
    zeta=(1+sqrt(1+8A))/2,
    e=A/zeta=(zeta-1)/2,
    p_m=(q_m,1+e).

There is no enclosing-m tau=1/4 recursion here. The empty-list count
and sum are zero. If q_j has count2^j-1 and sumlambda^j-1, its parent
has count2(2^j-1)+1=2^(j+1)-1 and sum

    2(lambda^j-1)+1+(lambda-2)lambda^j
       =lambda^(j+1)-1.

All entries are strictly positive since lambda>2. Appending the physical
cap1+e therefore makes p_m have count2^m and sumA+e. From the defining
radical, zeta^2-zeta=2A. Dividing by zeta gives A/zeta=(zeta-1)/2, and
substituting zeta=2e+1 gives 2e^2+e=A. Consequently e>0 and e<sqrt(A).

Fix a=7/10, b=6/5, k=3/5, tau=k, and the literal bridges

    b1=1+aA, b2=1+bA.

The exact source words, with no suppressed terminal step, are

    H_F=(p_m,b1,q_m,k,b2,q_m,1),
    H_M=(q_m,b1,p_m,k,b2,q_m,1),
    H_E=(q_m,b1,q_m,k,b2,p_m).

Write them as (L0,b1,L1,k,b2,L2). The complete lists are

| X | L0 | S0 | L1 | S1 | L2 | S2 |
|---|---|---:|---|---:|---|---:|
| F | p_m | A+e | q_m | A-1 | (q_m,1) | A |
| M | q_m | A-1 | p_m | A+e | (q_m,1) | A |
| E | q_m | A-1 | q_m | A-1 | p_m | A+e |

Here Sr denotes the sum of Lr. Their counts give, literally,

    N_F=N_M=2^m+3+(2^m-1)+2^m=3*2^m+2,
    N_E=(2^m-1)+3+(2^m-1)+2^m=3*2^m+1.

For completeness, the original module sums in each displayed H_X before
its external unit are3A+e-2. Adding the bridges and contraction gives
total mass49A/10+e+8/5 for F/M, and49A/10+e+3/5 for E.
Thus1+2sum H_F=1+2sum H_M=49A/5+2e+21/5, whereas
1+2sum H_E=49A/5+2e+11/5. These masses are not benchmark B_N.

The sole coarse parameter guard can be independently obtained as follows:

    A>2^m>=2^16=65536,
    e/A<1/sqrt(A)<1/256,
    8/(5A)<1/1000,
    (e+8/5)/A<1/256+1/1000<1/200.                (A1)

The last rational comparison is1256<1280 after multiplication by256000.
All inequalities are strict even at m16; no numerical case was run.
In particular (e+1)/A<1/200 and e/A<1/200. The argument pays a uniform
guard for every m in the declared domain and asserts no smaller threshold.

## 3. Static function, exact real metric, global regularity and minimum

Set

    t=A/(1+S0), d=t/A,
    v=(-3/8,sqrt(7)/8),
    P=d e1, Q=d v, R=-k d v,
    D=conv{0,P,Q,R},
    f(x)=max_(g in D){x.g-||g||^2/2}, x0=e1.     (A2)

All scalar products in this report use the stated ordinary Euclidean
metric. Directly, v.e1=-3/8 and ||v||^2=(9+7)/64=1/4.
Thus t_F=A/(A+e+1), while t_M=t_E=1. There are two functions:
the F scale and the shared M/E scale, one fixed function throughout each
run. No value/gradient-dependent selection is made during a run.

Q and R are opposite nonzero multiples, and 0=(3/8)Q+(5/8)R.
P and Q are independent because the determinant of e1,v is sqrt(7)/8.
Therefore D is the actual full-dimensional triangle with vertices P,Q,R.
The support function is exactly

    J(u)=max{0,P.u,Q.u,R.u},

with four zero intercepts and four weights equal to1. This printed
triangle specifies all of f; no affine-plane offset is left implicit.

Completing the square in (A2) identifies its maximizing g with the
unique Euclidean projection of x onto D. Existence follows from compactness;
strict convexity of squared distance gives uniqueness. To prove the
complete normal test rather than invoke a projection theorem, put u=x-g.
Necessity follows by comparing g with g+epsilon(z-g) and dividing the
squared-distance difference by epsilon as epsilon decreases to0.
It is exactly

    g in D, u=x-g, u.(z-g)<=0 for EVERY z in D.   (A3)

Conversely, if (A3) holds, then for every z in D,

    ||x-z||^2-||x-g||^2
      =||z-g||^2-2u.(z-g)>=0,

so g is that unique projection. Since the normal expression is affine in
z, comparing with all four printed vertices is sufficient and exhaustive.
The value is always f(x)=u.g+||g||^2/2.

For projections g,g' of x,x', the two normals imply

    (x-x').(g-g')>=||g-g'||^2.

Cauchy--Schwarz proves ||g-g'||<=||x-x'||, including the g=g' case.
Evaluating the two maxima at each other's optimizers gives

    0<=f(x+h)-f(x)-g.h<=(g'-g).h<=||h||^2,

where g' projects x+h. The remainder bound proves differentiability
with grad f(x)=g; the Lipschitz estimate proves continuous gradient and
global1-smoothness. The finite attained maximum is convex, as a maximum
of affine functions of x. On the nonempty interior of D, projection is
the identity and f(x)=||x||^2/2. Two distinct sufficiently close points
in that interior force any global gradient Lipschitz constant to be at
least1, proving sharp global constant1.

The function is nonquadratic. It has the identity gradient on that open
triangle interior. At u=e1 the four heights are d,-3d/8,9d/40,0,
so P wins strictly, and the strict comparisons persist for u in an open
neighborhood of e1. At every x=P+u in that neighborhood (A3) gives the
constant gradient P. These two gradient behaviors cannot belong to the
same global quadratic polynomial.

The full minimum set needs special care because it is not a singleton.
Since 0 belongs to D, f>=0. If x pairs nonpositively with all four
vertices, every g in D does also, and f(x)=0 by taking g=0.
If instead x.z>0 for some nonzero vertex z, then epsilon z belongs to
D for0<=epsilon<=1 and

    x.(epsilon z)-||epsilon z||^2/2
       =epsilon x.z-epsilon^2||z||^2/2>0

for a sufficiently small positive epsilon. This proves both necessity
and sufficiency, not merely that0 is one minimizer. Opposite Q/R then give

    Cmin={x:x1<=0, -3x1+sqrt(7)x2=0},
    min f=0.                                    (A4)

It is exactly the closed ray {r(sqrt(7),3):r<=0}.
For every x in this ray,

    ||e1-x||^2=(1-x1)^2+x2^2>=1.

Equality requires x1=x2=0. Hence the attained minimizer0 is the unique
closest minimizer and the TRUE initial distance to the complete minimum
set is1. The same proof holds for both t scales and every declared m;
adding the R plane changed the minimum set, and it has been recomputed.

## 4. Full initialized first sector and F's physical cap

Let ell0=length(L0). For original indices0 through ell0, including the
b1 query, let c be the sum of ALL already taken L0 steps. Because all
entries are positive, every such c is in[0,S0]. Starting from the actual
x0=e1, propose x=e1-cP and g=P. Put

    r(c)=1-(c+1)/(1+S0), u=r(c)e1,
    H_P=P.u=d r(c)>=0.

The complete support heights are

    P.u=H_P, Q.u=-3H_P/8, R.u=9H_P/40, 0.u=0.

Their differences from P's height are respectively0,
-11H_P/8,-31H_P/40,-H_P. All are nonpositive for every c in the
actual interval. Thus (A3) proves g=P and the exact value

    f(e1-cP)=d-(c+1/2)d^2.                        (A5)

For each literal next L0 entry h, the update is
e1-cP-hP=e1-(c+h)P. Its new partial mass remains in[0,S0].
Beginning at c=0, this is a complete induction over every original
query, not a reset or a module oracle.

In F, its cap query has c=A-1, and applying h=1+e makes c=A+e=S0.
Thus the cap is covered before and after its update by the same P law.
In M/E, L0 ends at c=A-1=S0. In every case the following b1 query has
r(S0)=0, x=P, and f=||P||^2/2>0. All four support heights tie at0,
but the strict-distance minimization still uniquely projects x=P to P.
It does not project to0 merely because 0 is also a support maximizer
for the zero normal. This is the only support tie on the initialized
first-sector path.

The actual long-bridge update is therefore

    x_next=P-(1+aA)P=-a t e1=:XQ.                 (A6)

The F scale is essential here: P=d e1 with d=1/(1+S0), so x=P
at b1 even when S0=A+e. No A-1 front normalization was silently used.

## 5. All middle queries, M's cap, the contraction and second bridge

The first middle index is jQ=ell0+1. Let ell1=length(L1).
For every original middle query, the contraction query jQ+ell1,
and the b2 query jQ+ell1+1, let s be the mass already taken from
(L1,k). It lies in[0,S1+k]. From the actual start (A6), set

    x=XQ-sQ, g=Q, u=XQ-(s+1)Q,
    z=(s+1)/A, T=t^2/A.

Direct Euclidean products give the full four heights

    Q.u=T(21/80-z/4),
    P.u=T(-7/10+3z/8),
    R.u=-k T(21/80-z/4),
    0.u=0.                                      (A7)

The relevant chronological ranges are explicit:

    F/E: 1/A<=z<=1+3/(5A),
    M:   1/A<=z<=1+(e+8/5)/A.

In particular0<z<201/200 by(A1). Therefore

So Q uniquely maximizes the support at EVERY original middle query.
This interval proof covers every child of q_m, every accumulated
partial mass, the objective cap in M, and both extra queries. It is not
an assertion that checking a few unspecified endpoints determines
unknown intermediate behavior: the positions have been defined at each

For each physical L1 or k step h, x-hQ=XQ-(s+h)Q. The next s remains
in the printed interval, so induction verifies all these initialized
updates. The exact middle objective is

    f(XQ-sQ)=T(21/80-z/4)+t^2/(8A^2).             (A8)

In M its cap query has s=A-1, and its h=1+e update gives s=A+e.
Both sides use Q. At the contraction query s=S1, the support heights
of the ACTIVE Q plane are

    F/E: T/(80),
    M:   T[1/80-(e+1)/(4A)].

The first expression means t^2/(80A), since T=t^2/A.
The actual k=3/5 update changes s to S1+3/5 and subtracts

At the resulting b2 query the exact active support heights are

    F/E: T[1/80-3/(20A)],
    M:   T[1/80-(e+8/5)/(4A)].

Both are strictly positive; the weaker uniform lower bound9T/800 also
applies. This checks the candidate's positive endpoint-height mechanism.

The queried gradient at b2 is still Q. Applying its actual h=1+bA gives

    XR=XQ-(S1+k+1+bA)Q.                          (A9)

This retains the contraction, both bridge offsets1, the M cap mass
where present, and every preceding original query.

## 6. Full last sector, every final unit/cap, and the actual terminal

The last sector starts at jR=ell0+ell1+3. Let w be the complete mass
already taken from L2, for every query and the unqueried terminal.
Then0<=w<=S2. From the actual XR of(A9), propose

    x=XR-wR, g=R,
    u=x-R=t[-a e1-C(w)v],
    C(w)=(S1+k+1+bA-k(w+1))/A
         =b+(S1+1)/A-kw/A.                      (A10)

The cancellation in the last equality uses precisely k=tau. It retains
the1 in b2 and the1 from subtracting the proposed final gradient.
Let C0=C(0) and C_N=C(S2). The exact boundaries are

| X | C0 | C_N |
|---|---:|---:|
| F | 11/5 | 8/5 |
| M | 11/5+(e+1)/A | 8/5+(e+1)/A |
| E | 11/5 | 8/5-3e/(5A) |

Since k>0, C is decreasing on the full w interval. From(A1), its
smallest possible boundary satisfies

    C_N,E>8/5-3/1000=1597/1000>3/2,

and its largest possible boundary satisfies

    C0,M<11/5+1/200.

Hence every physical partial mass, including the terminal, has

    3/2<C(w)<11/5+1/200.                         (A11)

This is a full physical partial-mass proof of the guards. It does not
omit any internal suffix gradient.

Directly from the actual Euclidean coordinates,

    Q.u=T(21/80-C/4)<-9T/80<0,
    R.u=T(3C/20-63/400)>27T/400>0,
    P.u=T(-7/10+3C/8),
    (R-P).u=T(217/400-9C/40)>371T/8000>0.        (A12)

For the last exact rational slack,

    217/400-(9/40)(11/5+1/200)
      =19/400-9/8000=371/8000.

Thus R wins strictly against0, Q and P at every last query and at the
actual terminal. The attained gradient is uniquely R by(A3), and

    f(XR-wR)=T(3C(w)/20-63/400)+9t^2/(200A^2).   (A13)

For each original remaining step h, subtraction gives
XR-wR-hR=XR-(w+h)R. The next w remains in[0,S2].
This induction verifies every q_m query and update in L2.

For F, the unit query has w=A-1 and C=8/5+3/(5A); its update makes
w=A and C=8/5. For M, the unit query has w=A-1 and
C=8/5+(e+8/5)/A; its update makes C=8/5+(e+1)/A.
For E, the cap query has w=A-1 and C=8/5+3/(5A); its step1+e
makes w=A+e and C=8/5-3e/(5A).
These are the actual final steps and actual terminal boundaries.

At each of these queries and AFTER the final step, the strict normals

## 7. Exhaustive original indexing and all normal boundary cases

With zero-based original indices, the sectors are exactly:

* P: indices0,...,ell0, including the b1 query;
* Q: indicesjQ,...,jQ+ell1+1, including all L1 queries, k and b2;
* R: indicesjR,...,N_X, including all L2 queries and the terminal N_X.

Their masses are respectively

within their indicated sectors. The physical counts in Section2 make

The global induction starts at x0 and passes through both literal
bridges; it proves the proposed sector formulas are the actual same-law

All ties are accounted for. P is strict at c<S0, and at c=S0 the
normal is0 while projection remains uniquely P. Q and R have strict

the beginnings, cap/unit queries, bridge/contraction queries and
terminals. The chosen optimum0 also has zero normal. Other minima

## 8. Every ordered remote/optimum/terminal row, with an explicit table

For each actual finite index i, including N_X, put
u_i=x_i-g_i and f_i=u_i.g_i+||g_i||^2/2.
For any minimizer x_star in(A4), put
g_star=0, f_star=0, u_star=x_star.
All these g are in D, and every normal satisfies(A3).

Use the EXACT residual orientation of accepted1252b8823bb5a290:

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+g_i.(x_j-x_i)+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.

Substituting x_i=u_i+g_i and both value formulas cancels every norm,
u_i term and cross-gradient term, leaving

    Q_ij=u_j.(g_i-g_j).                          (A14)

The comparison vector g_i is in D; the normal at j gives Q_ij<=0.
Changing i and j gives the other orientation, with the normal at i.
This proves every original ordered pair, not an adjacency subset.

For added explicitness, each finite ordered residual appears in the
following nine-case table. Its row names the sector of i; its column
names the sector of j. H_P, z and C use the actual partial mass at j.

| i sector / j sector | P at j | Q at j | R at j |
|---|---|---|---|
| P | 0 | T[-77/80+5z/8] | -T[217/400-9C/40] |
| Q | -11H_P/8 | 0 | (8T/5)[21/80-C/4] |
| R | -31H_P/40 | -(8T/5)[21/80-z/4] | 0 |

z<201/200. Its last entry is less than -9T/500<0.
The last-column first entry is less than -371T/8000<0, and its
middle entry is less than -9T/50<0. First-column entries may equal0
at the b1 query; their nonpositivity was already proved for all c.
Same-sector ordered pairs are exactly0 even if their positions differ.
Thus the table explicitly covers every old/future remote pair and
BOTH directions of every cross-sector pair.

The terminal is an actual R jet at its derived C_N, so its incoming
column and outgoing row are already included in this table. No reduced
terminal interpolation argument is being substituted for its actual
gradient. For an arbitrary minimum,

    Q_(i,star)=x_star.g_i<=0,
    Q_(star,i)=-u_i.g_i<=0.

The first is the minimum normal applied to g_i. For P it can be
strictly negative at a nonzero minimum; for Q and R it is0.
At the selected minimum0 every Q_(i,star)=0. The second is
minus the active support height: nonpositive for P, strictly negative
for Q/R. This proves both orientations of ALL optimum and terminal
rows, including the ties.

No module, restart, plane offset, coordinate quotient or missing jet
is hidden in(A14).

## 9. Actual Euclidean Gram, with no surrogate metric

The entire formal-vector list (x0,g0,...,g_(N_X)) is represented by
actual vectors in R2. Every gradient is one of P,Q,R; the unqueried
gradient is R. Its complete entries are obtained by repeating the
following exact4-by4 table for the sector labels:

    Gram(e1,P,Q,R)=
    [ 1       d          -3d/8       9d/40    ]
    [ d       d^2        -3d^2/8     9d^2/40  ]
    [-3d/8   -3d^2/8      d^2/4    -3d^2/20  ]
    [ 9d/40   9d^2/40    -3d^2/20   9d^2/100 ].

For any real coefficients on the full original list, let alpha0 be
the x0 coefficient and alphaP,alphaQ,alphaR be the total coefficients
on the three respective gradient sectors. Its full quadratic is

    [alpha0+d alphaP-(3d/8)(alphaQ-k alphaR)]^2
       +(7d^2/64)(alphaQ-k alphaR)^2>=0.

This is an explicit actual Gram factorization and establishes PSD on
all original coordinates. Singular rank2 is allowed. It does not assert
that a failed full dual array becomes PSD. The old rank-one formal
negative modes from round68 were not actual interpolable transcripts
and are not counterexamples to the static function audited here.

## 10. Independent reconstruction of terminal equations(17)--(20)

The position from(A9)--(A10), at w=S2, is exactly

    x_(N_X)=t[-a e1-(C_N+k/A)v],
    g_(N_X)=R=-kt v/A,
    u_(N_X)=t[-a e1-C_N v].                       (A15)

The k/A term comes from adding the final gradient back to u; dropping
it would lose the squared-gradient correction. With min f=0 and true
closest radius1, the project's W=2R payoff is

    V_X=2 f(x_(N_X))
       =(t^2/A)(3C_N/10-63/200)+9t^2/(100A^2).   (A16)

The leading coefficient at C_N=8/5 is
(3/10)(8/5)-63/200=33/200. The three exact substitutions give

    V_F=[A/(A+e+1)]^2[33/(200A)+9/(100A^2)],
    V_M=33/(200A)+(3e/10+39/100)/A^2,
    V_E=33/(200A)-9e/(50A^2)+9/(100A^2).         (A17)

For M the positive constant39/100 is3/10+9/100; both the1
in the middle excess and the squared-gradient term have been retained.
For E the negative coefficient is(3/10)(-3e/(5A))=-9e/(50A).
For F only its actual t scale changes the common leading expression.
These independently agree with the candidate's equations(17)--(18).

The exact uniform strict bounds, including m16, are:

    A V_M>33/200>3/20;
    A V_E>33/200-9/10000=1641/10000>3/20.

For F, (e+1)/A<1/200 implies t>200/201. Squaring positive
quantities gives

    t^2>(200/201)^2>10/11,

because11*40000=440000>404010=10*40401. Hence

    A V_F>(33/200)(10/11)=3/20.

Finally3/20>10/97 because3*97=291>200=10*20.
Thus EVERY declared m and EACH exact word satisfies

    V_X>3/(20A)>10/(97A).                        (A18)

As m increases, A tends to infinity, e/A<1/sqrt(A) tends to0,
and t_F=1/[1+(e+1)/A] tends to1. Every correction in(A17),
after multiplication by A, tends to0. Therefore each of the three
limits is

    lim_(m->infinity) A V_X=33/200.              (A19)

This matches the candidate's equations(19)--(20). No computed
trajectory or asymptotic surrogate was used to decide the finite
strict inequalities.

## 11. Original all-integer benchmark: independent lower chain

The benchmark is the ORIGINAL one, with every integer split retained:

    C_0=B_0=1,
    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,
    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2,
    C_n=max_(0<=j<n)Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=j<n)G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)),
    U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.

Accepted042's proof Section3 gives monotonicity. The simple direct
lower proof also shows B_(n+1)>=G(C_0,B_n)>B_n: the admissible
split j=0 uses C_0=1 and sqrt(B_n^2+8B_n)>B_n.
Its unconditional equation(7.1) gives B_(2n+1)>=lambda B_n,
for every integer n>=0.

For scope clarity, that doubling involves no seed or finite table.
The primitive recurrence has C_(2r+1)>=Omega(C_r,C_r)=lambda C_r.
For the dominant base, B1=G(1,1)=4>=lambda B0.
For a maximizing split j+l=n-1, the split
(2j+1)+(2l+1)=2n in B_(2n+1) is an original admissible split;
using the primitive lower bound and the already inductive dominant
bound on l<n, homogeneity gives lambda G(C_j,B_l)=lambda B_n.
This is precisely the unconditional proof of accepted042's(7.1).

At the actual small base,

    B1=4,
    B2>=G(C0,B1)=G(1,4)=4+2sqrt(3)>7,

where sqrt(3)>3/2 follows from3>9/4. The inequality for B2
uses an admissible original split, not a claimed optimizer identity.

Define n_j=3*2^j-1 for integers j>=0. It has n_0=2 and
n_(j+1)=2n_j+1. Iterating the accepted doubling bound yields

    B_(3*2^m-1)>=lambda^m B2>7A.

The true offsets are

    N_F=N_M=(3*2^m-1)+3,
    N_E=(3*2^m-1)+2.

Monotonicity therefore gives B_(N_X)>7A at EVERY one of the
different actual horizons. Combining with(A18) gives exactly

    B_(N_X) V_X>7A*3/(20A)=21/20.               (A20)

The static instance is admissible in the definition of W, so

    W_(N_X)(H_X)>=V_X>(21/20)U_(N_X)^ZJ.

This lower comparison is correctly oriented: it needs a LOWER bound
for B_N, never the earlier restricted report's upper bound.
Neither the artifact-authenticated3072 table nor its checker was
used, read for a needed numeral, or executed. No conditional four-step
seed hypothesis, decimal comparison, recurrence run or surrogate
benchmark enters(A20).

Within the joint unverified candidate, the proposed10/(97A) level
fails by(A18), and benchmark-improving behavior of THESE three families
fails separately by(A20). The separate derivations prevent one
exclusion from being inferred from the other.

## 12. Arbitrary advertised L and exact normalization

For any L>0, use f_L(x)=L f(x) and each literal physical step h_i/L.
Then

    grad f_L(x)=L grad f(x),
    x_next=x-(h_i/L)grad f_L(x)=x-h_i grad f(x).

Thus the entire initialized transcript of(A5)--(A15) is unchanged,
not just the terminal. The minimum set and closest radius remain(A4).
The least global gradient Lipschitz constant is sharply L, because
the normalized constant was sharply1. Values are multiplied by L.
Consequently

    2[f_L(x_N)-min f_L]/[L dist(x0,Cmin)^2]
       =2L f(x_N)/(L*1)=V_X.

This is W=2R, not the scalar R payoff without the factor2.
For every original ordered pair, the general-L signed residual is

    L f_i-L f_j+(L g_i).(x_j-x_i)
       +||L g_i-L g_j||^2/(2L)=L Q_ij<=0.

Every optimum and terminal row scales in the same way. There is no
new metric, hidden radius factor, positive-L exception, or claimed
L=0 limit.
