# Rebalanced unsaturated columns: two exact original-row obstructions

Status: JOINT UNVERIFIED analytical draft. Worker max4, invocation93,
guidance aef867c64b61120c, plan e41a10a18e2a3a3c. No formal submission is
authorized in this round. None of the new results below is a fact-graph fact.
The original exact-profile problem remains OPEN; W=2R throughout.

## 1. Outcome and exact scope

The assigned redistribution genuinely repairs the earlier source defect.
Its corrected unit-quadratic payment is positive, every linear skew
compatibility holds at theta=0,-2, and its source capacity is greater than
q0/5 at either of those values. This is only a necessary screen.

The entire first family is nevertheless excluded by the original middle
query together with ALL earlier queried destinations. Let

 k=2^(m-1)-1, H=sum_(j<k) h_j, z=1_[0,k), R=h_k.

Here z is a column of zeros and ones, not the initial point. The exact cut is

 C_root = H sum_(j<k) mu_j - z^T P z + a(mu_k-P_kk)
        = a(R-1) sum_(j>k) lambda_(k,j)
          +sum_(i>k,j<k) lambda_(i,j)(H-t_j)
          +sum_(j<k) lambda_(k,j)(H-t_j-a) >=0.             (1.1)

For the assigned u_hat its left side is strictly less than -S/640 for
EVERY m>=1536, EVERY real theta, EVERY xi>=0, and EVERY completing skew.
The source detail and the full last-three metric vanish on these test
vectors by exact support and zero-sum identities; their weights are not
assumed small or discarded. This is an original nonnegative-multiplier cut.

After that decisive failure, the ONE additional redistribution is frozen as

 u_new=b e_0+(D-b)e_l.                                   (1.2)

It transfers every interior coefficient of u_hat to the actual last query,
preserves u_0=b and sum u=D, and repairs the middle-query cut (1.1).
Its independently recomputed payment is also positive. But it fails the
original last-row clock cut

 C_last=2(Pe)_l-2(1-pi)-(T-gbar)mu_l-gbar P_ll
       =sum_(j<l)lambda_(l,j)(T-t_j-gbar) >=0,              (1.3)

where gbar=1+beta is the actual penultimate physical step. For (1.2),
C_last<-S/4 for every real theta and every xi>=0. Increasing the included
tail metric decreases this cut. Neither failure depends on selecting a bad
skew completion. At theta=-2 the second law additionally has mu_1<0.

These are exclusions of TWO specified common-column families. They do not
exclude other redistributions, other PSD details, all unsaturated pi, or all
original certificates. No full W bound, counterexample to the benchmark,
or solution of the original problem is claimed.

## 2. Fixed physical parameters and elementary same-m estimates

Fix ONE enclosing integer m>=1536. In this entire report put

 epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,
 c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+epsilon), a=beta/c, b=1+a,
 H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j), h=H_m,
 n=2^m-1, l=n-1, S=sum_i h_i, D=1+2S,
 T=S-a, Ctot=S+b, Ctot+T=D,
 pi=b/Ctot, alpha=(1-pi)e_l, q0=1/D,
 delta=q0-pi^2, q=17/(16D), kappa=q-q0=1/(16D),
 s=beta^n c^(-m), gbar=1+beta.

All children retain these same parameters. The original terminal is n;
l is the last queried gradient and is never substituted for n.

We will use explicit uniform estimates, proved here. Since sqrt(2)<10/7,
epsilon<1/100 and epsilon<1/69,

 2<c<5/2, 2/5<a<1/2, 7/5<b<3/2, 3/4<beta<1,
 7/4<gbar<2.                                             (2.1)

For the lower a bound, (1-epsilon)/(1+epsilon)>34/35 and
(7/17)(34/35)=2/5. For the upper c bound,
(17/7)(101/100)=1717/700<5/2. Every physical step exceeds1,
so S>2^m-1>4096.

Set vword=a/(c-2). Because c(c-2)>1 and beta<1, one has
0<vword<1. Substitution in the defining sum recursion proves, for every
child j, the finite identity

 1+sum H_j=(1-vword)2^j+vword c^j.

Indeed both sides equal1 at j=0, and each satisfies
A_(j+1)=2A_j+a c^j. Thus S<c^m. Also

 T>S/2, Ctot/T=1+(1+2a)/T<17/16,
 pi<3/(2S)<1/32,
 delta=S(S-2ab)/(D Ctot^2)>0.                             (2.2)

We need a quantitative bound on the tiny full product. The inequality
2^m>=m^2 holds at m=4 and propagates because
2m^2>(m+1)^2 for m>=4. Consequently n>=32m at our threshold.
Since (1-epsilon)^(-1)>1+epsilon and the binomial expansion gives
(1+epsilon)^(4m)>1+4m epsilon=2,

 beta^n<1/256, s S<1/256, D s^2<3/65536<1/64.             (2.3)

The last inequality uses D<3S<3c^m. These are symbolic inequalities;
no parameter, recurrence, product, or trajectory was evaluated by a program.

Two scalar abbreviations used only in the first-family calculations are

 K0=(S-ab)/(Ctot T), J0=pi^2+delta D^2/T^2.

Direct expansion over the denominator Ctot^2 T^2 gives

 J0=2K0-a^2/T^2,
 1/(2S)<K0<2/S, J0>1/(2S).                               (2.4)

For the lower K0 bound,
2S(S-ab)-Ctot T=S^2-(1+2ab)S+ab>0 since ab<3/4 and S>64.
The upper bound is K0<1/T<2/S. Therefore
J0>1/S-1/S^2>1/(2S), proving the last assertion.

## 3. Original polynomial, flow, and complete coefficient coordinates

Normalize smoothness to1 and the optimum to x_star=g_star=f_star=0.
The formal vectors x0,g_0,...,g_l,g_n remain independent. Write

 x_i=x0-sum_(j<i)h_j g_j,
 Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.

The signed original interpolation rows satisfy Q_ij<=0 on every legal
trajectory. Accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 supplies this orientation and
the exact terminal-reduction scope. Selecting incoming-terminal rows zero
does NOT delete any original ordered queried-prefix row. The selected
terminal multipliers are

 lambda_(n,star)=2pi, lambda_(n,i)=2alpha_i,
 lambda_(i,n)=lambda_(star,n)=0.

Initially keep both optimum directions, mu_i=lambda_(i,star) and
nu_i=lambda_(star,i). Let e be the n-dimensional all-ones column and e_i
its coordinate columns. Let Ltri have diagonal1, entry h_j in row i,column
j for j<i, and zero above its diagonal. Set

 v=(h_0,...,h_(l-1),1)^T=Ltri^T e_l,
 t=Ltri e, t_i=1+sum_(j<i)h_j, t_l=T,
 w=Ltri^(-1)e, y_i=x_i-g_i, zeta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2.

Expanding the original rows gives
Q_ij=zeta_i-zeta_j+<g_i,y_j-y_i>,
Q_(i,star)=zeta_i-<g_i,y_i>, Q_(star,i)=-zeta_i.
Define the full n-by-n matrix N by

 N_ij=-lambda_(i,j) for i!=j,
 N_ii=mu_i+sum_(j!=i)lambda_(i,j).

Cancellation of every queried value is exactly

 Ne=mu, N^T e=2alpha+nu.                                  (3.1)

Indeed the queried value coefficient is prefix outflow minus prefix
inflow minus 2alpha_i. This fixes the orientation of the column equation.
Let

 r=h-Ltri^T alpha=pi v+a e_l,
 r_i=pi h_i (i<l), r_l=a+pi,
 V=e^T r=a+pi T=Dpi-1.

The identity x_n-sum alpha_i y_i=pi x0-r^T g and expansion of the terminal
rows give the FULL formal-vector polynomial after value cancellation:

 M=q||x0||^2-2f_n+sum lambda_ij Q_ij
  =||g_n-pi x0+r^T g||^2+(q-pi^2)||x0||^2
    +2<x0,(pi r-mu/2)^T g>
    +g^T[sym(N Ltri)-r r^T]g.                             (3.2)

Here g denotes the column of queried formal gradient vectors and
sym(B)=(B+B^T)/2. This derivation uses no actual-trajectory restriction.

For either chosen column u below, with e^T u=D, define

 dsrc=(e_0-e_1)/b,
 dtail=a gbar e_(l-2)+(1-a beta)e_(l-1)-b e_l,
 chi=1-u^T w, gamma=q0-s^2-delta chi^2.

The family, and no larger unknown metric, is

 M_u=||g_n-pi x0+r^T g||^2
       +delta||x0-u^T g||^2+gamma||dsrc^T g||^2
       +kappa||x0+theta dsrc^T g||^2
       +xi||dtail^T g||^2,                               (3.3)

where theta is real and xi>=0. Coefficient comparison in (3.2) forces

 mu=2pi r+2delta u-2kappa theta dsrc,
 P=r r^T+delta u u^T+(gamma+kappa theta^2)dsrc dsrc^T
                        +xi dtail dtail^T.              (3.4)

Since e^T mu=2pi V+2delta D=2(1-pi), summing (3.1) gives
sum nu=0. Nonnegative optimum incoming multipliers therefore force every
nu_i=0 within THESE families, including at q>q0. This is not a claim
that nu vanishes in the unrestricted loose-target certificate cone.

Every remaining original coefficient, including remote rows, is specified
by the following necessary and sufficient equality interface:

 A^T=-A,
 Ae=E:=Pe-2Ltri^T alpha=V r+delta D u-2(1-pi)v,
 Aw=F:=mu-Pw,
 N=(P+A)Ltri^(-1).                                       (3.5)

It remains necessary that mu>=0 and N_ij<=0 for ALL i!=j. These signs
are not implied by the moment equations or by (3.3) being PSD.
The complete entry formula is

 N_ij=P_ij+A_ij
       -h_j sum_(vindex>j)(P_(i,vindex)+A_(i,vindex))
                         product_(j<uindex<vindex)(1-h_uindex). (3.6)

The inverse has diagonal1 and entry
-h_j product_(j<uindex<vindex)(1-h_uindex) below its diagonal in
row vindex,column j. Solving Ltri z=e_j proves this inverse: after the
first nonzero column coordinate, the next coordinate is multiplied by
1-h at each step. Thus (3.6) retains the original coordinates, rather than
the different older y-coordinate convention.

For any compatible skew, the original array is
lambda_(i,j)=-N_ij for distinct queries, lambda_(i,star)=mu_i,
lambda_(star,i)=0, with the terminal coefficients specified above.
No row remains to be silently selected or deleted.

## 4. Full constant-gradient and unit-quadratic checks; all skew compatibilities

The two detail columns have sum zero. In particular,
a gbar+1-a beta-b=0. On common queried gradients G the entire trial is

 ||g_n-pi x0+V G||^2+delta||x0-D G||^2+kappa||x0||^2.       (4.1)

This matches (3.2): the remaining affine Gram has entries
q-pi^2, pi(V+1)-1, D-(V+1)^2, and V+1=Dpi.
At the actual positive-affine Huber history x0=D G,g_n=G, it equals
kappa D^2||G||^2, the original objective slack. The initial radius is
the original one; there is no restart.

For f(x)=||x||^2/2 starting at x0, the actual queried gradients are w_i x0,
where w_i=product_(j<i)(1-h_j). The full signed product is w_n=-s.
To derive it, the exponent of c from the ruler levels equals n-m;
equivalently sum_(j=1)^(m-1)(2^(m-j)-1)=n-m. Multiplying the n factors
-a c^level gives -a^n c^(n-m)=-beta^n c^(-m).
The original last factors are -a,-beta,-a, so

 w_0=1, w_1=-a,
 w_l=s/a, w_(l-1)=-s/(a beta), w_(l-2)=s/(a^2 beta).

Consequently

 e^T dsrc=e^T dtail=0,
 dsrc^T w=1, dtail^T w=0,
 v^T w=1, r^T w=pi+s.                                   (4.2)

For v, telescope sum_(i<l)h_i w_i=1-w_l and add w_l.
For dtail its product is
s[gbar-(1-a beta)-b beta]/(a beta)=0.

The terminal square in (3.3) therefore vanishes on the entire actual
unit-quadratic trajectory. Every original interpolation row also vanishes.
The trial value is

 delta chi^2+gamma+kappa(1+theta)^2
   =q0-s^2+kappa(1+theta)^2.

The original polynomial value is q-s^2. Thus matching forces, for either
column and with the newly recomputed payment,

 theta(theta+2)=0, that is theta=0 or theta=-2.             (4.3)

This is independently rederived, not imported from the former u.

The necessary and sufficient linear conditions for a real skew A in (3.5)
are e^T E=0, w^T F=0, e^T F=-w^T E. The vectors e,w are independent
because w_0=1 and w_1=-a. With (4.2), the explicit second moment is

 F=(pi-s)r+delta(1+chi)u-[gamma+kappa theta(theta+2)]dsrc.  (4.4)

Direct substitution gives

 e^T E=V^2+delta D^2-2(1-pi)T=0,
 w^T F=q0-s^2-delta chi^2-gamma-kappa theta(theta+2)
      =-kappa theta(theta+2),
 e^T F+w^T E=e^T mu-2alpha^T Ltri w=0.                    (4.5)

Thus ALL linear compatibilities hold at both allowed theta values for every
xi>=0. To exhibit sufficiency, set wbar=e^T w/n,
cvec=w-wbar e, fvec=F-wbar E+(E^T w/n)e. Then

 Acan=(E e^T-e E^T)/n
       +(fvec cvec^T-cvec fvec^T)/(cvec^T cvec).           (4.6)

The three scalar identities give e^T fvec=cvec^T fvec=0, so multiplying
(4.6) by e and w gives E and F. Every completion is Acan+B with
B^T=-B and Be=Bw=0. The cuts below exclude them ALL.

The full clock column, sometimes lost by changing coordinates, is

 Nt=(P+A)e=2Pe-2Ltri^T alpha,
 Pe=V r+delta D u.                                       (4.7)

Although the detail metrics vanish in Pe, they remain in P_ii, in every
entry (3.6), and in all row-capacity comparisons. The mixed theta term
remains in mu. In particular theta=-2 has not been replaced by an unmixed
q0-plus-radius certificate.

## 5. Mechanism 1: check the assigned u_hat and its positive payment

Freeze exactly the assigned first law

 u_old=(D/T)v, Delta=b Ctot/T,
 u_hat=u_old-Delta(e_0-e_l).                              (5.1)

It has sum D and

 (u_hat)_0=b, (u_hat)_i=(D/T)h_i for 0<i<l,
 (u_hat)_l=Uhat:=D/T+Delta.

The source identity uses D-Ctot=T. Every coordinate is positive. Retaining
the last coordinate in (4.2) gives the EXACT product payment

 chi_hat=1-D/T+Delta(1-s/a)
        =(Ctot/T)(a-b s/a),
 gamma_hat=q0-s^2-delta chi_hat^2.                        (5.2)

This is not the old a^2/T^2-s^2. The terminal correction b s/a is retained.
By (2.1)-(2.3), b s/a<15/1024<1/4<a, and
0<chi_hat<(17/16)(1/2)=17/32. Since (17/32)^2<1/3,

 gamma_hat>q0(2/3-1/64)>q0/2, gamma_hat<q0.               (5.3)

Only now is PSD of (3.3) asserted: every displayed weight is nonnegative,
and delta,kappa,gamma_hat are strictly positive.

The forced optimum coefficients can be written explicitly as

 mu=2K0 v+2pi a e_l-2delta Delta(e_0-e_l)
                         -2kappa theta dsrc.             (5.4)

In particular mu_0=2q0 b-2kappa theta/b and
mu_l=2K0+2pi a+2delta Delta. At theta=0 every mu_i>0.
At theta=-2 only index1 loses mass; there
mu_1=2K0 gbar-4kappa/b>1/S-1/(8S)>0 by (2.4).
All terminal signs pass as 0<pi<1 and alpha=(1-pi)e_l.

### Source and tail checks, with the full paid terms retained

The first column of Ltri is (1,b,...,b)^T. Therefore

 mu_0-P_00=a sum_(j>0)lambda_(0,j).

For any of our u with u_0=b this exact left side is

 C_source=q0(1-a^2)-gamma/b^2
                  -kappa(theta^2+2b theta)/b^2.           (5.5)

At theta=0, gamma<q0 and (2.1) give
C_source>q0(3/4-25/49)=47q0/196>q0/5.
At theta=-2 it increases by 4kappa a/b^2, so the same strict margin holds.
Thus the advertised source repair is real.

Equation (4.7) at coordinate0 has (Pe)_0=b(1-pi), yielding the exact
additional source clock condition

 sum_(j>0)lambda_(0,j)(t_j-1)=mu_0.                       (5.6)

This includes the changed theta=-2 optimum coefficient; it has not been
removed from the row equations.

At the original last query, P_ll=N_ll. Hence the full tail capacities are

 P_ll-2(1-pi)=sum_(i<l)lambda_(i,l)>=0,
 P_ll-mu_l=sum_(j<l)lambda_(l,j)>=0,
 P_ll=(a+pi)^2+delta Uhat^2+xi b^2.                       (5.7)

Here D/T<3, Delta<2, so Uhat<5. Therefore the xi=0 diagonal is
less than (17/32)^2+25/(2S)<489/1024<1/2, whereas
2(1-pi)>31/16. The last-column condition requires the nontrivial price

 xi>=[2(1-pi)-(a+pi)^2-delta Uhat^2]/b^2.                (5.8)

No smallness assumption about xi is permitted or used. The last-row
capacity already passes at xi=0 because
P_ll-mu_l=a^2-pi^2+delta Uhat(Uhat-2)>0; Uhat>2 follows
from D/T>2 and Delta>0. The adjacent tail-pair sum, with p=l-1, is exactly

 N_(p,l)+N_(l,p)=2P_(p,l)-gbar P_ll
  =gbar[pi^2-a^2+delta Uhat(2D/T-Uhat)]
       -xi b[2(1-a beta)+gbar b].                         (5.9)

Its two individual signs are still required by (3.6), not inferred from
their sum. The entire last-row clock is (1.3) with Uhat substituted into
the general formula in Section7. These formulas retain the original
last-three metric, both original orientations, and the actual terminal.

## 6. Mechanism 1 decisive cut: a remote physical root

Here derive (1.1) directly from the original array, not from any assumed
skew support. Let k=2^(m-1)-1, H=sum_(j<k)h_j=sum H_(m-1),
R=h_k=1+a c^(m-1), and z=1_[0,k). The original step immediately
before k is b, and alpha_j=0 for every j<k. Since

 (Ltri z)_i=t_i for i<k, (Ltri z)_i=H for i>=k,

expanding z^T N Ltri z and using column sums (N^T e)_j=0 for j<k
gives the exact prefix incoming identity

 C_prefix:=H sum_(j<k)mu_j-z^T P z
          =sum_(i>=k,j<k)lambda_(i,j)(H-t_j).             (6.1)

For explicit cancellation, the internal prefix edges cancel when their
row and column contributions are collected. If I_cross and O_cross
denote total incoming and outgoing crossing weights, the prefix sum of
mu is I_cross-O_cross, and z^T Pz is
sum_(i>=k,j<k)lambda_(i,j)t_j-H O_cross. This proves (6.1).

The original k-th diagonal gives

 P_kk=mu_k+sum_(j<k)lambda_(k,j)
                       -(R-1)sum_(j>k)lambda_(k,j).      (6.2)

For every j<k,
H-t_j=sum_(r=j)^(k-1)h_r-1>=h_(k-1)-1=a.
Adding a times mu_k-P_kk to (6.1) proves precisely the nonnegative
original-row expansion (1.1), including all remote rows.

For the assigned u_hat, the source detail has z^T dsrc=0 and dsrc_k=0;
the last-three detail has z^T dtail=0 and dtail_k=0. Thus theta and xi
cancel EXACTLY from (6.1),(6.2), however large their permitted values.
The original terminal weights and alpha are unchanged.

The scalar prefix sums for (5.1) are

 z^T r=pi H, z^T u_hat=(D/T)H-Delta,
 sum_(j<k)mu_j=2K0 H-2delta Delta,
 z^T Pz=J0 H^2-2delta(D/T)H Delta+delta Delta^2,
 mu_k=2K0 R, P_kk=J0 R^2.

Consequently

 C_prefix=a^2 H^2/T^2
             +(delta Ctot^2/T^2)(2bH-b^2).               (6.3)

Because H<S/2, T>S/2, and
delta Ctot^2=S(S-2ab)/D<S/2,

 C_prefix<1/4+3<4.                                      (6.4)

The bound is an upper bound on the entire prefix resource, not a decision
that it alone is sufficient. At the actual root,

 R>a c^(m-1)>(a/c)S>4S/25>S/8,
 mu_k<4, P_kk>S/128,                                    (6.5)

using (2.4), R<S, and S<c^m. Since S>4096, the quantity
4-S/128 is negative and a>2/5. Combining (6.4),(6.5) gives

 C_root<4+(2/5)(4-S/128)=28/5-S/320<-S/640<0.            (6.6)

The last comparison uses S/640>4096/640>28/5. This contradicts (1.1)
for the entire first family, including both compatible theta values and
every xi meeting the mandatory tail price (5.8). It is independent of all
neutral skew freedom, not a failure of a guessed coefficient array.

## 7. Mechanism 2: the one frozen further redistribution and its payment

The negative term in (6.6) comes from the common-column diagonal at a
remote large step. The sole additional law transfers ALL interior common
mass to the original last query:

 u_new=u_hat-sum_(i=1)^(l-1)(u_hat)_i(e_i-e_l)
      =b e_0+(D-b)e_l.                                   (7.1)

This law was recorded before evaluating its signs. It has no fitted
coefficient or unspecified metric, and retains the identical r,pi,alpha,
q,dsrc,dtail,theta,xi from the first family. Write dlast=D-b=2S-a.
The exact full unit product is

 u_new^T w=b+dlast s/a,
 chi_new=-a-dlast s/a,
 gamma_new=q0-s^2-delta(a+dlast s/a)^2.                    (7.2)

No old payment is reused. Since dlast<2S and a>2/5,
dlast s/a<5/256<1/32. Hence |chi_new|<17/32 and the same explicit
argument as (5.3) gives

 q0/2<gamma_new<q0.                                      (7.3)

Thus (3.3) with this exact payment is PSD for every theta and xi>=0.
Equations (3.4)-(3.6) and (4.1)-(4.7), derived jointly for either column,
give its full coefficient and compatibility equations. In particular the
entire original unit-quadratic still forces theta=0,-2, and every linear
skew compatibility holds at those two values.

Here the optimum coefficients are

 mu_0=2q0 b-2kappa theta/b,
 mu_i=2pi^2 h_i-2kappa theta(dsrc)_i for 0<i<l,
 mu_l=2pi(a+pi)+2delta dlast.                             (7.4)

At theta=0 all are positive. At theta=-2,
mu_1=2pi^2 gbar-4kappa/b<9/S^2-1/(18S)<0 for S>4096.
The bound uses pi<3/(2S), gbar<2, D<3S and b<3/2.
So theta=-2 already fails an ORIGINAL optimum outgoing row.
The decisive cut below covers every theta anyway.

The source formula (5.5), with gamma_new, again has margin>q0/5 at
theta=0,-2. This second law also genuinely removes the first obstruction.
At the same root its exact cut is

 C_root,new=pi^2[H^2+a(2R-R^2)]+delta(2bH-b^2)>0.         (7.5)

To prove the sign, use vword=a/(c-2)>2a and the positive term
(1-vword)2^(m-1) in the mass formula. It gives
H>2a c^(m-1)-1=2R-3>R since R>S/8>3. Thus
H^2-aR^2>0, 2aR>0 and 2bH-b^2>0. This is a passed necessary
screen, not the claimed success of the new family.

Now preserve the actual tail and clock equations. For any u with u_l=U
in these two families, the last query has

 P_ll=(a+pi)^2+delta U^2+xi b^2,
 mu_l=2pi(a+pi)+2delta U,
 (Pe)_l=V(a+pi)+delta D U,
 (Nt)_l=2(Pe)_l-2(1-pi).                                 (7.6)

Both dsrc_l and its mixed contribution are zero, whereas dtail_l=-b.
Therefore the full xi b^2 term remains in the last diagonal. The same
formula (7.6) applies with U=Uhat or U=dlast without a change of coordinates.

The original last row has outgoing sum P_ll-mu_l. Since the actual
penultimate physical step is gbar, every earlier clock difference obeys
T-t_j>=gbar, with equality only at j=l-1. Expanding that row of Nt gives

 (Nt)_l=T mu_l+sum_(j<l)lambda_(l,j)(T-t_j).

Subtract gbar times the last-row outgoing sum. This proves the literal
nonnegative cut (1.3). Its full value, retaining every tail metric term, is

 C_last(U)=(1-beta)a^2+2b pi+gbar pi^2-2
             +delta U[2(Ctot+gbar)-gbar U]-gbar xi b^2.   (7.7)

For the constant part, substitute V=a+pi T into (7.6):
2(a+pi)(a+pi gbar)-gbar(a+pi)^2-2(1-pi)
=(1-beta)a^2+2b pi+gbar pi^2-2.
Thus no large terminal common coefficient or tail metric is omitted.

For the frozen endpoint law U=dlast=D-b, the bracket in (7.7) is exactly

 2(Ctot+gbar)-gbar(D-b)
   =-2beta S+b(2+gbar)+gbar
   <-(3/2)S+8<-S.                                       (7.8)

The constant part of (7.7) is negative: it is less than
1/4+3/32+1/512-2<0 by (2.1),(2.2).
Finally Dpi^2<27/(4S)<1/2, so delta>1/(2D), while
dlast=D-b>D/2. Consequently delta dlast>1/4. Equations (7.7),(7.8)
give the strict uniform result

 C_last(dlast)<-S/4-gbar xi b^2<=-S/4<0.                 (7.9)

This contradicts the nonnegative sum (1.3), for every real theta and
every xi>=0, regardless of completing skew. The second family is decisively
excluded. It repaired the first root cut but placed too much common mass
at a query whose nearest earlier clock is already gbar units away.
Adding the included last-three PSD detail can only worsen (7.9).

## 8. Status, dependencies, and bounded-round handoff

Both mechanisms are complete as scoped joint algebraic exclusions. No
additional common column or metric is opened. There is no assertion that
the displayed obstructions settle the feasibility of the unrestricted
original certificate cone.

The useful new cut interfaces are (1.1)/(6.1)-(6.2) and (1.3)/(7.7).
They expose respectively the available incoming resource before a remote
large query and the last query's weighted outgoing resource. A future
different law must satisfy both; passing either alone is insufficient.
These interfaces and the conclusions remain UNVERIFIED shared findings,
not premises licensed for use as fact-graph results.

Accepted1252b8823bb5a290 is used at its exact original interpolation and
terminal-reduction scope. All new coefficient identities and inequalities
are rederived in this one draft. The previous draft a40ecd591138eb2c was
read in full as context, not used as a verified predecessor. The former
accepted gate1e0bf3f41457d8e4 is not an all-depth premise.

Accepted931b9653ce0f7201 has a CONDITIONAL full-function implication from
W_(2^m-1)(H_m)<=17/(16D_m) for EVERY m>=1536 to an asymptotic benchmark
separation. This report supplies no such full-function bound. Its exact
benchmark scope therefore does not turn either family obstruction into a
resolution of PROBLEM.md. The original radius and W=2R are unchanged.

# Dependent Walsh dictionaries: scalar symmetry, nonacute cone memory, and a paid cap release

**Status: JOINT UNVERIFIED DRAFT.** Worker max2, one allocated analytical
invocation after ended baseline66. This is not a fact or a solution of the
original minimax problem. No verification call is allocated. Three explicit
laws, and no fourth law, are examined below.

The quantitative conclusions concern Laws II and III on the ENTIRE original
same-enclosing-m word H_(m,1/4), for every m>=1536. Law II, including every
static complete-prefix interpolant and its OWN closest radius, has payoff at
most the full unit-quadratic product. Law III allows an arbitrarily long
positive-height cap history followed by a controlled nonacute release. Its
release branch has a vanishing Huber-relative bound, and its all-cap branch
and all its completions have original-B ratio below19/20. These are precise
restricted-family boundaries, not upper bounds for unrestricted W.

Law I has a different conclusion: its whole run and maximal completion reduce
exactly to a scalar asymmetric-Huber transcript and its scalar completion.
That is a full-word loss of the proposed VECTOR mechanism. It is NOT a proof
that every such scalar transcript is dominated by the accepted K/ray channel.
No quantitative new advantage over that entire channel is established here.
No blanket failure of dependent characters, arbitrary initializations, metrics,
offsets, or other bodies is claimed.

Accepted inputs: fact_id1252b8823bb5a290, read in full, supplies the ordered
interpolation necessity and complete-prefix maximal conjugate. Only the
scalar same-word comparison in Section10 equation(77) of accepted
fact_id5fd396eba75651e7 is used for the final original-B comparisons.
The definitions of the existing scalar K channel were read in accepted
fact_idb98bb807d514e80d; its full-channel dominance is not assumed.
Every other argument is proved jointly below. In particular prior unverified
bb1edc657a2239e2 and related reports are awareness, not premises.

## 1. Normalization, the original word, and its literal products

Work first at smoothness1. For one actual function f with attained minimum0,
write

    E(f,h)=2 f(x_n)/dist(x_0,argmin f)^2,
    x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i grad f(x_i).

Initial minima are excluded. E uses W=2R, not the normalization R. Multiplying
f by any advertised L>0 and using physical steps h_i/L preserves all points
and this payoff. Every general-word statement below permits all finite
nonnegative steps, including zero, huge and unordered steps, unless its
explicit geometric guard restricts that word/initialization pair.

For the diagnostic fix m>=1536 and keep this SAME m in every child:

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,
    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,
    h=H_m, n=2^m-1, A_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, S_j=sum H_j, S=S_m=A_n.

The physical entry at index i is 1+beta*c^(v2(i+1)-1), where v2(k) is the
exponent of2 in a positive integer k. This follows by locating the central
entry of each literal recursive child. Let s2(i) count the ones in the binary
expansion of i, with s2(0)=0. Then

    p_i=product_(t<i)(1-h_t)=(-1)^i beta^i c^(-s2(i)),
    |p_i|<=a for 1<=i<=n, |p_n|=beta^n c^(-m).       (1)

Indeed sum_(k=1)^i v2(k)=sum_(r>=1)floor(i/2^r)=i-s2(i): each binary digit
2^r contributes 2^r-1 to the middle sum. This proves (1) symbolically; no
trajectory, product, or recurrence was executed. All p_i are nonzero here.
The last physical step is 1+a. In particular all original steps exceed1.

Put v_mass=beta/[c(c-2)]. Counting the occurrences of each level gives

    S_j+1=(1-v_mass)2^j+v_mass c^j,
    1/2<v_mass<1, 2<c<5/2, 0<a<1/2,
    S>600, 1+2S<2c^m.                              (2)

For the mass formula, sum the finite geometric sum
S_j=2^j-1+a(c^j-2^j)/(c-2). Since c>lambda and lambda(lambda-2)=1,
v_mass<1. Since beta>999/1000 and c(c-2)<5/4, v_mass>1/2. The stated
enclosures for c and beta follow directly from m>=1536 and
241/100<lambda<121/50; the latter follows by squaring the corresponding
rational bounds on sqrt(2). Also S>2^m-1>600.

For clarity the benchmark is always the ORIGINAL all-split one:

    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,
    Gbench(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2,
    C_0=B_0=1,
    C_n=max_(0<=j<n)Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=j<n)Gbench(C_j,B_(n-j-1)),
    U_n^ZJ=1/B_n, H_base=1/(1+2S).

These are definitions, not executed benchmark recurrences. Accepted5fd,
at its exact stated scope, gives

    B_n H_base<237/250, m>=1536, tau=1/4.            (3)

## 2. One actual projection function and every original row

Let D be any of the explicitly specified compact convex bodies below,
containing0, and put C=cone(D). Their cones are closed. Define ONE function
before GD by

    J(y)=max_(v a listed vertex of D)<v,y>,
    F_D(x)=max_(g in D){<x,g>-||g||^2/2}.             (4)

The zero plane is included, explicitly or as a convex combination of the
listed planes. All offsets in the three laws are0. The metric is Euclidean
identity; no uncharged change of metric or expected Gram is used.

The unique maximizer in (4) is g=P_D(x), characterized by

    g in D, <x-g,w-g><=0 for every w in D.           (5)

Adding (5) for two points proves
<x-y,P_D(x)-P_D(y)> >= ||P_D(x)-P_D(y)||^2. Thus P_D is1-Lipschitz.
Comparing the two maximizers in (4) bounds its first-order remainder between
0 and ||x-y||^2. Consequently F_D is finite, convex, differentiable and
1-smooth, with gradient P_D. On a nonempty interior of D the gradient is the
identity, so the least smoothness constant is1 in both full-dimensional
bodies used below.

For actual samples, including the terminal and any minimum, write

    g_i=P_D(x_i), r_i=x_i-g_i,
    delta_i=<r_i,g_i>, f_i=delta_i+||g_i||^2/2.

Comparison with0 in (5) gives delta_i>=0. Expansion proves every ordered row:

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
        =<r_j,g_i-g_j><=0.                          (6)

This checks opposite, remote, optimum and terminal rows in BOTH directions.
All original vectors are real vectors in the stated space, so the WHOLE
Gram matrix is PSD. No formal gradient, physical query or row is deleted.

The complete minimum is

    min F_D=0, argmin F_D=C^polar,
    C^polar={x:<x,v><=0 for every listed vertex v}.  (7)

One direction follows by comparing every term in (4) with0. If x is not
polar, a sufficiently small positive multiple of a vertex with positive
inner product makes (4) strictly positive. This proves the other direction.

## 3. All-prefix completion and a general zero-height boundary

Retain EVERY original queried jet 0<=i<n and the optimum jet(0,0,0). The
maximal complete-prefix function from accepted1252 is, for these projection
jets,

    H_pre(u)=||u||^2/2+max(0,max_(i<n)(<r_i,u>-delta_i)),
    F_pre(x)=sup_u(<x,u>-H_pre(u)).                  (8)

All coefficients are mathematical functions of the fixed old function,
initial state and full known word; (8) defines one static function BEFORE its
own run. Induction on every physical index gives the same old prefix and
terminal POSITION. Its terminal gradient is its own unique maximizer in(8).
It is never silently assigned the old terminal gradient. Accepted1252 gives
all ordered rows for this actual function, including every own minimizer.

Its ENTIRE minimum set is

    R_pre=conv({0} union {r_i:i<n,delta_i=0}).         (9)

Indeed F_pre(x)=0 iff x is a subgradient of H_pre at0. At0 exactly the
zero-offset affine terms are active. Their convex hull gives all such
subgradients: a point outside that hull is strictly separated, and a small
positive displacement in the separating direction contradicts the
subgradient inequality, since positive-offset terms stay inactive there.

Every other static convex1-smooth K interpolating the ENTIRE same prefix
and optimum has K<=F_pre pointwise and reaches the same x_n. For n>=1 its last outgoing
terminal row, with its actual terminal gradient unrestricted, gives

    K(x_n)<=delta_(n-1)+||x_n-r_(n-1)||^2/2.         (10)

This is completion of the terminal-gradient square in Q_(n,n-1), and uses
the ACTUAL last step. Pointwise maximality alone never transfers a radius.

Here is a useful boundary that is not restricted to projection functions.
Assume n>=1. Suppose an actual convex1-smooth prefix with minimum0 has

    f_i=||g_i||^2/2 for EVERY 0<=i<n, g_0!=0.       (11)

For i<n-1 its original row Q_(i+1,i)<=0 becomes exactly

    ||g_(i+1)||^2+(h_i-1)<g_(i+1),g_i><=0.

Cauchy--Schwarz yields
||g_(i+1)||<=|1-h_i| ||g_i||, including h_i=0 or1 and zero gradients.
Also r_0=x_0-g_0 is a minimum: smoothness gives f(r_0)<=0. The original
initial optimum row for ANY own minimum y gives
<g_0,y-r_0><=0. Thus r_0 is the closest minimum and the exact radius is
||g_0||. These arguments apply separately to every legal completion sharing
the prefix; it also has that minimum and the same exact closest radius.
Using (10) now gives the complete bound

    E(K,h)<=product_(i<n)(1-h_i)^2.                 (12)

This keeps the unqueried terminal free. It does not require its height to be
zero. In particular purely zero-height queried memory cannot beat the
unit-curvature scalar quadratic, regardless of nonacuteness, dimension or
terminal completion. A claim that (11) holds follows neither from initial
height0 alone nor from a polar normal alone; it must be proved through the
WHOLE queried prefix. Laws II and III below do that where claimed.

## 4. The genuinely dependent finite character dictionaries

Choose an integer q>=2, let G=(Z/2Z)^q and N=2^q. For every nonzero a in G
use the real character chi_a(s)=(-1)^(a dot s), with the dot product modulo2.
All N orbit weights are1/N. The N-1 character coordinates are DEPENDENT:

    chi_a(s)chi_b(s)=chi_(a+b)(s), a!=b, a,b!=0.

They are not N-1 independent sign bits. Pairing s with s+t for a character
nontrivial on t proves its sum is0. The same pairing over a gives

    sum_(a!=0)chi_a(s)chi_a(t)=N-1 if s=t, -1 otherwise. (13)

For q=2 the full four sign patterns, in coordinates a=(10),(01),(11), are

    s=00: (+,+,+), s=01: (+,-,-),
    s=10: (-,+,-), s=11: (-,-,+).

The formulas below enumerate EVERY slope for arbitrary q; none is sampled.

Law I uses the centered slopes

    z_s=sigma*(chi_a(s))_(a!=0) in R^(N-1), sigma>0,
    D_I=conv{z_s:s in G}.                            (14)

Their Gram has diagonal sigma^2(N-1), off-diagonal -sigma^2, and sum_s z_s=0.
They span R^(N-1), so0 is interior to their simplex.

Laws II and III use the carried slopes, with b>1,

    v_s=(1,b*(chi_a(s))_(a!=0)) in R^N,
    D=conv({0} union {v_s:s in G}), C=cone{v_s},
    M=||v_s||^2=1+(N-1)b^2>N>=4.                   (15)

Distinct slopes have inner product1-b^2<0. This is genuinely NONACUTE.
The matrix V with columns v_s is invertible: its Gram is

    V^T V=d I+e 11^T, d=Nb^2, e=1-b^2,

whose eigenvalues are Nb^2 on the sum-zero space and N on the constant
space. The dual columns are explicitly

    w_s=(1/N,(chi_a(s)/(Nb))_(a!=0)),
    <w_s,v_t>=1 if s=t and0 otherwise.              (16)

Thus C={V alpha:alpha>=0}, C^polar={r:<r,v_s><=0 for all s}, and
D={V alpha:alpha>=0,sum alpha<=1}. Its cap is the ORIGINAL first-coordinate
bound (g)_carrier<=1. In particular

    C intersect {g:||g||<=1} is contained in D.     (17)

This is a deterministic real realization with identity metric and offsets0.
There is no averaging of distinct or illegal trajectories.

## 5. Law I: exact whole-word scalar equivalence, and its precise limit

Choose ANY nonempty proper subset A of G, let p=|A|, r=N-p, and set

    u_A=(1/p)sum_(s in A)z_s,
    R_plus=sigma*sqrt(r/p), R_minus=sigma*sqrt(p/r),
    e_A=u_A/R_plus, x_0=t_0 e_A, t_0!=0.            (18)

All choices, including t_0, are fixed before the run. Equation(13) proves
||u_A||=R_plus, <z_s,e_A>=R_plus on A and -R_minus off A. The opposite
barycenter is -R_minus e_A. The ENTIRE projection along this line is

    P_(D_I)(t e_A)=clip(t,[-R_minus,R_plus])*e_A.    (19)

For interior t the displayed point lies on the segment joining the two
barycenters, hence is its own projection. For t>=R_plus, its residual is a
nonnegative multiple of e_A; every original vertex has e_A-coordinate at
most R_plus, proving ALL its normal guards. The negative case uses the
minimum coordinate -R_minus. These arguments include both boundary ties.

Therefore (4) on this line is exactly the scalar asymmetric Huber phi with
phi(0)=0 and phi'(t)=clip(t,[-R_minus,R_plus]). For EVERY finite nonnegative
word, including arbitrarily many cap returns,

    x_i=t_i e_A, g_i=phi'(t_i)e_A,
    t_(i+1)=t_i-h_i phi'(t_i), f_i=phi(t_i),
    E(F_(D_I),h)=2phi(t_n)/t_0^2.                   (20)

This is an identity with the actual scalar GD, not an executed recurrence
or a choice of gradients. The complete minimum of F_(D_I) is{0}, because
the cone of D_I is the full space. Its exact radius is|t_0|.

All old residuals are on this same line. With gamma_i=phi'(t_i) and
d_i=t_i-gamma_i, the full completion is exactly

    F_pre(t e_A+z)=phi_pre(t)+||z||^2/2, z perpendicular to e_A,
    phi_pre(t)=sup_u[t u-u^2/2-max(0,max_(i<n)(d_i u-delta_i))]. (21)

Its actual terminal gradient is phi_pre'(t_n)e_A, and its value is
phi_pre(t_n). Here delta_i=phi(t_i)-gamma_i^2/2. A zero-height Huber jet has
d_i=0, including cap boundaries. Hence its complete minimum is{0}, with
the SAME exact radius|t_0|. Formula(21) keeps every original scalar anchor.

For n>=1 and ANY other legal completion K, the original initial optimum row reduces
to gamma_0<e_A,y><=0 for every minimum y, since
phi(t_0)=t_0 gamma_0-gamma_0^2/2. gamma_0 has the sign of t_0, so
dist(t_0 e_A,argmin K)>=|t_0|; the retained minimum0 gives equality. Thus
its OWN true radius is|t_0| and K(t_n e_A)<=phi_pre(t_n). The scalar function
phi_pre itself attains this maximum over completions. No orthogonal
terminal gradient or newly closer minimum gives a vector advantage.
For n=0 there is no initial queried jet: the radius equality for arbitrary
completions is NOT asserted. The maximal empty-prefix completion is simply
||x||^2/2, and every other empty-prefix completion has its own normalized
no-step payoff at most1 by smoothness at its own closest minimum. The old
function's exact scalar identity(20) still includes the empty word.

This is the exact full-word boundary of Law I: these dependent-character
initializations, even with unrestricted legal prefix completion, supply
only scalar information. It does NOT establish a whole-family inequality
against K_n or the alternating-ray channel. That scalar comparison remains
open here and is not silently replaced by a claim about all scalar functions.
For illustration only, the no-crossing choices
t_0=R_plus(1+2S) attain H_base on H_m by (20): every query is on the positive
cap and t_n=R_plus(1+S). This attains an existing channel, not an improvement.

## 6. Exhaustive original face guards for the carried simplex

For ANY x in R^N put z=V^T x and write g=V alpha. The exact projection onto
D is characterized by

    alpha_s>=0, T=sum_s alpha_s<=1,
    nu>=0, nu(T-1)=0,
    z_s-d alpha_s-eT<=nu for every s,
    equality whenever alpha_s>0.                   (22)

These are sufficient directly: r=x-V alpha has <r,v_s><=nu, equality on
the support. Then <r,g>=nu T, so every convex combination of the original
vertices and0 satisfies (5). They are necessary by comparisons in each
coefficient direction, within each face and toward0. Uniqueness follows
from the positive definite Gram. Thus (22) includes every opposite vertex,
the origin and the cap, not only the currently proposed face.

There is an explicit finite cell description. For a nonempty proposed
support I of size k, the cone candidate (nu=0) has

    T=(sum_(s in I)z_s)/(d+ke),
    alpha_s=(z_s-eT)/d for s in I, alpha_s=0 otherwise. (23)

Here d+ke=b^2(N-k)+k>0. Retain the candidate exactly when its coefficients
are nonnegative, T<=1, and z_t<=eT off I. The origin candidate is valid
exactly when every z_s<=0. A capped candidate has

    tau_I=(sum_(s in I)z_s-d)/k,
    alpha_s=(z_s-tau_I)/d for s in I, T=1,
    nu=tau_I-e.                                   (24)

Retain it exactly when alpha_s>=0, nu>=0 and z_t<=tau_I off I. At ties all
valid candidates give the same g. This enumerates the whole finite normal
fan. No matrix inverse, active-face search or trajectory was executed.

Removing T<=1 and taking nu=0 gives the cone projection. Its residual is
polar and orthogonal to g: compare the cone normal with0 and2g for
orthogonality, then with every generator for polarity. If r is polar then
P_C(r)=0. The same two-normal argument used in Section2 proves
nonexpansiveness of P_C. Consequently every cone decomposition x=r+g gives

    ||P_C(x-hg)||=||P_C(r+(1-h)g)-P_C(r)||
                     <=|1-h| ||g||               (25)

for EVERY h>=0. This bound needs no acute-angle condition. It does NOT say
P_C(r-tg)=0 for t>0. That stronger assertion is generally false.

## 7. Law II: arbitrary unequal exterior normals, with a small cone component

Choose ANY nonempty proper subset I of G, positive numbers a_s for s in I,
and nonnegative eta_s for s outside I, at least one nonzero. Set

    z_I=sum_(s in I)a_s v_s,
    g_init=theta*z_I/||z_I||, 0<theta<=1,
    r_init=-sum_(s outside I)eta_s w_s,
    x_0=r_init+g_init.                             (26)

The eta_s are independent and unbounded; initial coordinates may lie
arbitrarily far outside the base. The positive a_s need not be equal and
can put the initial cone component in any proper face. All slopes, weights,
offsets, dimension, metric and the initialized state are now explicit.
Equation(16) proves r_init is polar and orthogonal to g_init, so g_init is
P_C(x_0). By (17) it is also P_D(x_0). The true old closest minimum is
r_init, with exact squared radius theta^2, however large the eta_s become.

For a general finite word this law has the sufficient domain guard

    theta*max_(0<=i<=n) product_(t<i)|1-h_t|<=1.     (27)

No positivity, ordering or upper step bound replaces that guard. It allows
zero steps and arbitrary individual huge steps whenever the displayed
initial scale pays for them. At h_t=1, the cone component becomes0 and the
minimum absorbs every remaining physical step.

Inductively (25), (27), and (17) make EVERY original D projection equal the
cone projection. Thus

    ||g_i||<=theta*product_(t<i)|1-h_t|,
    delta_i=0 for EVERY 0<=i<=n.                    (28)

All actual active faces are determined by (22)--(23). The cap never has a
positive normal multiplier; its possible zero-height boundary at the first
query causes no exception. This is a proof through ALL physical indices,
not an inference from an early cap-free prefix.

The full old terminal has value ||g_n||^2/2 and its actual gradient g_n.
For n>=1, the complete-prefix function has every height zero, so let

    R=conv({0} union {r_i:0<=i<n}).

Then (8) gives the exact complete terminal and minimum formulas

    F_pre(x)=dist(x,R)^2/2, grad F_pre(x)=x-P_R(x),
    argmin F_pre=R, dist(x_0,R)^2=theta^2.           (29)

To prove the first identity, let y=P_R(x), u=x-y. The projection normal
makes y a maximizer of <u,r> over R; hence x is a subgradient of
||u||^2/2+max_(r in R)<u,r>. Substitution gives the asserted value and
unique gradient. For the radius, R is contained in C^polar and contains
r_init=r_0, so the original polar orthogonality proves nearestness.
For n>=1 every other completion separately has this same exact closest radius
by(11)--(12); its whole minimum need not equal R. All completion claims in
this law assume n>=1. With an empty word the old function has payoff1,
and the maximal empty-prefix quadratic has payoff1 about its own minimum;
an arbitrary empty-prefix completion need not have the old radius theta.

For all these functions on the unchanged H_m, guard(27) follows from(1),
and the quantitative FULL terminal bound is

    E<=|p_n|^2=beta^(2n)c^(-2m),
    E/H_base<2 beta^(2n)c^(-m)<1/2,
    B_n E<237/500.                                 (30)

The final inequality uses only (3). The relative bound tends to0. In
particular no rank, unequal-normal or large-initial-coordinate choice in
THIS declared law repairs its deficit.

One explicit scope test refutes negative-ray absorption in this very
dependent dictionary. Take I={0}, and choose eta_t=0 at one nonzero t,
while a different eta_s is arbitrarily large and positive (N>=4 permits
this). Then at the first original leaf

    x_1=r_init-a*theta*v_0/sqrt(M),
    <x_1,v_t>=a*theta*(b^2-1)/sqrt(M)>0.

Thus x_1 is NOT polar and g_1!=0; it is not absorbed merely because the
coefficient of the old ray became negative. Its projection is nevertheless
governed by all of(22), and its ENTIRE completed terminal still obeys(30).
This is a complete-domain counterexample to the false absorption step,
not an assertion of useful terminal memory.

## 8. Law III: delayed positive-height cap history and a controlled release

Assume n>=1 throughout Law III. This law deliberately permits positive interpolation heights in a whole
original prefix. Use the carried dictionary(15), distinguish v=v_0, and set

    r=-sum_(s!=0)eta_s w_s, eta_s>=0 independently,
    x_0=r+t*v, t>=1.                               (31)

The normal can be nonzero and arbitrarily large. Its inner products with
the other vertices need not be equal. Since r is polar and <r,v>=0, the
true closest old minimum is r and the exact old radius squared is M t^2.
For EVERY scalar u>=1 the actual cap projection is v: its residual
r+(u-1)v supports v against0 and every other vertex, because all vertices
have squared norm M and <v,v_s><=M. Consequently while t-A_i>=1,

    x_i=r+(t-A_i)v, g_i=v,
    r_i=r+(t-A_i-1)v,
    delta_i=M(t-A_i-1), f_i=M(t-A_i-1/2).           (32)

The law's domain is the union of exactly these two cases:

* **Release:** the first queried index k with t-A_k<1 satisfies 1<=k<n,
  d_rel=t-A_k>=0, and

      d_rel*sqrt(M)*T_k<=1,
      T_k=max_(k<=j<=n) product_(k<=i<j)|1-h_i|.    (33)

* **All queried indices capped:** t-A_i>=1 for every 0<=i<n.

These guards are part of the law, not assertions for arbitrary starts.
All finite nonnegative words are permitted subject to them. All products
are literal finite definitions; no product or recurrence is executed.
On H_m, a release can be specified explicitly by choosing any original
k in{1,...,n-1}, choosing
0<=d_rel<1 with d_rel sqrt(M)T_k<=1, and setting t=A_k+d_rel.
Every earlier original state is then capped because its remaining mass
includes the preceding step>1. In the all-cap case take any t>=S-a.
The dictionary and initial state are fixed before the entire run.

### 8.1 Every later face, complete minimum and actual completed terminal

At release x_k=r+d_rel v has cone projection d_rel v and residual r.
Equations(25), (33), and(17) inductively prove that all later original
projections are cone projections, including the terminal. All their heights
are zero and their norms are bounded by

    ||g_j||<=d_rel sqrt(M) product_(k<=i<j)|1-h_i|. (34)

They can change to other nonacute faces; no negative-ray absorption is
asserted. All actual faces follow the exhaustive original guards(22).
The maximum in(33) proves that NO positive-height cap reactivation is
possible anywhere in the remaining word. This is precisely the restriction
that the unrestricted outside-base problem does not currently supply.

For the full completion let

    R=conv({0,r} union {r_i:k<=i<n}).                (35)

Any earlier zero-height cap anchor also equals r. By(9), this is exactly
the complete new minimum. It is contained in C^polar and contains r, so
the new true radius squared is M t^2. For ANY other completion, the release
jet forces r to be a minimum (also when d_rel=0). The initial optimum row
gives <v,y><=0 for every own minimum y. Since <v,x_0>=Mt, its OWN closest
radius is exactly t sqrt(M), attained at r. This pays each completion
separately.

All positive-height cap anchors are RETAINED in (8). Nevertheless at the
ACTUAL terminal its value and gradient have the exact closed geometric law

    y_n=P_R(x_n), u_n=x_n-y_n,
    F_pre(x_n)=||u_n||^2/2, grad F_pre(x_n)=u_n.     (36)

Here is the proof that the old cap anchors do not change that optimizer.
For the maximum using only the zero-height normals, (29) gives u_n. Since
r_(n-1) belongs to R,

    ||u_n||<=||x_n-r_(n-1)||
            =|1-h_(n-1)| ||g_(n-1)||<=1

by(33)--(34). Thus <v,u_n><=sqrt(M)<M. Each old cap anchor is

    <r,u>+(t-A_i-1)(<v,u>-M).

At u_n it is at most <r,u_n>, already in the zero-height maximum. The full
H_pre is no smaller than that reduced maximum anywhere, and equal at its
unique terminal optimizer. Their conjugate terminal values and optimizers
therefore coincide. This proves (36) while preserving every old anchor in
the actual static function, not by deleting a prefix sample.

In particular every completion has the quantitative full-word bound

    E<= (d_rel/t)^2 product_(k<=i<n)(1-h_i)^2,
    E<=1/(M t^2).                                  (37)

The first follows from(36), using the last original residual and(34).
The second follows from the same calculation and(33). The maximal
completion itself is a single actual function attaining(36), with its
actual gradient and own complete minimum as stated.

For completeness P_R in(29),(36) has a finite exact face formula, not an
unpaid feasible-interpolation oracle. List the finitely many residuals in
R, including0. For each affinely independent sublist q_0,...,q_l let E be
the matrix of columns q_j-q_0. For l>0 set
b=(E^T E)^(-1)E^T(x-q_0), y=q_0+E b; for l=0 set y=q_0.
Retain it iff b_j>=0, sum b_j<=1, and <x-y,q-y><=0 for EVERY listed q.
At least one such sublist exists for the true closest point: start with
its convex coefficients; whenever their positive support is affinely
dependent, vary the coefficients along a nonzero affine dependence until
one becomes zero, preserving their sum and represented point. Repeating
this finite support reduction gives an independent sublist. Every
retained candidate is that same point by the squared-distance expansion.
This formula retains every remote normal, handles repeated normals via
independent sublists, and is NOT executed here. The bound(37), rather than
this face rule alone, is the quantitative conclusion.

### 8.2 Entire original H_m: early and late releases are both paid

On H_m the product in(37) is |p_n/p_k|. If
k<=2^(m-1)-1, then n-k>=2^(m-1) and s2(k)<=m. Since d_rel<1 and t>=1,

    E<=beta^(2(n-k))*c^(2(s2(k)-m))<=beta^n<c^(-2m). (38)

To check the final strict bound textually, log beta<=-1/(4m), c<5/2<exp(1),
and 2^m-1>8m^2 for m>=1536. The last elementary inequality follows from
2^10>10^3 and induction on 2^j>=j^3 for j>=10, using
2j^3-(j+1)^3=j^2(j-3)-3j-1>0 for j>=4, followed by m^3-1>8m^2.
Thus beta^n<exp(-2m)<c^(-2m). No asymptotic threshold is left implicit.

If k>=2^(m-1), the preceding original capped query has
A_(k-1)>=S_(m-1). Hence t>=1+S_(m-1)>c^(m-1)/2 by(2). Since M>4,
the SECOND bound in(37) gives

    E<c^(2-2m)<7c^(-2m).                           (39)

This split exhausts all original queried release indices. Combining(38),
(39), and(2)--(3), for EVERY m>=1536 and EVERY release in the declared law,

    E/H_base<14c^(-m)<1/2, B_n E<237/500.           (40)

This covers the entire right child, all large bridges and the literal last
leaf. It is not a finite-prefix cap-return observation.

### 8.3 All-cap history: the true completed terminal and every own radius

In this paragraph specialize to h=H_m, whose last step is1+a. All old
queried coefficients t-A_i are at least1. Put

    w=t-S, d_last=w+a>=0, N_r=||r||^2,
    R_last=r+d_last v, X=x_n=r+w v.

The full completion retains every cap anchor from(32). If N_r+M d_last^2>0
define

    p=clip((N_r+M d_last*(w-1))/(N_r+M d_last^2),[0,1]),
    u_n=X-p R_last.

Then its ACTUAL terminal is

    grad F_pre(X)=u_n,
    F_pre(X)=||u_n||^2/2+p M d_last.               (41)

If N_r=d_last=0, take u_n=X and F_pre(X)=||X||^2/2 instead.

To verify all remote cap anchors, first optimize the single last hinge
||u||^2/2+max(0,<R_last,u>-M d_last). Its hinge weight p minimizes
||X-pR_last||^2/2+pM d_last on[0,1]; the derivative and endpoint signs give
the displayed clipped coefficient and its subgradient condition. Its
coefficient along v is <=1: if p=0, the defining inequality forces
d_last<=1+a whenever d_last>0, so w<=1. If p>0 the coefficient is
(-a N_r+M d_last^2)/(N_r+M d_last^2)<=1. The zero denominator case has
coefficient -a. Every earlier cap coefficient t-A_i-1 is at least d_last.
When <v,u><=M its anchor is therefore at most the last one. The full maximum
is equal to the last-hinge maximum at this actual optimizer and no smaller
elsewhere. This proves (41) without suppressing a remote anchor or imposing
the old terminal gradient.

The COMPLETE completed minimum and its closest radius are

    d_last>0: argmin F_pre={0}, radius^2=M t^2+N_r;
    d_last=0: argmin F_pre=[0,r], radius^2=M t^2.    (42)

For the second line, earlier zero-height normals, if any, are also r. The
point r is nearest since x_0=r+t v and r is perpendicular to v. For the
first line every queried cap height is positive, so(9) applies directly.

For ANY other completion, its own minimum y obeys <v,y><=0 by the initial
optimum row. Thus its OWN radius squared is at least M t^2; equality is
not asserted without a minimum attaining it. Equation(10) at the actual
last step1+a gives, for every such completion and for the old function,

    E<= (2t-2S+2a+a^2)/t^2
       <=1/(2S-2a-a^2).                            (43)

The last denominator is positive by(2), and the inequality follows by
expanding (t-(2S-2a-a^2))^2>=0. The actual terminal of the old function
is the unique full projection prescribed by(22); if w<0 it need NOT be
absorbed, and no such assumption entered(43).

Since a<1/2 and S>600,

    (1+2S)/(2S-2a-a^2)
      =1+(1+a)^2/(2S-2a-a^2)<475/474.

Combining(3),(40),(43) proves the uniform WHOLE-Law-III comparison

    B_n E<19/20                                    (44)

for every old function and EVERY complete-prefix completion in its declared
domain, at every m>=1536. The old all-cap choice t=1+2S attains H_base
with its true closest minimum r, even for nonzero unequal normal data.
No exact supremum over all Law-III completions is claimed.

## 9. What this pass establishes, what failed, and what is still missing

All new statements above are UNVERIFIED. Their proposed scopes are:

1. Law I is exactly scalar for the whole word, including all cap returns,
   all original prefix anchors, the maximal terminal completion, and each
   completion's own true radius. It does not settle every scalar K/ray
   comparison, so it is not recorded as a uniform scalar-family deficit.
2. Law II allows genuinely dependent slopes, nonacute pairwise geometry,
   arbitrary unequal unbounded exterior normals, and any proper initial
   cone face. Its proved initialization guard forces every queried height
   to vanish, and its full payoff, including all terminal completions, is
   bounded by the exact quadratic product. A negative coefficient along
   the old ray demonstrably does not imply absorption.
3. Law III pays a whole positive-height cap prefix and then all nonacute
   face changes after a controlled release. Early releases lose through
   the entire remaining product; late releases pay the original prefix
   mass in their OWN radius. The all-cap branch pays the free completed
   terminal and its changed minimum. The entire stated class stays below
   the original benchmark by(44).

The missing useful mechanism is not character orthogonality. Within these
projection bodies it would require a larger or negatively overshooting
release that escapes(33), and then repeated positive-height returns or a
different positively offset geometry whose full terminal gain survives the
original word after its initial radius is paid. Equations(22)--(24) identify
the exact guards such a claim must satisfy. They do not prove that this
remaining regime is impossible. Law I also leaves untested general
multi-group starts not confined to its two-block line.

No attained improvement by a useful amount over the ENTIRE existing scalar
K/ray channel was obtained. No all-word exact-profile comparison, unrestricted
W upper bound, original target fact, or negative minimax certificate is
claimed. The original problem remains OPEN. This ends the one allocated
analytical invocation; it does not request or assume an automatic renewal.

TASK.md, the complete original PROBLEM.md, relevant latest master_guidance
bea337a7f1631842, the complete previous bb1edc report, own local handoff,
and accepted sources at the scopes stated above were read. Later guidance
0bfc668f1c92a6d1 explicitly leaves this max2r67 assignment unchanged.
Fact search preceded proof work. Relevant shared dead ends were recalled;
no other worker's private memory was read.

The query-memory, search-math-results, direct-proving and
construct-counterexamples procedures were used. The explicit zero-formal
allocation overrides their default submission step: all new mathematics is
one joint draft and no unverified claim is cited as an accepted predecessor.

Matlas queries covered dependent-simplex projection and cone-normal memory.
The latter returned arXiv2006.08426 Theorem1 (polar decomposition),
2007.14466 Proposition3 (projection nonexpansiveness), and2107.07257
LemmaS17 (normal faces). No external theorem is used as a black box.
The projection facts needed here are proved from the displayed normal tests.

The background paper Weiran Wang and Miguel A. Carreira-Perpinan,
“Projection onto the probability simplex: An efficient algorithm with a
simple proof, and an application,” arXiv1309.1541 (2013), was downloaded to
downloads/1309.1541.pdf. Its Sections1--3 and proof were read through public
PDF text extraction, saved in downloads/1309.1541_public_pdf_text.txt.
It concerns equality-sum Euclidean simplex projection;
our carried simplex instead has Gram dI+e11^T and a sum inequality. We
rederived(22)--(24) with those differences, rather than importing its
algorithm or executing it. It supplies no GD terminal guarantee.

Local extraction failed exactly with
`ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'pypdf'`. No installation was attempted.
Some initial broad combined retrieval outputs were truncated; selected
mandatory sources and relevant accepted passages were re-read in bounded
calls. No success is inferred from an errored extraction or empty search.
Two editorial patch attempts returned `Failed to find expected lines` and
made no requested changes. The corrections were applied against the exact
sentences in a subsequent successful patch and the changed passages read
back. These were text edits, not mathematical or formal checks.

Local glossary: E is attained W=2R payoff; m,beta,c,a,H_j,h,n,A_i,S,p_i,s2,v2
are the unchanged hierarchy, physical masses and literal products from
Section1. C_n,B_n,Omega,Gbench,U_n^ZJ,H_base are its original benchmark and
Huber reference. D,C,C^polar,F_D,J,g_i,r_i,delta_i,f_i,Q_ij denote the fixed
body, cone, polar, smooth objective, support, actual gradients, residuals,
heights, values and ordered interpolation rows. H_pre,F_pre,R_pre,K denote
the full conjugate, maximal completion, complete minimum and another legal
static completion. G,q,N,chi_a,sigma,z_s,v_s,b,V,w_s,M,d,e are the finite
dependent dictionary objects in Section4; the local Gram scalar e is
distinct from the unit vector e_A. Law I uses A,p,r,u_A,R_plus,R_minus,e_A,
t_i,gamma_i,d_i,phi,phi_pre. In Laws II/III, I,a_s,eta_s,theta,z_I,g_init,
r_init,r,t,k,d_rel,T_k are their explicitly initialized faces, normals,
scales, cap duration and suffix-product guard. R in Sections7--8 is exactly
the convex hull of the specified zero-height normals, not the performance
normalization R_n. The all-cap symbols w,d_last,N_r,R_last,X,p,u_n are
defined in Section8.3. Every reuse of a scalar letter is local to its named
law or formula and is not a conflicting project-glossary assertion.

ZERO mathematical programs, numerical/arithmetic replay, executed
trajectories/recurrences, optimizers/solvers/benchmark DP, formal calls,
fact_submit, installations, paid consults and subagents. Only textual proof
search, normal retrieval and file I/O were used. No truth-store edit,
outward publication, finalization, revocation, project shutdown or original
Goal completion occurs. Post-return runtime counters and process exit are

# Signed height transport and the two convex height extensions

JOINT UNVERIFIED — max4 invocation84, gd-exact-profile-20260926.
Steering: TASK.md and master_guidance b2215cdc6c21a4c3.
Plan: 322dae312e1b1c16. Interim finding: 52de0461cdbd8db0.
All new arguments in this document form ONE draft. There was no authorized
formal gate, no fact_submit, and no new accepted fact. No new assertion
below is a truth-store building block.

## 1. Verdict and scope

The signed-height identity in the assignment is correct as an algebraic
identity. It retains a nonpositive original-row sum and all adjacent error
squares, but is not by itself an upper certificate.

Mechanism1 specifies a nonnegative original-row law collecting the signed
height mass at the ACTUAL last queried leaf. It uses remote leaf-to-leaf
and remote checkpoint-to-leaf rows outside E80, has the exact full value
flow, and keeps every physical step. Nevertheless its full quadratic has
explicit negative directions at every q, in particular q=17/(16D).
These directions are NOT legal transcripts. They refute this literal
coefficient law, not the adaptive selector, not the entire signed-height
method, and not the full interpolation cone. Two necessary original-space
payment inequalities identify the missing source and short-leaf reserves.

Only after that decisive law failure was Mechanism2 opened. The proposed
finite maximum of affine height supports does satisfy phi(0)=min phi=0,
phi(y_i)=delta_i, and y_i=prox_phi(x_i). Its smooth envelope interpolates
the ENTIRE legal prefix on one static function. But it is generally
strictly BELOW the maximal legal terminal extension. A same-m, same-word
legal quadratic prefix gives explicit strictly different terminal values,
true minimum sets, and globally optimized level thresholds. This refutes
the proposed zero-error transfer from the lower height envelope to a
full terminal upper. It does not refute the one-anchor inequality.

The correct upper height extension is the convex roof of the finite
height data, not their affine-support maximum. The report proves the
inclusions between their level sets, gives a paid anchor-alignment bound,
and evaluates its finite one-anchor threshold minimization. The still
missing all-prefix bound on that payment is theorem-level. No epigraph
identity or unevaluated threshold is presented as a rate.

Original target: OPEN. Full W<=17/(16D): OPEN.
Adaptive single-anchor cover: OPEN, NOT REFUTED.
Exactly TWO mechanisms were opened; no two-anchor selector was opened.

## 2. Original problem, accepted inputs, and notation

For a predetermined nonnegative word h of length n, W_n(h) is the
supremum of 2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over every finite
Euclidean dimension, every convex differentiable L-smooth function with
an attained minimum, and every x_0 different from the selected minimizer.
The updates are x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). Thus W=2R.
The original target is to decide whether

    lim_(n->infinity) W_n^star/U_n^ZJ = 1,
    W_n^star=inf_(h in [0,infinity)^n) W_n(h).

The ORIGINAL benchmark has C_0=B_0=1 and, for every integer n>=1,

    Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt((u+v)^2+4uv))/2,
    G(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2,
    C_n=max_(integer 0<=k<n) Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)),
    B_n=max_(integer 0<=k<n) G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)),
    U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.

Every integer split and the original all-word quantifier remain.
Accepted1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the exact original interpolation and
maximal prefix extension. Accepted931b9653ce0f7201 supplies B_n/D<47/50
on the hierarchy below. Its 799/800 separation is CONDITIONAL on the
full-function W upper, which has not been established here.

Fix any enclosing integer m>=1536, once and for all. Put

    epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,
    lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda*(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,
    h=H_m, n=2^m-1, R=n-1, S=sum_(i<n)h_i,
    D=1+2S, A=h_R=1+a, q=17/(16D).

Every child uses this SAME m and these SAME parameters. The actual
last leaf is R, the actual unqueried terminal is n. All h_i>1 and
0<a<1/(1+sqrt(2))<1/2. In particular A<3/2. No products or trajectories
appearing below were evaluated by a mathematical program.

Normalize L=1, x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Set g_i=grad f(x_i), f_i=f(x_i),
x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t. Retain the ORIGINAL ordered rows

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <=0.

The queried set is I={star,0,...,R}. Put

    y_i=x_i-g_i, delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2,
    y_star=delta_star=0.

Direct expansion, for every ordered pair in I, gives

    Q_ij=delta_i-delta_j+<g_i,y_j-y_i>.               (2.1)

The two optimum rows give

    delta_i>=0, delta_i<=<g_i,y_i>.                  (2.2)

All vectors are real and finite dimensional. A full-space quadratic test
below means a test on independent formal x_0,g_0,...,g_n after the exact
GD substitution; such a test need not satisfy interpolation. All local
symbols introduced below are local to this report, not glossary overrides.

## 3. Mechanism1: signed heights and exact adjacent squares

Define positive physical excesses and their suffix weights by

    r_i=h_i-1,
    omega_i=product_(t=i+1)^R r_t^2, omega_R=1,
    p=product_(t=0)^R r_t^2,
    c_0=omega_0,
    c_i=omega_i-omega_(i-1)=omega_i*(1-r_i^2), 1<=i<=R.

Empty products equal1. The c_i are SIGNED and telescope to sum c_i=1.
For i>=1 with a long step h_i>2, c_i<0. At both level0 leaves and level1
steps c_i>0: their excesses are a and beta. At levels at least2 the
excess is at least beta*c=lambda*(1-epsilon^2)>2, since epsilon<1/8,
lambda>12/5, and (12/5)*(63/64)>2. These signs use the original physical
word, not a reset child schedule. Also c_R=1-a^2>0.

For i<R the update gives y_(i+1)=y_i-r_i*g_i-g_(i+1). Equation (2.1)
then gives the EXACT adjacent row

    Q_(i+1,i)=delta_(i+1)-delta_i
                   +r_i<g_i,g_(i+1)>+||g_(i+1)||^2.

Consequently

    ||g_(i+1)||^2
      =r_i^2||g_i||^2+2(delta_i-delta_(i+1))
          -||r_i*g_i+g_(i+1)||^2+2Q_(i+1,i).        (3.1)

Multiply (3.1) by omega_i and sum over every original i<R.
The gradient terms telescope because omega_i*r_i^2=omega_(i-1)
for i>=1, omega_0*r_0^2=p, and omega_(R-1)=a^2. Adding 2delta_R gives

    E_R:=2delta_R+a^2||g_R||^2
       =p||g_0||^2+2sum_(i=0)^R c_i*delta_i
         -sum_(i<R)omega_i||r_i*g_i+g_(i+1)||^2
         +2sum_(i<R)omega_i Q_(i+1,i).             (3.2)

This derivation retains signed height changes. No delta_i-delta_(i+1)
was replaced by a maximum height, and no error square was dropped.
The last original step gives x_n-y_R=-a*g_R, so E_R is exactly the
last-anchor segment cost at weight1. It is only a sufficient upper
surrogate for the adaptive selector.

## 4. A concrete remote-row law and its full-space refutation

Let Pplus={i: c_i>0}, Pminus={j: c_j<0}. Define the complete height
transport t, with unlisted entries zero, by

    t_(i,R)=c_i,          i in Pplus, i!=R,
    t_(R,j)=-c_j,        j in Pminus,
    t_(R,star)=1.                                       (4.1)

Every t is nonnegative. Its net finite flow at i is c_i. At a positive
non-R node the outgoing mass is c_i; at a negative node the incoming
mass is -c_i. At R, outgoing minus incoming is
1+sum_(-c_j)-sum_(i in Pplus,i!=R)c_i=c_R, using sum c_i=1.
The star has net flow -1.

This law compares every positive height to the actual last leaf using
Q_(i,R), and the last leaf to every negative long-bridge height using
Q_(R,j). It includes both whole physical children and their real bridge.
It leaves the E80 support: Q_(0,R) is a remote leaf-to-leaf row, and
Q_(1,R) is a remote checkpoint-to-leaf row, since c_1>0 and R-1>1.
Thus its failure is not inferred merely from the old E80 obstruction.

The proposed ORIGINAL prefix multipliers are twice t, plus 2omega_i
on every row (i+1,i), i<R. Add the sole terminal row lambda_(n,R)=2.
All overlaps are added, all other entries are zero, and no incoming
terminal edge is used. Prefix flow is0 at i<R and2 at R: at0 it is
2c_0-2omega_0=0; at an interior i it is
2c_i+2omega_(i-1)-2omega_i=0; at R it is2c_R+2omega_(R-1)=2.
After adding the terminal row every finite node has the required flow.

Subtract (3.2) and add these prefix rows. The complete prefix remainder is

    K_t(q)=q||x_0||^2-E_R
           +2sum_(i<R)omega_i Q_(i+1,i)+2sum_ij t_ij Q_ij
      =q||x_0||^2-p||g_0||^2
           +sum_(i<R)omega_i||r_i*g_i+g_(i+1)||^2
           +2sum_ij t_ij<g_i,y_j-y_i>.               (4.2)

All heights, equivalently all sample values, cancel exactly. Its FULL
original certificate polynomial is

    q||x_0||^2-2f_n+2Q_(n,R)
         +2sum_(i<R)omega_i Q_(i+1,i)+2sum_ij t_ij Q_ij
       =||g_n+a*g_R||^2+K_t(q).                     (4.3)

For a valid upper certificate, (4.3) must be PSD on every original
formal vector assignment. Nonnegative coefficients alone do not suffice.

### 4.1 Free initial direction

Take any nonzero vector v and set x_0=0, g_0=v, g_i=0 for every i>0,
including n. Then y_0=-v and y_i=-A*v for every finite i>=1.
The only nonzero adjacent square is omega_0*a^2||v||^2=p||v||^2,
which cancels the source product term. The only nonzero transport
quadratic is its source0 row to R:

    2t_(0,R)<v,y_R-y_0>=-2a*omega_0||v||^2<0.

Both q||x_0||^2 and the terminal square vanish. Therefore the entire
polynomial (4.3) is strictly negative, for EVERY q. In particular it
fails at the full requested scale17/(16D) for every enclosing m>=1536.

This is a rank-one ORIGINAL-SPACE discriminator, not a legal prefix.
Indeed x_0=0 and g_0!=0 already contradict the two optimum rows.
No lower bound on W or failure of a true legal-prefix inequality follows.

### 4.2 An interior short-leaf direction

The failure is not confined to how c_0 was defined. Choose the actual
interior leaf j=2, and set x_0=0, g_j=v!=0, all other gradients zero.
Since m>=1536, 0<j<R and the neighboring physical indices all exist.
Here y_j=-v, all preceding y_i=0, and all later y_i=-A*v. The adjacent
squares have total

    (omega_(j-1)+a^2*omega_j)||v||^2
                       =2a^2*omega_j||v||^2.

Only the outgoing transport from j contributes. As
c_j=omega_j*(1-a^2), equation (4.2) is exactly

    K_t(q)=2omega_j*a*(a^2+a-1)||v||^2<0,             (4.4)

because 0<a<1/2 implies a^2+a<3/4. Again the terminal square vanishes.
This is a second full-space, noninterpolable discriminator. A change
only to the initial transport cannot repair this literal collector.

### 4.3 Necessary payments for a future different transport

For any other nonnegative height transport T with finite net flow c,
(4.2) still holds with T in place of t. This is an identity, not a
feasibility assertion about T. On the slice with only g_0=v nonzero,
keep x_0 free and put

    u=T_(0,star), z=sum_(j=1)^R T_(0,j).

All other sources have zero gradient. Exact substitution gives

    K_T(q)=q||x_0||^2-2u<x_0,v>+2(u-a*z)||v||^2.

For q>0 this slice is nonnegative for all x_0,v if and only if

    2(u-a*z) >= u^2/q.                               (4.5)

Complete the square q||x_0-(u/q)v||^2 to obtain both directions.
Thus a free initial optimum payment cannot be omitted, and its mixed
x_0/gradient term costs u^2/q. The collector has u=0,z=omega_0>0.

Likewise, at any interior leaf j, put
u_j=T_(j,star), L_j=u_j+sum_(k<j)T_(j,k),
Z_j=sum_(k>j)T_(j,k). On the slice with only g_j=v nonzero and x_0 free,

    K_T(q)=q||x_0||^2-2u_j<x_0,v>
          +2(omega_j*a^2+L_j-a*Z_j)||v||^2.

A necessary condition is therefore

    2(omega_j*a^2+L_j-a*Z_j) >= u_j^2/q.             (4.6)

Neither (4.5) nor (4.6) suffices for full PSD; mixed-gradient directions
remain. These are explicit audits of this signed-height payment
mechanism, not a third proposed law or an unknown PSD problem claimed
as solved. Reverse remote payments or direct optimum mass would change
the law and must pay the indicated source terms.

## 5. Mechanism2: the affine-support height function is genuinely proximal

After Section4's decisive first-law failure, define from ALL original
legal prefix rows

    phi(y)=max{0, max_(0<=i<=R)[delta_i+<g_i,y-y_i>]}.
                                                               (5.1)

It is finite, continuous, convex and nonnegative. By (2.1), every affine
piece at y_j is at most delta_j, while piece j equals delta_j. Equation
(2.2) handles the zero piece. Thus phi(y_j)=delta_j. The active affine
piece also gives, for every z,

    phi(z)>=phi(y_j)+<g_j,z-y_j>,

so g_j belongs to the subdifferential of phi at y_j. At zero, the
finite pieces have values delta_i-<g_i,y_i><=0 by Q_(i,star)<=0.
Hence phi(0)=0=min phi. Both optimum-row orientations were used.

Write prox_phi(x) for the unique minimizer of phi(z)+||x-z||^2/2.
It exists because phi>=0 and the quadratic is coercive, and is unique
by strict convexity. With x_i=y_i+g_i, the subgradient inequality gives

    phi(z)+||x_i-z||^2/2
       >=delta_i+||g_i||^2/2+||z-y_i||^2/2.

Equality holds at z=y_i. Therefore

    prox_phi(x_i)=y_i,
    f_phi(x_i):=min_z[phi(z)+||x_i-z||^2/2]=f_i.       (5.2)

For completeness, f_phi is one static convex differentiable 1-smooth
function on the SAME finite-dimensional space. Let u=prox_phi(x),
u'=prox_phi(x'), v=x-u, v'=x'-u'. To prove v in partial phi(u), fix z
and compare the minimizing u to u+s(z-u), 0<s<=1. Convexity bounds
phi(u+s(z-u)) by phi(u)+s(phi(z)-phi(u)). Expanding the quadratic
in this comparison, dividing by s, and sending s down to zero gives
phi(z)>=phi(u)+<v,z-u>. Add this subgradient inequality at u and u'
to get

    <u-u',v-v'>>=0,
    <x-x',v-v'>>=||v-v'||^2.

Thus ||v-v'||<=||x-x'||. For d=x'-x, trial points u,u' in the two
envelope minima give
<v',d>-||d||^2/2 <= f_phi(x')-f_phi(x)
                         <= <v,d>+||d||^2/2.
The Lipschitz estimate shows both bounds equal <v,d> up to a term
bounded in absolute value by 3||d||^2/2. Hence grad f_phi(x)=v,
and that gradient is 1-Lipschitz.
Convexity follows by using a convex combination of u,u' as a trial point
and convexity of phi and the squared norm. Equation (5.2) and its gradient
version show that every original update follows the exact supplied prefix.
The entire function is fixed before the run.

Its COMPLETE minimum set is

    C_low(0)={y: <g_i,y><=<g_i,y_i>-delta_i for all i},     (5.3)

which contains0. Indeed the envelope is nonnegative, and its value is
zero precisely when its minimizing point equals x and phi(x)=0.
The true initial distance is dist(x_0,C_low(0)). This can be smaller than
||x_0||, so the new interpolant's closest minimum must not be inherited
silently from a different original function. The selected minimum0 is
nevertheless always explicitly valid.

These identities make phi a correct height representation of the
QUERIED proximal pairs. They do not make f_phi a maximal extension.

## 6. The upper height model and a paid level-set comparison

Accepted1252 gives the maximal legal prefix extension F by

    H(v)=||v||^2/2+max_(i in I)(<y_i,v>-delta_i),
    F(x)=sup_v[<x,v>-H(v)].

For every probability alpha on ALL I, completing the square gives

    2F(x)<=2sum_i alpha_i delta_i+||x-sum_i alpha_i y_i||^2.

The minimum over alpha is attained on its compact simplex. At a
minimizer let V=x-sum alpha_i y_i. The derivatives of half the displayed
quadratic are delta_i-<V,y_i>. Exchanges of probability imply that they
are equal on positive alpha_i and no smaller elsewhere. Therefore the
maximum affine term in H(V) equals its alpha-average, and evaluating
the conjugate at V gives equality. This proves jointly

    2F(x)=min_(alpha probability on I)
